# Fork and distribution polish — file 1 of 4: fork correctness and the round trip

This file is self-contained. It is the second of four `/st-plan` artifacts for **Package 20**, which makes stamity's
public and private fork support and its three distribution routes — the CLI, APM and the client plugins — work end to
end for any organization. Each file is one pull request per session; since 2026-10-06 file 0 (`docs/plans/016-fork-distribution-00.md`, the security fixes) runs before Package 19, and this file runs as two sessions. This file merges without a release; file 2
(`docs/plans/016-fork-distribution-02.md`) builds the routes, and file 3 (`docs/plans/016-fork-distribution-03.md`)
writes the docs, proves every route and cuts 1.12.0.

1.12.0 is taken by file 0's release (2026-10-07); a release this file names as 1.12.0 takes the next minor number when it runs.

intent chosen: feature because the request names net-new capability (a fork CI job, `fork-identity.mjs --suffix`,
`clean --purge`, the `copilot.setupSteps` key) and asks for the distribution routes to be "polished and state of the
art"; the fork defects ride as units, each opening with the test that fails today, and no dependency moves, so the
migration intent does not fire.

## Context

An enterprise re-set up its private fork on upstream 1.10.0, released it as `1.10.0-<suffix>.2` and rolled it out
through its own orchestrator on 2026-09-29 and 30, then sent a short feedback list and a 67-item brief. On 2026-10-01
this planning run applied the fork identity to 1.11.0 with a suffixed version and a documented
`stamity.distribution.sources` value, and ran the whole gate: 8 tests in 6 files fail only because the tree is a fork.
The fork release's `npm publish` fails on every suffixed version, because npm 11 refuses a prerelease without `--tag`
(`npm error You must specify a tag using --tag when publishing a prerelease version.`, npm 11.21.0, dry run), and the
workflow test stubs npm, so it pins the defect in. The released 1.11.0 CLI, measured in throwaway repositories, does not
round trip: `clean` leaves four `.gitignore` lines and a `settings.json.bak`, `clean -y` deletes every user file under
`.stamity/`, `init --force` doubles the charter, the CLI writes into a folder APM deployed, and the generated Copilot
setup workflow can be neither opted out of nor SHA-pinned.

This file fixes what breaks a fork or a round trip, and adds the guard that would have caught the fork defects: a
canonical CI job that runs the whole gate as a suffixed fork on every pull request. **Out of scope:** the routes' new
capabilities (file 2), the docs pages, the route proofs and the release (file 3), and the drop list.

## Decisions

### The maintainer's walk (2026-10-01, through the question tool; every answer the recommended option unless the row says otherwise)

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| Where the package goes | Its own **Package 20**, after Package 11 track B and before Package 12, so Package 12 re-proves the final distribution once and its "nothing actionable left" close stays true. Order: Package 19 → Package 18 → Track B → Package 20 → Package 12. |
| The 1.11.0 ops-pack regression (`stamity add ops`, then `sync` fails on Cursor and on Codex alone) | Its own `/st-debug` run before Package 19, merged to `main` without a release; not in this plan. |
| Where each release's slim APM package lives | A folder of the distribution tree, fetched alone (`apm install <owner>/<repo>/apm#plugins/v<version>`); measured first, with an orphan tag as the fallback. |
| What APM alone can do, and how the CLI joins | APM alone works: every placeholder is filled at generation time and the charter rides through APM where a target can take it. A repository that also runs the CLI gets an `apm-backed` mode in which the CLI writes only the charter, hooks, MCP wiring and state, and `check` fails any class delivered twice. |
| How the package ships | **1.12.0 at its end:** one fresh full eval run (about 3.5 h unattended), QA and the tag. It also carries Packages 19 and 18 and the ops-pack fix. |
| Optional plugin items | **All four:** release channels with a verified promotion and a soak; `plugin setup --declare`; overrides in plugin mode; the review gate on every client. |
| Optional CLI and fork items | **`stamity upgrade` only.** Review reasons in `--json` and `release.json`, `stamity status --json` and a release on lane merge go to the drop list. |
| Accounts for live proofs | **VS Code with Copilot on the maintainer's machine only.** The Copilot organization install, the auto-update legs and Cursor's team marketplace stay `Not done:` with their owner. |

### What 1.11.0 already settled (no unit here)

- **Codex no longer grows the shared `AGENTS.md`.** 1.11.0 moved Codex's rules into a root `AGENTS.override.md`; with Codex selected `AGENTS.md` stays 5,330 bytes and the 25,360-byte override is read by Codex alone (measured 2026-10-01 with the released 1.11.0 CLI; at 1.10.0 the same fixture's `AGENTS.md` grew from 5,279 to 25,277 bytes).
- **Codex gets the nine touchpoint workflows** as shared `.agents/skills/` skills (`$st-<id>`).
- **`test/replay/fixture.test.ts` is gone** with the rest of the replay (`d5d3fee7`).
- **Owned by other packages:** the import without `--mirror`, the lane's release-tag-only fetch with the snapshot sweep, the nightly guard and the records move are Package 18 (`docs/plans/014-lean-repository-01.md`, `-02.md`); the paired with/without value evidence (brief Q5) is Package 11 track B.

### Settled by this plan (declared defaults; the maintainer may reverse any before the run starts)

| # | Default | Why |
|---|---|---|
| S1 | A fork release publishes with `--tag "$DIST_TAG"`, where `DIST_TAG` is the repository variable `STAMITY_RELEASE_DIST_TAG`, else `latest`. | npm 11 refuses a prerelease without `--tag`; the CLI's update notice reads the `latest` dist-tag (`src/cli/notice/updateNotice.ts:270`), so `latest` keeps a fork's notice working. |
| S2 | `scripts/fork-identity.mjs --suffix <id>` writes suffix-aware tag patterns into the fork's Renovate presets; the canonical presets stay strict (an `rc` tag still fails). | The two tests that pin opposite expectations then each read their own preset, and the canonical repository never offers a prerelease. |
| S3 | The fork CI job runs only in the canonical repository, on one Linux leg, and is merge-blocking through `all-ci-checks`. | A fork's own release already runs its gate; the job's purpose is to stop upstream changes that break every fork. |
| S4 | Tests read a test-owned global git config written by the vitest global setup (`GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`); the git-heavy cases carry 60 s; the default timeout stays 20 s. | 22 test files spawn git with the operator's own config today; corporate laptops with endpoint security ran past 20 s. |
| S5 | Secret-shaped fixture values are assembled at runtime, and a guard test fails on a real-format literal under `test/`. | Enterprise scanners open incidents on forks for literals such as the `AKIA…EXAMPLE` key AWS publishes as its example and PEM headers. |
| S6 | `clean -y` removes engine-owned files and keeps user state; `clean -y --purge` removes `.stamity/` entirely; an installed pack keeps its install and loses its client copies. | Principle "the engine never deletes anything a user owns"; the inbox Warning on the `st-setup` body's `clean -y` wording. |
| S7 | `.gitignore` lines are owned per line in the ledger, and merged settings and hook documents per entry (file 0's S10); `clean` removes exactly the lines it added and writes no `.bak` when only foreign content survives. A 1.11.0 setup's unledgered lines stay after `clean`, as they do today (amended 2026-10-06: the per-key settings half moved to file 0). | A true round trip without touching what another tool added; the 1.11.0 rule keeps an upgrade from deleting what it cannot prove it wrote. |
| S8 | `copilot.setupSteps` takes `managed` (default), `user` (the engine stops tracking the file and leaves it as it is) or `off` (not written; one the engine wrote earlier is reclaimed). In `managed` mode every action ref comes from the engine's SHA table, with the tag as a trailing comment, and no project workflow is read (revised 2026-10-06; it reused a full 40-hex SHA pin found in the project's own workflows). | Settles the spec author's marker on REQ-FLOW-031. Revised 2026-10-06: copying the project's refs coupled every Dependabot bump of the owner's workflow to `check` drift; an organisation that must use its own SHAs takes `user` mode. |
| S9 | The manifest's `updatedAt` moves only when an entry changes. The pinned `npx -y <package>@<version> <verb>` calls are pins (REQ-FLOW-002 pins them so they never run `@latest`) and keep the version; only non-pin places, such as the `CLAUDE.md` marker `STAMITY:BEGIN v<version>`, drop it, and remedy text inside a file a client trusts by hash (`.codex/hooks.json`) names the version as `VERSION from .stamity/manifest.json generatedBy`, never a number (amended 2026-10-06). | Settles the marker on REQ-FLOW-032 (option a). Amended 2026-10-06: Codex records hook trust against each hook's hash, so a version there asks every user to re-trust after every upgrade (brief C3; vendor page read 2026-10-06). |
| S10 | A hook that cannot start keeps every call it governs blocked and stops blocking calls it never governs. Claude Code's pre-tool-use guard, when Node cannot start or `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` is unset, blocks every call by a `stamity-` agent and lets the main thread and the client's own agents through with a message naming Node and the floor 22.22.2 (never `stamity sync`); a missing guard script or any other launch failure still blocks every call. Copilot's identity-free pre-tool-use guard is not wired, because it can never refuse a call there while Copilot denies every call on any non-zero exit. Cursor's subagent guard is scoped to `stamity-` subagents by its matcher and stays `failClosed`; the MCP guard stays unscoped and `failClosed`. (revised 2026-10-06; it read: "A hook that cannot start Node keeps its guard closed and only changes its message".) | The floor never relaxes for a governed call: the guard governs only `stamity-` agents (`src/hooks/scripts.ts:1709-1712`), so blocking the main thread or a client's own agents for want of Node was never part of it (measured, r3 and d2 lab, 2026-10-06). |
| S11 | The lane closes only its own marker-carrying issues for tags at or below the integrated release, with a comment naming it; never a human's issue, never a newer tag's. | The enterprise closed four such issues by hand; the marker already exists (`.github/workflows/upstream-update.yml:879,1143`). |
| S12 | The lane derives nothing unless `.stamity/upstream.json` carries `identityFiles`; the shipped template and the guide's recommended file carry it, so a fork that writes its file with `fork-identity.mjs --lane-config` opts in, and one that deletes the key opts out. | The maintainer chose to build F11 as an opt-in setting; an identity-only fork wants it on every release, and a fork with its own dependencies is stopped by the guard either way. |
| S13 | The derivation re-locks offline: it rewrites only the lock's root `name` and `version` and runs no npm; the recommended `regenerate` list's `npm ci --ignore-scripts` then installs from the derived pair through the fork's own npm configuration with lifecycle scripts off and fails when the pair is out of sync. | The CI `prepare` job runs Node 22.22.2, whose npm 10 drops the lock's 38 `libc` fields on any re-lock (plan 016's 2026-10-01 measurement); the root rewrite is what npm 11 writes when nothing else changed; invariant 7 keeps the lane off the network. Reading "re-locks with the fork's registry configuration" as a networked `npm install --package-lock-only` is the alternative (see Notes). |
| S14 | N is read from the fork's own version: `<X.Y.Z>-<suffix>.1` for a new upstream base, the fork's N + 1 for a re-release of the same base; no tag is read. | The guide states no rule (`docs/enterprise-forks.md:953` shows `1.11.0-acme.1` only); the brief's burned `-.1` re-released as `-.2` agrees; the trees alone decide it, so `preview` and `integrate` agree offline. |
| S15 | Fork-owned `package.json` keys are `fork-identity.mjs`'s identity keys, `version`, and every key under `stamity.distribution`; a difference anywhere else stops the derivation of both package files. | The guide documents `branch`, `tagPattern`, `ownerEmail` and `sources` as the fork's (`:869-878`) and file 02 adds `channels` and `soakDays`; the brief asks the lane to stop on anything beyond the identity. |
| S16 | The lane-config template is `scripts/upstream-config.template.json`; `--lane-config` adds what the template has and keeps every fork key and entry (`upstream`, `remote`, `branch`, `releases`, `shadows`, `acceptedTagMoves`, extras); the identity gate is `npm ci --ignore-scripts && node scripts/fork-identity.mjs <args> --lane-config --check`. | A file under `.stamity/` would arm the canonical lane; a fork may fetch from a mirror or add gates; `validate` can run the gates in a fresh worktree with no `node_modules`, and both generators import dependencies. |
| S17 | Credential order App, then `STAMITY_UPSTREAM_TOKEN`, then the repository token; the variable holds the App's client ID (the action's `client-id` input; `app-id` is its legacy input); the token is narrowed to this repository (name derived from `GITHUB_REPOSITORY`) and Contents, Pull requests and Issues write. | GitHub's action documents `client-id`; a `schedule` payload may lack `github.event.repository`, and an empty `repositories` with `owner` set would cover the whole installation. |
| S18 | `GH_TOKEN` moves from the publish job's `env` to each step that runs `gh`. | A job `env` cannot read a step output; the artifact steps stop holding the token as a side effect. |
| S19 | Fork attestations are opt-in (`STAMITY_RELEASE_ATTEST=true`) and run in their own `attest` job after `publish`; the canonical release adds a second attest step for the stamped `release.json`. | `publish` keeps no OIDC grant beside npm; a plan without attestation support leaves a complete release; the stamped asset and the branch copy have different digests and both are read by consumers. |
| S20 | The npm dry run lives in `gates`, on every armed run, not in `dry-run-summary`. | It then runs before the approval on a tag push as well as in a rehearsal, with no artifact download under `permissions: {}` (one unverified risk of the 2026-10-01 plan retires). |
| S21 | The fork's release notes come from a root `FORK-CHANGELOG.md` the fork owns (upstream ships none); a missing section is named in the body, never a failure. | Upstream never touches the file, so the lane never conflicts on it, and it is reviewed through pull requests, unlike a tag message. |
| S22 | A public repository is refused unless `STAMITY_RELEASE_PUBLIC=true`; GitHub Packages visibility is read before and after the publish with the per-run token, a refused read is a warning. | The brief asks for an assertion around publishing; the packages REST pages document classic tokens only, so a hard failure on an unreadable answer could block every release. |
| S23 | The docs rewrite changes four spellings in nine pages; `docs/enterprise-forks.md` is excluded; three upstream sentences are reworded so the canonical name never appears as a contrast. | Upstream URLs in the fork guide are deliberate; evidence links and attribution must keep naming upstream. |
| S24 | A moved integrated tag (or covered tag) stops the lane until `acceptedTagMoves` names its current commit. | Principle 3; acceptance lives in the reviewed configuration, and a second move stops again. |
| S25 | Published-release freshness takes a caller-supplied `--releases-file`; without it the document says `basis: "tags"`. | The lane stays git-only (invariant 7); the heartbeat already holds `gh`. |
| S26 | `STAMITY_UPSTREAM_SCHEDULE` is `off` or a list of UTC hours; the slot is the hour of the run's start less 17 minutes. | A cron cannot come from a variable; the offset keeps a late tick in its slot. |
| S27 | `STAMITY_LANDING_POLICY=rulesets-only` replaces the classic read's failure with a declaration; a contradicting readable document wins with a warning. | Neither token holds Administration; the rulesets surface stays read. |
| S28 | The suite caps itself at 4 workers by default (`STAMITY_TEST_MAX_WORKERS` overrides). | The enterprise's endpoint-security laptops passed at 4; CI's 3 workers are unchanged; the maintainer's 8-core machine drops from 7 to 4. |
| S29 | The userinfo guard scans `test/`, `scripts/`, `src/` and `.github/`, not `docs/plans/` or `.stamity/runs/`. | Records stay as written; the comment at `scripts/upstream.mjs:981` came with the commit r4 links to the older incidents. |
| S30 | The reference exemption covers only `inline-secret-assignment`, only in the screens over owner text a write keeps (merge lane, Codex's republish, `.gitignore`), and only the closed list of reference forms (REQ-FLOW-039); a literal anywhere in the value refuses; the learnings, handoff, override, pack and MCP-description gates are unchanged. | A reference names a secret without holding one, so exempting it moves no floor; the closed list and the whole-value rule keep `${{ 'literal' }}`, `\|\|` fallbacks and `${X:-default}` refused. The other gates were not reported (follow-up row). |
| S31 | `init` is all or nothing: every write refusal is checked before the state directory, live and dry runs alike; `sync` stays per path. | `init` has no manifest to account for a partial write (r2: 66 orphan files that `clean` cannot see); `sync`'s per-path rule exists so one collision does not strand a pack. |
| S32 | `--import-config` takes `<mode>` or, repeatably, `<path>=<mode>`; the config keys are `importChoice.<path>` for the four import targets. | One flag keeps one concept, a default and its exceptions, and every existing command line keeps its meaning; the key is the manifest field plus the path, so `config get` reads back exactly what is recorded. |
| S33 | `config set importChoice.<path>` moves the owner's bytes itself (a backup on the way to `replace`, a restore on the way back); `sync` never takes or restores an import backup and overwrites under `replace` only when the target is the engine's output or the recorded backup holds exactly its owner text. | Principle 5: a committed manifest edit cannot make `sync` overwrite owner bytes that are not already preserved; it is the property file 0's refusal of unrecorded decisions needs, and it keeps ≤1.11.0 `replace` decisions (no backup record) working. |
| S34 | `init --strict` exits 1 when a planned path was skipped, after writing the rest; the default stays 0. | Matches `sync`'s per-path exit for a rollout that asks for it, without breaking scripts that rely on today's 0. |
| S35 | `check` exits 3 only when every drift row is `resync` and no doctor row fails; `ok: false`, `error.code: RESYNC_NEEDED` (a CLI-edge code); a pending deletion and a hashless row are always `tamper`; no other verb returns 3. | The maintainer chose an exit code (2026-10-06). Treating 3 as a failure keeps every consumer that branches on non-zero safe; keeping deletions out of `resync` means no deletion rides an automated re-sync. |
| S36 | `sync`'s backups (and `init`'s, except an import `replace`) go to `.stamity/backups/<path>.bak`, a folder that ignores itself with a `.gitignore` holding `*`; `clean -y` keeps it, `--purge` removes it; an import `replace` original stays beside the file, bounded and recorded. | A fleet PR neither commits a backup nor trips an ownership guard, the root `.gitignore` gains no line (no chicken-and-egg with u1-gitignore-lines), and the import original must reach a fresh clone for `clean` to restore it. |
| S37 | Every verb that reads a setup walks up to the nearest `.stamity/manifest.json` within the git top-level; with no git it uses the working directory; `init` and `plugin setup` refuse under a governing setup, `init --force` proceeds. | The git model operators already know; the walk stops where the repository does; `--force` follows the `workspace init` nesting precedent instead of adding a flag. |
| S38 | Ignored output warns at `init` and `sync` and fails `check` (`ignored-output`), over ledgered paths and the manifest; tracked paths are not ignored (git's index rule). | The brief asks for warnings and a named path in `check`; refusing setup would block a local-only choice, while CI is where an ignored ledger path breaks. |
| S39 | `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` stamps the manifest's `createdAt` and `updatedAt` in `init` and `sync`; a malformed value refuses the run. | The reproducible-builds convention; a rebuilt rollout branch is then byte-identical. `sync` follows `init` for the same branch (the brief asked for `init`). |
| S40 | The import prepend keeps the owner's bytes verbatim, so the round trip is byte-exact (inbox ``"`prependManagedBlock`'s `trimStart` and final-newline normalisation keep a supplemented file's round trip inexact" at 077e8a78`` re-opened and fixed). | The brief asks for byte for byte and says the enterprise restores by hash today; recording the original's shape would be a second mechanism for the same result. |

### The maintainer's walk on the updated brief (2026-10-06, through the question tool; every answer the recommended option)

On 2026-10-06 the enterprise sent an updated brief: the earlier items with fresh measurements on its fork (it has
since built a fleet rollout in its own orchestrator, tried it on disposable copies of two real canary repositories and
reviewed it in 19 automated passes; no repository carries stamity yet), eleven new items (C14, N6–N8, F11–F15 and the brief's S4 and S5; the brief's S-labels are not this plan's S-rows) and a sixth principle — the CLI is safe to run unattended next
to a token: offline unless asked, never interactive, kept away from the caller's credentials. Eight research passes
the same day re-measured every claim on `main` at `d10db029` (1.11.0 plus the pack-engine fixes of #77) in throwaway
repositories, mapped each to this plan, and re-read the vendor documentation. Five security defects of the released
1.11.0 surfaced that this plan did not cover. Then the maintainer answered:

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| When the security fixes run | **Their own run before Package 19:** the new file `docs/plans/016-fork-distribution-00.md` (file 0) holds the owned-path bound for the ledger, per-entry ownership of `.claude/settings.json` and the hook files (absorbing `u1-settings-key-ownership`), `check --expect-*` and registry-bound CLI calls for forks; one pull request to `main`, no release; the next release ships it (Track B's cut if Track B releases, else 1.12.0). |

| Supply-chain additions | **Build** N2 (a published `npm-shrinkwrap.json`) and the brief's S5 + S4 (npm's sha512 integrity in `release.json` and a digest per client root, checked by the promotion; file 02's S18). **F13/F14**, publishing only the routes a fork uses, stays on the drop list. |
| New CLI behaviours | **Build** N7 (exit code 3, "re-sync needed", separating drift the owner's own changes cause from tampering), a per-file import mode with a config key (N1) and a JSON output contract (C1 part 1: a schema version and warning codes with a severity). **C1 part 2**, review reasons and release classification, stays on the drop list (inbox ``"review reasons in every write command's `--json`" at 077e8a78``). |

Every defect the research found is fixed whether or not a question asked about it. Terraform and HCL detection

### A. `docs/specs/plugin-lifecycle.md`

#### REQ-PLUGIN-031 A fork release publishes its suffixed version under an explicit npm dist-tag (ADDED)

Added `<date>` (plan 016 file 1, unit `u1-npm-dist-tag`). A fork's version carries a prerelease suffix, as the fork guide
recommends (`1.12.0-acme.1`). npm 11 refuses to publish a prerelease version without `--tag`. So
`.github/workflows/fork-release.yml` publishes with `--tag "$DIST_TAG"`. `DIST_TAG` is the repository variable
`STAMITY_RELEASE_DIST_TAG` when it is set, and `latest` otherwise, because the update notice reads `latest`. The
`gates` job runs the same npm check with npm 11 (`npm publish <tarball> --dry-run --tag <DIST_TAG>`) on every armed run,
before the approval, so a rehearsal cannot stay green while the real publish fails; `dry-run-summary` keeps its one
report step (aligned 2026-10-07 with REQ-PLUGIN-050 and this file's decision S20; it read "The `dry-run-summary`
rehearsal runs the same npm check"). The fork guide's "Tag a release" step names the regeneration
step and npm 11 for the lockfile refresh.

- **Units:** `u1-npm-dist-tag`.
- **Evidence:** the publish line is `npm publish "./$TARBALL" --registry "$REGISTRY"` with no `--tag`
  (`.github/workflows/fork-release.yml:666`); the rehearsal job `dry-run-summary` (`:803-847`) runs no npm; the update notice reads
  the `latest` dist-tag (`src/cli/notice/updateNotice.ts:270`); the workflow test stubs npm for `1.10.0-acme.1` and so
  locks the defect in (`test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts:963-1001`). Measured 2026-10-01: npm 11.21.0 `publish
  --dry-run` of a prerelease version without `--tag` exits 1 with "You must specify a tag using --tag when publishing a
  prerelease version"; `publishConfig.tag` does not show in the dry run; npm 10 strips 38 `libc` entries from the
  lockfile.
- **Proof:** `test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts`; the fork CI job of REQ-PLUGIN-033 runs the real npm 11 dry run.

Criteria:
- GIVEN `fork-release.yml` THEN its publish step passes `--tag` with a value read from `vars.STAMITY_RELEASE_DIST_TAG`, and
  `latest` when that variable is empty.
- GIVEN the `gates` job THEN it runs `npm publish` with `--dry-run` and the same `--tag` value through npm 11 or newer,
  on the tarball it packed; GIVEN `dry-run-summary` THEN its one step is the report and it runs no npm.
- GIVEN a tarball at version `<X.Y.Z>-acme.1` WHEN npm 11 runs the rehearsal's command THEN it exits 0; WHEN the same
  command runs without `--tag` THEN it exits 1. The test runs real npm 11 or newer for this pair, not a stub, on the
  CI legs whose Node ships it (skipped with its reason below npm 11 and on Windows), and the fork probe of REQ-PLUGIN-033
  runs it on every pull request.
- GIVEN the fork guide's "Tag a release" section THEN it names the regeneration step and npm 11 for the lockfile refresh,
  and `test/docsPages.test.ts` pins both.

#### REQ-PLUGIN-032 The Renovate presets track a fork's suffixed versions and tags (ADDED)

Added `<date>` (plan 016 file 1, unit `u1-renovate-suffix`). `scripts/fork-identity.mjs` takes `--suffix <id>`, where
`<id>` matches `[a-z0-9]+`; any other value exits 1 with nothing written, as a bad `--scope` does. With it, the script
rewrites `renovate/plugins.json`'s `versioningTemplate` to accept the fork's own suffix as an optional fourth part,
`regex:^plugins/v(?<major>\d+)\.(?<minor>\d+)\.(?<patch>\d+)(?:-<id>\.(?<build>\d+))?$` — Renovate compares `build` like
`patch`, so `-<id>.1` orders before `-<id>.2` and the release stays stable — and sets `renovate/companion.json`'s
`ignoreUnstable` to `false`, so a pin such as `1.10.0-<id>.2` is offered `1.11.0-<id>.1`. The companion keeps the npm
datasource's default versioning, which orders a prerelease by semver precedence and still reads ranges for `pin`
(Renovate's `semver` versioning reads no ranges). The canonical presets stay strict: `plugins.json` accepts only
`plugins/v<X.Y.Z>`, and `companion.json` carries `ignoreUnstable: true`, Renovate's default made explicit so the script
flips a value in place. The template comes from one exported function, `tagVersioningTemplate({ prefix, suffix,
required })`, which file 2's APM preset reuses with the suffix required.

- **Units:** `u1-renovate-suffix`.
- **Evidence:** the pattern at `renovate/plugins.json:15` matches no `-<suffix>.<n>` tag, while `:12`'s `matchStrings`
  already captures one; two tests pin opposite expectations — an rc tag must fail (`test/ci/pluginDistribution.test.ts:490-509`)
  and the fork's own tag must pass (`test/ci/releaseManifest.test.ts:279-287`, red in the 2026-10-01 fork probe);
  `ignoreUnstable` (default true) moves an unstable pin only within the same `major.minor.patch` (Renovate's
  configuration options, read 2026-10-01); the script rewrites the presets in place at `scripts/fork-identity.mjs:199-215`.
- **Proof:** `test/ci/forkIdentityScript.test.ts`, `test/ci/releaseManifest.test.ts`, `test/ci/pluginDistribution.test.ts`.

Criteria:
- GIVEN the canonical presets THEN `plugins.json`'s template equals `tagVersioningTemplate({ prefix: "plugins/v" })` and
  matches `plugins/v1.12.0` but not `plugins/v1.12.0-rc.1`, and `companion.json` carries `ignoreUnstable: true`.
- GIVEN `fork-identity.mjs --suffix acme` THEN `plugins.json`'s template matches `plugins/v1.12.0`, `plugins/v1.12.0-acme.1`
  and `plugins/v1.12.0-acme.12` (its `build` group `12`), and matches neither `plugins/v1.12.0-rc.1` nor
  `plugins/v1.12.0-other.1`; `companion.json` carries `ignoreUnstable: false`.
- GIVEN `--suffix` in `Acme`, `acme.1`, `ac-me`, `acme_1` or an empty value THEN the script exits 1 naming `--suffix` and
  the presets' bytes are unchanged.
- GIVEN the script run twice with the same arguments THEN the second run reports every file unchanged, and a run without
  `--suffix` restores the canonical template and `ignoreUnstable: true`.
- GIVEN a checkout whose version is `<X.Y.Z>-<x>.<n>` and whose presets are strict THEN `test/ci/releaseManifest.test.ts`
  fails with "run node scripts/fork-identity.mjs --suffix <x>"; GIVEN the canonical tree THEN it passes.

#### REQ-PLUGIN-033 A fork's gate passes as a fork, proved on every pull request (ADDED)

Added `<date>` (plan 016 file 1, units `u1-fork-neutral-tests`, `u1-git-isolation-headroom`, `u1-secret-shaped-fixtures`
and `u1-fork-ci-job`). A copy of this tree with a fork's identity passes the full gate unchanged: a suffixed version, a
renamed package, a documented `stamity.distribution.sources` value. A merge-blocking Linux CI job proves it on every pull
request in the canonical repository only. The job runs `scripts/ci/fork-probe.mjs`, which also runs locally: it copies
the tree to a temporary directory, applies `scripts/fork-identity.mjs` with a synthetic repository, a registry and
`--suffix fork`, sets the version to `<X.Y.Z>-fork.1`, adds a documented `sources` value, regenerates, runs each gate step
separately (gate, typecheck, lint, vitest, build, knip) plus both generators' `--check`, and runs
`npx -y npm@11 publish <tarball> --dry-run --tag latest`. The job joins `all-ci-checks`.

Three robustness rules hold for every fork and every laptop:
- tests that spawn git use a test-owned global git config (`GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` set by `test/support/globalSetup.ts`,
  `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`) and never read the operator's;
- the git-heavy cases carry a 60 s timeout; the default stays 20 s;
- no fixture under `test/` holds a real-format secret literal; such values are assembled at runtime.

- **Units:** `u1-fork-neutral-tests`, `u1-git-isolation-headroom`, `u1-secret-shaped-fixtures`, `u1-fork-ci-job` (which
  depends on the other three and on REQ-PLUGIN-031 and -032's units).
- **Evidence:** the fork probe of 2026-10-01 found 8 failures in 6 files, all fork-induced: `test/ci/distributionIdentity.test.ts:113`
  (pins `{branch, tagPattern}`, fails with documented `sources`), `test/ci/pluginDistribution.test.ts:397`,
  `test/ci/downstreamFixture.ts:100`, `test/emit/sharedCharterIdentity.test.ts:320` twice (`test/emit/goldenFixture.ts:52`
  holds the literal `@zomarit/stamity`; `runDriftGate` takes no package name, `src/cli/commands/check.ts:1106-1107`, and
  falls back to the checkout's, `src/cli/commands/sync/engine.ts:473`), and `test/ci/forkIdentityScript.test.ts:30-32`.
  `vitest.config.ts:18-22` injects only `TMPDIR`/`TEMP`/`TMP`, and 22 test files spawn git with the operator's global
  config. Secret-shaped literals sit at `test/mcp/secretScan.test.ts:13-20,107-108,115`,
  `test/cli/commands/validate.test.ts:325`, `test/cli/commands/check.test.ts:1097`, `test/merge/bakEscape.test.ts:202,357`
  and `test/merge/writeEscape.test.ts:211`. The opt-in `STAMITY_FORK_SUITE` group never runs in CI
  (`test/ci/forkIdentity.test.ts:321-410`).
- **Proof:** the CI job and `scripts/ci/fork-probe.mjs`; `test/ci/workflow.test.ts` pins the job; a guard test for secret
  literals; the full gate.

Criteria:
- GIVEN `ci.yml` THEN a fork-probe job runs on Linux, carries `if: github.repository == 'zomarit/stamity'`, and
  `all-ci-checks` needs it.
- GIVEN `node scripts/ci/fork-probe.mjs` on a clean checkout WHEN it runs THEN it exits 0, prints one result line per
  gate step and per generator check, and leaves the checkout's `git status --porcelain` output unchanged.
- GIVEN the fork copy the probe builds THEN its `package.json` carries a non-canonical scoped name, a version ending
  `-fork.1` and a `stamity.distribution.sources` value, and every gate step exits 0 there.
- GIVEN one of the six files above restored to its `88fcfd32` content WHEN the probe runs THEN it exits non-zero and names
  the failing step.
- GIVEN an operator global git config with `commit.gpgsign=true` and a `gpg.program` that always fails WHEN `npm test`
  runs THEN no test fails for that reason.
- GIVEN `vitest.config.ts` THEN `testTimeout` stays 20,000 ms, and each git-heavy case the unit names carries 60,000 ms.
- GIVEN a real-format secret literal added to any file under `test/` WHEN `npm test` runs THEN the guard test fails naming
  the file and line. The shape list it uses is the one the unit names (unverified: whether it reuses the leak gate's).
- GIVEN `CONTRIBUTING.md` THEN it names `--maxWorkers` for laptops running endpoint security.

#### REQ-PLUGIN-049 Fork releases can carry build attestations, and the canonical release attests every asset (ADDED)

Added `<date>` (plan 016 file 1, unit `u1-fork-attestations`). With the repository variable `STAMITY_RELEASE_ATTEST` set
to `true`, `.github/workflows/fork-release.yml` runs an `attest` job after a successful `publish`: it holds
`id-token: write`, `attestations: write` and `artifact-metadata: write` and nothing else, verifies the artifacts against
the `gates` outputs, and attests the tarball, every plugin archive, `release.json` as the distribution branch carries it
and `release.json` as the release asset carries it (stamped), with `actions/attest-build-provenance` at
`release.yml`'s pin. `publish` keeps no OIDC grant. Attestations need a public repository, or GitHub Enterprise Cloud for
a private or internal one; GitHub Enterprise Server has none. The canonical `release.yml` attests `release.json` (both
copies) and the tarball beside the archives. A fork still carries no npm provenance.

- **Evidence (before):** fork releases carry none, "until a company asks" (`fork-release.yml:23-28`, `:410-415`, `:795`;
  inbox ``"GitHub build attestations for fork releases are not built" at 077e8a78``); the canonical attests `plugins/*.zip` only (`release.yml:911-914`).
- **Proof:** `test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts`; `test/ci/workflow.test.ts`; the 1.12.0 cut's
  `gh attestation verify` of each canonical asset (file 3).

Criteria:
- GIVEN `fork-release.yml` THEN `attest` runs only on an armed run with `STAMITY_RELEASE_ATTEST` `true` whose publish
  succeeded, holds exactly the three grants, names no environment and reads no secret; any value other than `true` or
  empty fails the probe naming the variable.
- GIVEN the attest step THEN its subjects are the archives, both copies of `release.json` and the tarball.
- GIVEN `publish` THEN it holds no `id-token`.
- GIVEN `release.yml` THEN one step attests the archives, the branch copy of `release.json` and the tarball before the
  push, and a second attests the stamped `release.json` after the stamp and before the release is created.

#### REQ-PLUGIN-050 A fork release is rehearsed before its tag, states its distribution commit, and carries both changelogs (ADDED)

Added `<date>` (plan 016 file 1, units `u1-npm-dist-tag` (as amended) and `u1-fork-release-hardening`). Every armed run's
`gates` job runs `npm publish --dry-run` with the real registry and dist-tag before the environment approval; the guide
dispatches that rehearsal from the release branch and tags only once it is green. `publish` stamps
`distribution.commit` into the `release.json` it attaches, as `release.yml` does, leaving the branch copy `null`. The
release body carries upstream's `CHANGELOG.md` section for the base version and the fork's own `FORK-CHANGELOG.md`
section for the release, composed in `gates` and verified by digest in `publish`; a missing section is named in the
body, never a failure.

- **Evidence (before):** the dry run never ran npm (`fork-release.yml:803-847`); no stamp (`:751` computes the commit);
  a fixed body (`:793-797`).
- **Proof:** `test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts`; `test/docsPages.test.ts`.

Criteria:
- GIVEN `gates` THEN its one `npm publish` line carries `--dry-run`, the registry and the dist-tag, and runs on every armed
  trigger shape.
- GIVEN the release asset's `release.json` THEN `distribution.commit` is the distribution commit; the branch copy stays
  `null`.
- GIVEN a fork at `1.11.0-acme.2` with both changelog sections THEN the body carries both under their headings; without
  `FORK-CHANGELOG.md` THEN it says the fork has no notes.
- GIVEN the guide's `### Tag a release` THEN the rehearsal dispatch comes before the first `git tag`.

#### REQ-PLUGIN-051 A fork release refuses a public repository or package unless allowed (ADDED)

Added `<date>` (plan 016 file 1, unit `u1-fork-release-hardening`). The probe refuses a public repository, and a
repository whose visibility it cannot read, unless the repository variable `STAMITY_RELEASE_PUBLIC` is `true`; `publish`
repeats the check. On GitHub Packages, `publish` reads the package's visibility before publishing (a public package
refuses before npm; a missing package is a first publish) and after it (a public package fails the run naming its
settings); a read the per-run token cannot make is a warning. Other registries expose no visibility, and the run says so.

- **Evidence (before):** the only privacy check is `private` in the manifest (`fork-release.yml:221`, `:255-257`).
- **Proof:** `test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts`.

Criteria:
- GIVEN a public repository and no `STAMITY_RELEASE_PUBLIC` THEN the probe exits 1 naming the variable; with `true` THEN
  it passes; GIVEN an empty visibility THEN it exits 1.
- GIVEN GitHub Packages answering `public` before the publish THEN no npm call is made and the run fails; GIVEN a 404 THEN
  the publish proceeds; GIVEN a 403 THEN a warning and the publish proceeds.

#### REQ-PLUGIN-052 A fork's hand-written docs carry its identity (ADDED)

Added `<date>` (plan 016 file 1, unit `u1-fork-docs-identity`). `scripts/fork-identity.mjs` rewrites four spellings of
the canonical identity — the scoped package name, the issue and advisory URLs, `apm install <owner>/<repo>` and the
Renovate extends `github><owner>/<repo>//` — in nine pages (`README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md`,
`CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, and `docs/getting-started.md`, `migration.md`, `packs-and-trust.md`, `plugins.md`,
`troubleshooting.md`) to the fork's, and `--check` reports a page that carries another. The fork guide keeps naming
upstream where it means upstream and is not rewritten. Upstream's own pages use those four spellings only where a fork
should see its own identity. `test/docsPages.test.ts` derives the repository URLs and the package name from the checkout,
so a fork's gate passes on its rewritten pages.

- **Evidence (before):** 43 + 6 + 1 lines name the canonical identity and nothing rewrites them; `test/docsPages.test.ts:407`,
  `:830`, `:1576`, `:2790` fail a fork that fixed them.
- **Proof:** `test/ci/forkIdentityScript.test.ts`; `test/upstream/identityFiles.test.ts`; `test/docsPages.test.ts`.

Criteria:
- GIVEN a fork identity applied THEN none of the nine pages carries a rewriteable spelling of the canonical identity, and
  `docs/enterprise-forks.md` is unchanged.
- GIVEN the script rerun THEN every page is unchanged; GIVEN `--check` on a page that fell back THEN exit 1 naming it.
- GIVEN the canonical tree THEN every docs case passes with the derived values equal to today's literals.

#### REQ-PLUGIN-053 The fork version scheme is documented once and proved on every pull request (ADDED)

Added `<date>` (plan 016 file 1, units `u1-renovate-suffix` (as amended), `u1-lane-package-files` and `u1-fork-ci-job`
(as amended)). A fork versions its releases `<X.Y.Z>-<suffix>.<N>`: N starts at 1 for each new upstream base and counts
up for a re-release of the same base. The fork guide states the scheme and its four consequences once — npm needs a
dist-tag, Renovate needs the suffix-aware presets (`ignoreUnstable` and `respectLatest` false), an imported plain
`vX.Y.Z` tag sorts above every suffixed one, and the plugin locator accepts a companion at exactly the root's own
prerelease — and the canonical fork probe proves the dist-tag rule, the presets' ordering of `-fork.2` above `-fork.1`,
the companion's flags, the version rule, and the locator's acceptance of the fork's own prerelease.

- **Proof:** `test/ci/forkProbe.test.ts`; the probe's `version scheme` step; `test/ci/pluginLocate.test.ts`;
  `test/docsPages.test.ts`.

Criteria:
- GIVEN the probe's copy THEN its preset orders `plugins/v<X.Y.Z>-fork.2` above `-fork.1` and refuses `-rc.1`, and its
  companion carries both flags false.
- GIVEN the guide THEN `### Version a fork's releases` names the scheme, `STAMITY_RELEASE_DIST_TAG`, `--suffix`,
  `respectLatest` and the locator.

#### REQ-PLUGIN-027 A fork releases through its own workflow (MODIFIED)

Replaces `docs/specs/plugin-lifecycle.md:1333-1336`, "A fork's release is proved by checksums and the registry's own
authentication. It carries no provenance and no attestation: npm provenance needs a public source repository, and GitHub
attestations need GitHub Enterprise Cloud and are not built (decided 2026-09-24)." New text: "A fork's release is proved
by checksums and the registry's own authentication, and, when the fork sets `STAMITY_RELEASE_ATTEST`, by GitHub build
attestations (REQ-PLUGIN-049). It carries no npm provenance, which needs a public source repository." The criterion list
gains: "GIVEN a public repository THEN the probe refuses it unless `STAMITY_RELEASE_PUBLIC` is `true` (REQ-PLUGIN-051)."
(Amended `<date>`, plan 016 file 1, units `u1-fork-attestations` and `u1-fork-release-hardening`; it read "It carries no
provenance and no attestation".)

### B. `docs/specs/everyday-flows.md`

Each requirement's criteria land under `## Acceptance criteria` as a bold block headed by the requirement's id, in id order.

#### REQ-FLOW-027 — Setup then `clean` restores the repository byte for byte (ADDED)

From plan 016 file 1, units `u1-gitignore-lines` and `u1-import-config-round-trip`, with file 0's `u0-settings-ownership`
(`docs/plans/016-fork-distribution-00.md`). A
repository that runs setup (`init` or `plugin setup`) and then `clean -y`, with no user state written between them, is
byte-identical to what it was before setup. Four parts make that true:

- **`.gitignore` lines.** Setup appends a line only for a file it emits: `.env.mcp` only when an MCP server is selected,
  the review gate's three lines only when the review gate is emitted. The ledger records each appended line. `clean`
  removes exactly those lines — from a `.gitignore` that existed before setup too — and deletes the file only when the
  engine created it and it is empty afterwards.
- **Merged settings and hook entries.** `.claude/settings.json`, `.cursor/hooks.json` and `.codex/hooks.json` are owned
  per entry in the ledger, and `.codex/config.toml` per table (file 0's `u0-settings-ownership` and
  `u0-hook-files-ownership` in `docs/plans/016-fork-distribution-00.md`, file 0's per-entry settings and hook-file
  requirements). `clean` removes the
  engine's entries, keeps every foreign one, writes no `.bak` when only foreign content survives, and deletes the file
  only when the engine created it and nothing foreign remains (REQ-PLUGIN-016 as amended).
- **The import-config round trip.** After `init --import-config supplement` or `--import-config replace`, `clean -y`
  restores the original `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` byte for byte, with no added blank line and no leftover
  `AGENTS.md.bak`.
- **Pack removal.** `clean --pack <id>` removes that pack's client copies at once, so `check` passes right after it.

- **Units:** `u1-gitignore-lines`, `u1-import-config-round-trip`; the per-entry part is file 0's `u0-settings-ownership`
  (`docs/plans/016-fork-distribution-00.md`), whose core is a shared contract file 2's `u2-declare` builds on.
- **Evidence (before):** `.env.mcp` is appended unconditionally (`src/cli/commands/init/apply.ts:430`,
  `src/cli/commands/sync/engine.ts:782`) from the four-entry list (`src/mcp/env.ts:193-198`), and `clean` leaves the lines
  on purpose (`src/cli/commands/clean.ts:73`, `:696`). The co-owned reclaim backs up on a whole-file hash
  (`src/merge/reclaim.ts:685-688`). Measured 2026-10-01: `supplement` leaves two blank lines at the top of `AGENTS.md`
  and `CLAUDE.md` after `clean`; `replace` deletes `AGENTS.md` and keeps only an untracked `AGENTS.md.bak`; `clean --pack`
  leaves 12 client copies until the next `sync`, and `check` exits 1 with `reclaimPending: 12`.
- **Expand/contract:** a ledger from 1.11.0 records no `.gitignore` line and no per-entry hash. Lines a 1.11.0
  setup wrote carry no row, so `clean` leaves them, as 1.11.0 does (settings and hook entries follow the legacy rule
  of file 0's per-entry settings requirement, `u0-settings-ownership` in `docs/plans/016-fork-distribution-00.md`); the first `sync` under the new engine records rows
  only for what it writes itself. Rollback is a re-sync at the prior version. (This adoption rule is the draft's reading;
  the unit confirms it against the ledger schema.)
- **Proof:** round-trip suites over fixture repositories for `init`, `plugin setup` and `clean`; `test/mcp/env.test.ts`;
  `test/merge/settingsKeyOwnership.test.ts`; QA.

Criteria (merged as **REQ-FLOW-027**):
- GIVEN a fixture repository with no `.gitignore` WHEN `plugin setup` runs with no MCP server selected THEN no
  `.gitignore` line names `.env.mcp`.
- GIVEN a fixture repository whose tracked files and `.gitignore` are recorded WHEN `init -y` for all four clients and
  then `clean -y` run THEN every file that existed before is byte-identical, no other file exists, and
  `git status --porcelain --ignored` prints what it printed before setup.
- GIVEN the same with `plugin setup` instead of `init` THEN the same holds.
- GIVEN a `.gitignore` that existed before setup WHEN setup and then `clean -y` run THEN it is byte-identical to before.
- GIVEN a `.claude/settings.json` to which another tool added a key after setup WHEN `clean -y` runs THEN the file holds
  that key alone and no `.bak` exists.
- GIVEN an `AGENTS.md` and a `CLAUDE.md` WHEN `init -y --import-config supplement` and then `clean -y` run THEN both are
  byte-identical to before; WHEN `--import-config replace` is used instead THEN the same holds and no `AGENTS.md.bak`
  remains.
- GIVEN an installed pack WHEN `clean --pack <id>` runs THEN no client copy of that pack remains, and `check` exits 0
  with no `reclaimPending` entry.
- Two residues are content-equal rather than byte-identical, and the unit records them: a pre-existing JSON document
  written in another style comes back with the same keys and values, and an imported original that had leading
  whitespace or no final newline comes back with the same text. A repository set up by 1.11.0 or earlier records no
  member hashes until its first `sync` under the new engine; until then `clean` keeps 1.11.0's whole-file rule.

**Amended 2026-10-06 (the updated brief) — REQ-FLOW-027 — Setup then `clean` restores the repository byte for byte (ADDED in this file; text amended):**
- Its last criterion read "Two residues are content-equal rather than byte-identical, and the unit records them: a
  pre-existing JSON document written in another style comes back with the same keys and values, and an imported original
  that had leading whitespace or no final newline comes back with the same text." New text: "One residue is
  content-equal rather than byte-identical, and the unit records it: a pre-existing JSON document written in another
  style comes back with the same keys and values. An imported original comes back byte for byte, leading whitespace, BOM
  and missing final newline included." (`u1-import-config-round-trip`; inbox ``"`prependManagedBlock`'s `trimStart` and final-newline normalisation keep a supplemented file's round trip inexact" at 077e8a78``.)
- New criteria: "GIVEN a committed manifest whose `.gitignore` row lists a line outside `REQUIRED_GITIGNORE_ENTRIES` WHEN
  `sync` or `clean` runs THEN it stops with `CONFIG_ERROR` naming the row and `.gitignore` is byte-identical."
  (`u1-gitignore-lines`) · "GIVEN an import decision whose `backup.path` is not `<path>.bak` or `<path>.bak.<8 hex>`
  beside the file THEN validation names it and `clean` moves nothing." (`u1-import-config-round-trip`)

#### REQ-FLOW-028 — `clean` keeps user state unless `--purge` (ADDED)

From plan 016 file 1, unit `u1-clean-keeps-state`. `clean -y` removes the files the engine owns and keeps user state:
learnings, handoffs, overrides, installed packs (their install stays; their client copies go), run records, the user
hooks folder, the inbox and verify artifacts. `clean -y --purge` removes `.stamity/` entirely. `clean --dry-run` lists
what it would remove and what it keeps. The generated `st-setup` body says what `clean -y` keeps and what `--purge`
removes.

- **Units:** `u1-clean-keeps-state`.
- **Evidence (before):** `clean -y` deletes the whole state directory (`src/cli/commands/clean.ts:229-244`; the `rm` at
  `:235`), and no flag keeps state. The `st-setup` body says `clean -y` removes ledger rows and deletes the whole
  `.stamity/` directory unless a hooks file it keeps still runs a script there
  (`scripts/plugins/setupCommand.mjs:172-176`; an inbox Warning row).
- **Expand/contract:** the default narrows; nothing a user owns is deleted that was kept before. A script that relied on
  `clean -y` emptying `.stamity/` adds `--purge`; the CHANGELOG says so. Rollback is the prior version's `clean`.
- **Proof:** `test/cli/commands/clean.test.ts`; the `st-setup` body pins in `test/ci/`; the adversarial eval case for
  `st-setup` with its `source:` range moved; QA.

Criteria (merged as **REQ-FLOW-028**):
- GIVEN a setup with one learning, one handoff, one override, one installed pack, one run record, one user hook and one
  inbox row WHEN `clean -y` runs THEN each of those files is byte-identical, the pack's client copies are gone, and no
  engine-owned file remains.
- GIVEN the same WHEN `clean -y --purge` runs THEN `.stamity/` does not exist.
- GIVEN `clean --dry-run` THEN it lists both sets, the removals and the kept files, and the tree is unchanged.
- GIVEN the generated `st-setup` body for each client THEN it names `--purge` as the way to remove `.stamity/` and says
  that `clean -y` keeps user state.

**Amended 2026-10-06 (the updated brief) — REQ-FLOW-028 — `clean` keeps user state unless `--purge` (ADDED in this file; text amended):**
Its list "learnings, handoffs, overrides, installed packs (their install stays; their client copies go), run records,
the user hooks folder, the inbox and verify artifacts" gains "and `sync`'s backups under `.stamity/backups/`
(REQ-FLOW-046)". (`u1-clean-keeps-state`.)

#### REQ-FLOW-029 — `init --force` never duplicates the charter (ADDED)

From plan 016 file 1, unit `u1-init-force-charter`. `init --force` recognises the charter the engine wrote, with or
without its markers, and replaces it. It never treats it as user content to keep beside a new copy.

- **Units:** `u1-init-force-charter`.
- **Evidence (before):** measured 2026-10-01, `init --force` over the engine's own unmarked `AGENTS.md` doubles
  `# Charter` (10,711 bytes) and `check` passes. Unverified: the code site; the unit names it.
- **Proof:** `test/cli/commands/init.test.ts`; QA.

Criteria (merged as **REQ-FLOW-029**):
- GIVEN `init -y` WHEN `init -y --force` runs THEN `AGENTS.md` holds exactly one `# Charter` heading and is
  byte-identical to the file the first `init` wrote.
- GIVEN the engine's charter with its markers removed WHEN `init -y --force` runs THEN `AGENTS.md` holds exactly one
  `# Charter` heading.
- GIVEN an `AGENTS.md` with user prose and no charter WHEN `init -y --force --import-config supplement` runs THEN the user
  prose survives byte for byte.

#### REQ-FLOW-030 — The CLI never writes a path another tool owns (ADDED)

From plan 016 file 1, unit `u1-foreign-paths`. The CLI reads `apm.lock.yaml` and treats every path the lock records as
deployed as foreign. It treats a pre-existing folder at an emitted path that no ledger row owns, and whose bytes differ from what the engine
would write, the same way; a folder that already holds exactly the engine's bytes is adopted. It never
writes inside a foreign path. Where a write would collide, it refuses and prints a remedy that never overwrites: remove
the duplicate, or (once file 2 lands) use the APM-backed mode. No remedy it prints offers `--force` as the way past a
foreign path.

- **Units:** `u1-foreign-paths`. The lock reader is a shared contract: file 2's
  `u2-apm-backed-mode` reuses it.
- **Evidence (before):** `src/` holds no `apm.lock.yaml` reader. Measured 2026-10-01 next to an APM package: `init` exits
  0 and marks colliding files `skipped`, yet writes `agents/openai.yaml` into the foreign `.agents/skills/st-qa/`;
  `sync` and `check` exit 1 offering `--force`, which would overwrite.
- **Proof:** a fixture with a hand-written `apm.lock.yaml` and deployed files, under `test/cli/commands/`; QA.

Criteria (merged as **REQ-FLOW-030**):
- GIVEN a fixture whose `apm.lock.yaml` records `.agents/skills/st-qa/` as deployed WHEN `init -y --tools cursor` runs THEN
  no file under `.agents/skills/st-qa/` changes or appears, and the report names that path as foreign.
- GIVEN the same WHEN `sync` and then `check` run THEN each exits 1 naming the path, and neither output contains
  `--force`.
- GIVEN an untracked, unledgered folder at an emitted skill path THEN the same holds.
- GIVEN no `apm.lock.yaml` and no foreign folder THEN `init`, `sync` and `check` behave byte for byte as before.

**Amended 2026-10-06 (the updated brief) — REQ-FLOW-030 — The CLI never writes a path another tool owns (ADDED in this file; text amended):**
Its last sentence read "No remedy it prints offers `--force` as the way past a foreign path." New text: "No remedy
`check` or `sync` prints offers `--force` for a collision it cannot clear — a foreign path, a screen refusal, a hard link
or a linked source; `--force` is offered only for an unproven file it replaces behind a verified `.bak`." New criterion:
"GIVEN a hard-linked `AGENTS.md` or a screen-refused line WHEN `check --json` and `sync` run THEN neither output contains
`--force`." (`u1-foreign-paths`.)

#### REQ-FLOW-031 — The Copilot setup-steps workflow is controllable, and its managed form pins full SHAs (ADDED)

From plan 016 file 1, unit `u1-copilot-setup-steps`. A config key `copilot.setupSteps` takes `managed`, `user` or `off`,
default `managed`.

- `managed`: the engine writes `.github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml`, and every `uses:` line in it pins a full
  40-character commit SHA with the tag as a trailing comment, taken from a table in the engine.
- `user`: the engine writes nothing to the file, leaves an existing file in place, stops tracking it, and `check`
  reports no drift for it.
- `off`: the engine writes no such file and reclaims one it wrote earlier; a file it did not write stays.

The plugin and getting-started docs list the file, say that writing a workflow file needs the `workflows` permission,
and name the key.

Settled (file 1, decision S8): in `managed` mode a full 40-hex SHA pin found in the project's own workflows is
reused; otherwise the engine's table decides, with the tag as a trailing comment. A tag-only project ref such as
`@v7` is never copied. The `user` and `off` meanings above stand.

- **Units:** `u1-copilot-setup-steps`.
- **Evidence (before):** the fallback ref is `DEFAULT_ACTION_REF = "v5"` (`src/adapters/copilot.ts:618`), used by
  `actionUse` (`:621-623`; also `:535-537`, `:590`, `:599`); the file is written in both modes whenever Copilot is
  selected (`:270-273`, `:314-318`); no opt-out exists without `check` drift, and `sync` reverts an edit behind a `.bak`;
  `docs/plugins.md:334-353` does not name the file.
- **Proof:** `test/adapters/copilot.test.ts`, the config-key tests, `test/docsPages.test.ts`; QA.

Criteria (merged as **REQ-FLOW-031**):
- GIVEN Copilot selected and no `copilot.setupSteps` set WHEN `sync` runs THEN the workflow exists and every `uses:` line
  pins a full 40-hex commit SHA, and each line taken from the engine table carries `# v<version>`.
- GIVEN `copilot.setupSteps: user` and an edited workflow WHEN `sync` and `check` run THEN the file is byte-identical to
  the edit, no `.bak` exists, and `check` reports no drift for it.
- GIVEN `copilot.setupSteps: off` and a workflow the engine wrote earlier WHEN `sync` runs THEN the file is gone; GIVEN a
  workflow the engine never wrote THEN it stays.
- GIVEN any other value THEN `config set` exits 1 naming the three values.
- GIVEN `docs/plugins.md` and `docs/getting-started.md` THEN each names `copilot-setup-steps.yml`, the `workflows`
  permission and the key.

**Amended 2026-10-06 (the updated brief) — REQ-FLOW-031 — The Copilot setup-steps workflow is controllable, and its managed form pins full SHAs (ADDED in this file; text amended):**
The paragraph "Settled (file 1, decision S8): in `managed` mode a full 40-hex SHA pin found in the project's own
workflows is reused; otherwise the engine's table decides, with the tag as a trailing comment. A tag-only project ref
such as `@v7` is never copied. The `user` and `off` meanings above stand." becomes "Settled (file 1, decision S8 as
revised 2026-10-06): in `managed` mode every action ref is the engine table's full 40-hex SHA with the tag as a trailing
comment, and no project workflow is read. When Copilot hooks are emitted, a repository with no Node evidence also gets
`setup-node` at the engine's floor. The `user` and `off` meanings above stand." New criteria: "GIVEN Copilot hooks emitted
in a repository with no Node evidence THEN the workflow sets up Node `22.22.2` with a SHA-pinned `setup-node`." · "GIVEN
the owner's workflow pins of `actions/checkout` bumped after `sync` THEN `check` reports no drift."
(`u1-copilot-setup-steps`.)

#### REQ-FLOW-032 — A no-op `sync` leaves every file byte-identical (ADDED)

From plan 016 file 1, unit `u1-manifest-stable`. A `sync` whose inputs did not change writes no byte. The manifest's
`updatedAt` moves only when a manifest entry changes. The version string leaves every place where it is not a pin: the
`STAMITY:BEGIN v<version>` marker in `CLAUDE.md` is the measured example.

Settled (file 1, decision S9): the pinned call form the everyday-flows spec requires (`npx -y <package>@<version> <verb>`) is a
pin and keeps the version, because that spec pins the call so it never runs `@latest`; only places that are not pins
drop it.

- **Units:** `u1-manifest-stable`.
- **Evidence (before):** every no-op `sync` rewrites `updatedAt` in `.stamity/manifest.json` (two identical fixtures differ
  only in `createdAt`/`updatedAt`; REQ-UPSTREAM-016 records the restamp at
  `docs/specs/enterprise-upstream-lane.md:494-496`); the version sits in 22–25 files; 16 of 79 files in a 1.10.0 → 1.11.0
  sync changed only the version (measured 2026-10-01).
- **Proof:** `test/cli/commands/syncEngine.test.ts`, the cross-client goldens; QA.

Criteria (merged as **REQ-FLOW-032**):
- GIVEN a setup WHEN `sync` runs twice with no input change THEN the second run reports 0 changed files and
  `.stamity/manifest.json` is byte-identical, `updatedAt` included.
- GIVEN a sync that changes one emitted file THEN `updatedAt` moves.
- GIVEN `CLAUDE.md` after `sync` THEN its managed-block marker carries no version string.
- GIVEN two engines at different versions over one unchanged corpus and selection WHEN each runs `sync` THEN the files
  that differ are exactly the manifest and the pin sites: the pinned `npx -y <package>@<version>` calls and the
  `package.json` pin.

**Amended 2026-10-06 (the updated brief) — REQ-FLOW-032 — A no-op `sync` leaves every file byte-identical (ADDED in this file; text amended):**
Its last criterion gains "… and `.codex/hooks.json` is byte-identical: the remedy text in a file a client trusts by hash
carries no version." New criteria: "GIVEN `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` set WHEN `init` runs twice on identical inputs THEN the
trees are byte-identical and `createdAt` is that instant; GIVEN a malformed value THEN `init` exits 1 and writes nothing."
· "GIVEN a sync that rewrote the manifest THEN `sync --json` carries `manifestWritten: true`." (`u1-manifest-stable`.)

#### REQ-FLOW-035 — A hook that cannot start Node says so, and the guards stay closed (ADDED)

From plan 016 file 1, unit `u1-node-missing-hint`. When `node` is not on `PATH`, an emitted hook fails as it does today:
the guards stay closed (the security floor). Its message names Node, the floor `22.22.2` and how to install it. It no
longer tells the operator to run `stamity sync`, which needs Node too.

  127; Cursor's `subagentStart` and `beforeMCPExecution` are `failClosed: true` (measured 2026-10-01). The floor is
  `engines.node` `>=22.22.2` in `package.json`.
- **Proof:** hook-command tests per adapter under `test/adapters/` and `test/hooks/`; QA.

Criteria (merged as **REQ-FLOW-035**):
- GIVEN Claude's emitted PreToolUse command run with a `PATH` holding no `node` THEN it exits 2, and stderr names Node and
  `22.22.2` and does not name `stamity sync`.
- GIVEN each client's emitted hook commands THEN every command that carries a fallback message carries the same Node
  message, and no hook that blocks today stops blocking.
- GIVEN Cursor's emitted hook configuration THEN `subagentStart` and `beforeMCPExecution` stay `failClosed: true`.
- What each client shows for a hook command with no fallback message is that client's own error; the capability
  disclosure states it per client, measured at execution.

**Amended 2026-10-06 (the updated brief) — REQ-FLOW-035 — A hook that cannot start Node says so, and every governed call stays blocked (ADDED in this file; text amended):**
The title ended "and the guards stay closed". The opening sentences "When `node` is not on `PATH`, an emitted hook fails as
