| `interfaces` | **Current.** `privateTempRoot(setupFile, base, pid)` returns `{ env: { TMPDIR, TEMP, TMP }, globalSetup: ["test/support/globalSetup.ts"] }`, or `{}` when the setup file is absent (`vitest.config.ts:18-22`); `test.env` reaches every worker and every child spawned with `process.env`. Three helpers already cut git's configuration for their own children (`GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`: `test/support/cliHarness.ts:103-104`, `test/support/repoFixtures.ts:173-174`, `test/upstream/fixtures.ts:95-96`). 20 test files spawn git with the inherited environment (census at `88fcfd32`: `test/ci/downstreamFixture.ts`, `evidenceArchive`, `forkIdentity`, `forkIdentityScript`, `packSigningRehearsal`, `pluginDistribution`, `pluginDownstream`, `pluginLifecycle`, `pluginRuntime`, `repoHygiene`, `test/cli/commands/check`, `sync`, `syncEngine`, `test/cli/docs/cliReference`, `test/evals/manualRunner`, `test/gate/leakGateEvasion`, `test/hooks/scripts`, `test/qa/run`, `test/records/ledgers`, `test/records/specStatus`), so an operator's `commit.gpgsign`, `gpg.program`, hooks, `include.path` or a system configuration reach them. `setup(project)` (`test/support/globalSetup.ts:110-115`) reads `project.config.env["TMPDIR"]`, prepares the root and returns its removal. `testTimeout` and `hookTimeout` are 20,000 ms (`vitest.config.ts:85-86`, pinned `test/ci/testScheduling.test.ts:68-69,94-95`). The probe's loaded full-suite run (2026-10-01, 337 s, `fork-probe/vitest.json`) re-read against the source: of the cases at ≥5,000 ms that its script listed as "default", only these really run under the 20 s default — the slow cases of `test/upstream/workflowRecovery.test.ts`'s one describe (`:34`; 13 cases at ≥4 s, the slowest 9,318 ms; git-heavy fork fixtures; skipped on Windows) and `test/merge/coverageGaps.test.ts:591` (8,054 ms; it stubs `win32` and so sleeps through the whole win32 rename schedule, 6,950 ms of base delay and an 8,687.5 ms ceiling, `src/merge/atomicWrite.ts:969-994`, which matches the enterprise's "20.06 s on a GitHub Windows runner for a test that normally takes 8 s"). The others already carry budgets: `test/cli/dogfoodDist.e2e.test.ts:112-113` 240 s, `test/cli/flows.e2e.test.ts:71-72` 180 s, `test/cli/kit/packageName.test.ts:455` 60 s, `test/docsPages.test.ts:1011` 180 s, `test/ci/leakGate.test.ts:68` 180 s, `test/qa/hookRuns.test.ts:220` 30 s. The flows case's 20 s exposure is its fixture hook: `useCliFixture`'s `beforeEach` and `afterEach` (`cliHarness.ts:298-306`) seed and remove a git repository under the 20 s hook default; `workflowRecovery`'s `beforeAll` (`:38`, `createUpstream`) builds a tagged upstream under it too. **New, config.** `privateTempRoot` returns `env: { TMPDIR: root, TEMP: root, TMP: root, GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL: join(root, "gitconfig"), GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM: "1" }` (still `{}` without the setup file); its comment says why: a test's git must not read the operator's global or system configuration (signing, hooks, includes, credential helpers), and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` replaces both `~/.gitconfig` and `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/git/config` (git 2.32 or newer). **New, setup.** Exported `TEST_GIT_CONFIG` (string) = `# Written by test/support/globalSetup.ts for one vitest run, and removed with its temp root.\n[user]\n\tname = Stamity Test Suite\n\temail = test-suite@example.invalid\n[commit]\n\tgpgsign = false\n[tag]\n\tgpgsign = false\n[init]\n\tdefaultBranch = main\n[core]\n\tautocrlf = false\n\tfscache = true\n` (`fscache` is a Git for Windows switch the skipped system configuration normally carries; other gits ignore an unknown key; the `.invalid` address passes the leak gate's email rule). Exported `writeTestGitConfig(path: string, root: string): boolean` writes it only when `dirname(path) === root` and returns whether it wrote. `setup(project)` calls `prepareTempRoot`, then `writeTestGitConfig(project.config.env["GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL"], root)` when that value is a string; the teardown is the root's (`:114`). The module still imports only `node:` (held by `test/support/support.test.ts`). **New, budgets.** `workflowRecovery.test.ts:34` → `describe.skipIf(!SHELL_AVAILABLE)("…", { timeout: GIT_HEAVY_TIMEOUT_MS }, () => {` (vitest 5 hands a suite's options to each test in it: `{ ...currentSuite?.options, ...options }` then `options.timeout ?? runner.config.testTimeout`, `node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/run.C5UmxDPh.js:2623-2628`), and `beforeAll(() => { … }, GIT_HEAVY_TIMEOUT_MS)` at `:38`; `coverageGaps.test.ts:591-630` gains the third argument `WIN32_RENAME_BUDGET_TIMEOUT_MS` at `:630`; `useCliFixture`'s two hooks take `FIXTURE_HOOK_TIMEOUT_MS` as their second argument. Each constant is 60_000 with a derivation comment in the style of `test/upstream/lane.test.ts:105-121`: the loaded maximum (9.3 s; 8.1 s; the flows case's whole run, hook included, 6.3 s), the enterprise's endpoint-security ratio (20.06 s for an 8 s case, 2.5x) and the margin left. The 20 s defaults stay (declared default 4). **Docs.** `CONTRIBUTING.md:56-58` gains two sentences: the suite gives every git it spawns a test-owned configuration (an identity, no signing, `main`), so your own `~/.gitconfig` and system configuration never reach a test; the suite runs at most four workers, the count the enterprise's endpoint-security laptops passed with; set `STAMITY_TEST_MAX_WORKERS` (a whole number, or a percentage such as `50%`) to change it, or pass `--maxWorkers` for one run. Header `:1` → the commit form with the date `u1-npm-dist-tag` set. **Follows:** `test/upstream/lane.test.ts:105-121` (a named budget constant with its derivation) and `test/support/globalSetup.ts:80-94` (an idempotent setup step that never throws). |
| `testCriteria` | **Given** `privateTempRoot(SETUP, "/base", 4242)`, **then** it equals `{ env: { TMPDIR: root, TEMP: root, TMP: root, GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL: join(root, "gitconfig"), GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM: "1" }, globalSetup: ["test/support/globalSetup.ts"] }` (`testScheduling.test.ts:104-112`, re-pinned with `TEST CHANGE, justified:`), and with an absent setup file `{}` (`:114-117`, unchanged). **Given** the Windows scheduling (`:38-86`), **then** both projects' `env.GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` and `env.GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM` equal the root config's. **Given** this worker (`:119-127`, extended), **then** `process.env.GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` names an existing file inside `tmpdir()`'s root whose text equals `TEST_GIT_CONFIG`; `git config --global --get user.email`, spawned with `process.env`, prints `test-suite@example.invalid`; `git config --get commit.gpgsign` prints `false`; `git init` in a scratch dir followed by `git symbolic-ref HEAD` prints `refs/heads/main`. **Given** a scratch `HOME` whose `.gitconfig` sets `commit.gpgsign = true`, `gpg.program = false` (a binary that always fails) and `user.email = operator@example.invalid`, **when** `git commit --allow-empty -m x` runs in a scratch repository with `{ ...process.env, HOME: <scratch> }`, **then** it exits 0 and the commit's author email is the test identity (the REQ-PLUGIN-033 operator-config criterion). **Given** `setup({ config: { env: { TMPDIR: root, GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL: join(root, "gitconfig") } } })`, **then** the file exists with `TEST_GIT_CONFIG` and the teardown removes it with the root; **given** a `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` outside the root, **then** no file is written outside it and setup does not throw. **Given** the three budgets, **then** the describe at `workflowRecovery.test.ts:34` and its `beforeAll`, `coverageGaps.test.ts:591`'s case and `useCliFixture`'s two hooks pass 60,000, and `testScheduling.test.ts:68-69,94-95` still read 20,000. **Given** `npm run test`, **then** it exits 0 on darwin and Linux, and CI's two Windows shards are green with Test-step durations within 20% of main's last green runs (`gh run view <id> --json jobs`). |

A child spawned with an env built from scratch → it never inherited the operator's configuration; unchanged. git older than 2.32 ignores `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` → today's behaviour; the existing helpers already rely on the variable. A copy of `vitest.config.ts` without `test/support/` (the downstream fixtures) → `privateTempRoot` returns `{}` and no git env, as today. Windows: `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` also drops Git for Windows' system `core.fscache`, `core.symlinks` and credential helper; `fscache = true` restores the first, the symlink cases already skip on Windows, and no test may reach a credential helper. unverified: whether the `windows-latest` image's system configuration sets `core.fscache`; probe: a temporary `git config --system --list` step on a windows leg, or the Test-duration comparison above. |

**Amended 2026-10-06 (the updated brief; read at `d10db029`; sentences of the table above that this amendment replaces are already replaced in place):**
- **files, added:** `vitest.config.ts` (a new export beside `privateTempRoot`; the root `test` block `:73-86`), `test/ci/testScheduling.test.ts` (`:63`; new cases).
- **interfaces, added:** **Workers.** Vitest 5.0.2 runs `max(availableParallelism() − 1, 1)` workers when neither the project nor the root config names `maxWorkers` (`node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/index.C-uw7tH9.js:11777-11782`); this config names none at its root (`vitest.config.ts:73-86`; only the Windows heavy group's `maxWorkers: 1`, `:65`), so a 14-core laptop runs 13 workers, the maintainer's 8-core machine 7, and CI's 4-vCPU runners 3. New export `testWorkers(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv, cores: number): number | string` — `env.STAMITY_TEST_MAX_WORKERS` matching `^[1-9]\d*$` → that number; matching `^(?:100|[1-9]\d?)%$` → that percentage; empty or absent → `Math.max(1, Math.min(cores - 1, TEST_WORKER_CAP))` with `export const TEST_WORKER_CAP = 4`, the count the enterprise's endpoint-security laptops passed with; anything else throws `Error("STAMITY_TEST_MAX_WORKERS must be a positive whole number or a percentage such as 50%; got <value>")` at config load. The root `test` block gains `maxWorkers: testWorkers(process.env, availableParallelism())`; vitest's own `--maxWorkers` still overrides it for one run. CI keeps 3 workers; the 8-core machine goes from 7 to 4.
- **testCriteria, added:** **Given** `testWorkers`, **then** `({}, 14)` is 4, `({}, 4)` is 3, `({}, 1)` is 1, `({ STAMITY_TEST_MAX_WORKERS: "7" }, 4)` is 7, `"50%"` gives `"50%"`, and `"0"`, `"-1"`, `"x"`, `"150%"` throw naming the variable (`test/ci/testScheduling.test.ts`). **Given** the config, **then** `test.maxWorkers` equals `testWorkers(process.env, availableParallelism())`; **given** the Windows scheduling case, **then** the parallel group's `maxWorkers` equals the root value (TEST CHANGE of `:63`, justified: the root now names a cap, which the group inherits) and the heavy group's stays 1.
- **edgeCases, added:** A downstream copy of `vitest.config.ts` without the support tree → the cap still applies; it does not depend on the setup file.

file 02 `u2-review-gate-all` extends it) · `export function requiredGitignoreEntries(input: { tools: readonly Tool[]; envVarsRequired: boolean; present: ReadonlySet<string> }): string[]` — in `REQUIRED_GITIGNORE_ENTRIES` order: `.env.mcp` when `envVarsRequired \|\| present.has(".env.mcp")`; each gate entry when `tools` meets `REVIEW_GATE_TOOLS` or `present.has(entry)` · `export async function presentGitignoreTargets(rootDir: string): Promise<Set<string>>` — `.env.mcp`, `.stamity/review-gate.json` and `.stamity/review-gate.json.lock` when `lstat` finds anything there; the temp entry when `readdir(.stamity)` holds a name starting `review-gate.json.tmp-` · `export function reduceGitignoreToForeignContent(raw: string, record: CoOwnership, keep: ReadonlySet<string>): CoOwnedReduction` (pure) — for each `record.lines` entry not in `keep`, remove the LAST line whose trimmed text equals it, with that line's terminator; when the removed lines were the file's last lines and `record.terminatorAdded`, drop one more trailing `\r\n`/`\n`; nothing removed → `untouched`; result `""` and `record.createdFile` → `engine-only`; otherwise `reduced` with the remaining bytes (an empty file is kept when the engine did not create it); every arm carries `proven: true` · `export function gitignoreReclaimReducer(record: CoOwnership, keep: ReadonlySet<string>): CoOwnedReducer` · `export async function reconcileGitignore(rootDir: string, opts: { required: readonly string[]; previous: CoOwnership \| null }): Promise<{ added: string[]; removed: string[]; record: CoOwnership \| null }>` — removes owned lines not in `required` (the reducer's rule), appends the `required` entries `isCovered` does not cover with today's separator logic (`:643-646`), writes only when something changed (through `refuseRepublish` then `atomicWriteFile(path, content, { boundaryDir: rootDir })`), and when the result is empty in an engine-created file `unlink`s it after `lstat` proves a regular unshared file; `record` = `{ lines: <owned lines still present> ∪ added, createdFile: previous?.createdFile \|\| <file absent before>, terminatorAdded: previous?.terminatorAdded \|\| <a separator was added now> }` (optional flags omitted when false), `null` when `lines` is empty · `export function gitignoreRecordOf(ledger: readonly LedgerEntry[]): CoOwnership \| null` (union of the `.gitignore` rows' `coOwned`) · `export function gitignoreLedgerRows(tools: readonly Tool[], record: CoOwnership \| null): LedgerEntry[]` — one row per selected tool in `TOOLS` order, `{ path: ".gitignore", adapter, artifactId: "gitignore", artifactType: "infra", coOwned: record }`; `[]` for `null`. `ensureGitignoreEntry(rootDir, entries: readonly string[] = REQUIRED_GITIGNORE_ENTRIES)` keeps its behaviour for its remaining caller and its tests. **`src/manifest/ledger.ts`:** `export function replacePathRows(ledger: readonly LedgerEntry[], path: string, rows: readonly LedgerEntry[]): LedgerEntry[]` — drops every non-pack row at `path`, appends `rows`, clones and stable-sorts by path like `replaceAdapterEntries` (`:122-129`). **Callers:** `sync` replaces `:782` with `required = requiredGitignoreEntries({ tools: plan.manifest.tools, envVarsRequired: collectRequiredEnvVars(plan.manifest.mcp?.servers ?? [], packMcpSupply).length > 0, present: await presentGitignoreTargets(rootDir) })` and `reconcileGitignore(rootDir, { required, previous: gitignoreRecordOf(plan.manifest.ledger) })` at the same point (before the first emitted write, so a refusal still stops the run with nothing written); it pushes `gitignoreLedgerRows(plan.manifest.tools, record)` into `emitted` before the rebuild; `SyncApplyReport.gitignoreRemoved?: string[]` sits beside `gitignoreAdded`; `planSync` drops `.gitignore` candidates from `reclaim` (the apply reconciles them). `report.ts` prints `  .gitignore: removed <list>` after the added line and adds `gitignoreRemoved` to the JSON. `init` computes `required` in both modes (`InitApplyReport.gitignoreRequired: string[]`), runs `reconcileGitignore` on live runs after the write loop and BEFORE the ledger loop (`previous` = the `.gitignore` record of the previous manifest that file 0's `readPreviousManifest` reads once under `--force`, `src/cli/commands/init/apply.ts`), adds the rows to `emittedByAdapter`, and reports `gitignoreAdded` and `gitignoreRemoved: string[]`; `init.ts:785` passes `report.gitignoreRequired`. `config mcp add` replaces `:465` with the same reconcile over `manifest.tools` and the new server list, writing `ledger: replacePathRows(manifest.ledger, GITIGNORE_FILE, gitignoreLedgerRows(manifest.tools, record))` with the manifest it already writes (`:466-469`). The carry calls `ensureGitignoreEntry(root, [ENV_MCP_FILE])` (the credential file it placed keeps its rule; unrecorded, so `clean` leaves file and line alike). `coOwnedReclaimReducers(manifest, packServers, opts: { gitignoreKeep?: ReadonlySet<string> } = {})` maps `.gitignore` → `gitignoreReclaimReducer(record, opts.gitignoreKeep ?? new Set())` when `gitignoreRecordOf` finds a record. Full `clean` passes `gitignoreKeep` = `{".env.mcp"}` when `.env.mcp` exists (this era's clean then deletes `.stamity/`, gate state included; `u1-clean-keeps-state` widens the set), and its header and closing line say `clean` removes the lines it added. **Docs:** `docs/getting-started.md:346-356` — each line is added only when it is needed (MCP credentials; the review gate's state on Claude Code), and `clean` removes the ones it added. **Follows:** `src/mcp/env.ts:634-649` (the gated, boundary-pinned append) and `src/manifest/claudeSettings.ts:625-672` (reducer shape). **Backward compatibility:** a ≤1.11.0 ledger has no `.gitignore` row; the lines 1.11.0 appended are covered, so nothing is appended or owned and `clean` leaves them as 1.11.0 does; no manifest field moves (the rows use the `coOwned` field file 0's `u0-settings-ownership` added). **Coverage floor:** none of these files. |

An engine-created file the user emptied → `engine-only` → deleted. `.gitignore` deleted by the user → `check` lists it missing (the rows name it); the next `sync` recreates the required lines with `createdFile: true`. - **interfaces, added:** `.gitignore` is not a lane of `coOwnedDocumentLanes`. Its rows carry `coOwned.lines` and its reducer joins `coOwnedReclaimReducers` as declared here. File 0 validates `lines` for shape only, so the bound `lines ⊆ REQUIRED_GITIGNORE_ENTRIES` (r1 P5.1, r2 item 4) is this unit's to enforce.

Amended 2026-10-07 (build): file 0 left `OWNED_PATHS` at `version` 2 (the renamed Cursor guards joined it), so this unit raises it from 2 to 3; it read "`version` 2".
- **interfaces, added:** file 0's bound refuses a `.gitignore` ledger row until this unit adds the path, and it adds it together with its co-owned reducer only, so a `.gitignore` row never reaches the sweep's whole-file branch. Validation bounds the record: every `coOwned.lines` entry of a `.gitignore` row is a member of `REQUIRED_GITIGNORE_ENTRIES` (`src/mcp/env.ts:193-198`), else the manifest is refused with the one error the SECURITY bullet below states (`` `ledger[${i}].coOwned.lines[${j}]` … ``).
- **testCriteria, added:** **Given** a `.gitignore` row whose `coOwned.lines` names `.env`, **when** `sync -y` or `clean -y`, **then** exit 1 `CONFIG_ERROR` naming the line, and `.gitignore` is byte-identical (`test/manifest/manifest.test.ts`).
- **edgeCases, added:** a hand-edited record naming an owner's ignore line → refused when the manifest is read; nothing is removed.
- **files, added:** `src/manifest/manifest.ts` (`collectLedgerEntryErrors` `:317-352`), `test/manifest/manifest.test.ts`.
- **interfaces, added:** SECURITY (principle 5; r1 P5.1, r2 N6-8): a ledger row's `coOwned.lines` is accepted only when the
  row's `path` is `.gitignore` and every line is an element of `REQUIRED_GITIGNORE_ENTRIES` (`src/mcp/env.ts:193-198`;
  `manifest.ts` is wave 4 and may import `env.ts`, wave 3). Any other line is one error,
  `` `ledger[${i}].coOwned.lines[${j}]` ${JSON.stringify(line)} is not a line this engine writes to .gitignore (only ${REQUIRED_GITIGNORE_ENTRIES.join(", ")}) ``,
  so `readManifest` raises `CONFIG_ERROR` and `sync`, `clean` and `config` stop before any write. Defence in depth:
  `reduceGitignoreToForeignContent` and `reconcileGitignore` remove only lines in `record.lines ∩ REQUIRED_GITIGNORE_ENTRIES`.
  If file 0's `coOwned` validation (`u0-settings-ownership`, which absorbs `u1-settings-key-ownership`) already bounds
  `lines`, this unit keeps the reducer bound and the criteria below as regressions.
- **testCriteria, added:** **Given** an initialised repository whose `.gitignore` holds the owner's `.env` line and a
  committed manifest whose `.gitignore` row lists `.env` in `coOwned.lines`, **when** `clean -y` and `sync -y` run,
  **then** each exits 1 with `CONFIG_ERROR` naming `ledger[i].coOwned.lines[0]` and `.gitignore` is byte-identical
  (`test/manifest/manifest.test.ts`, `test/cli/commands/clean.test.ts`). **Given** `reduceGitignoreToForeignContent`
  called with a record listing `.env`, **then** the `.env` line stays (`test/mcp/env.test.ts`).
- **edgeCases, added:** `coOwned.lines` on a row at any path but `.gitignore` → refused. An engine line the owner also
  wrote (`.env.mcp` twice) → only the last occurrence goes, as the reducer already rules.

- **interfaces, added:** `backup.path` is bounded beside file 0's import-target rule in `collectImportChoiceErrors`: it equals `<path>.bak` or `<path>.bak.<8 hex>` in the decision path's own folder, else ``` `importChoice[k].backup.path` "<value>" is not a backup of "<path>" ``` and the manifest is refused, so a forged record cannot rename an arbitrary file onto an instruction file at `clean`.

  `:602-606`; the prepend guards `:716-717`, `:1255-1258`; `SafeWriteFileOptions` `:89`; `backupBeforeOverwrite`
  `:1048-1081`), `src/merge/atomicWrite.ts` (`resolveNonClobberingBakPath` `:1471-1478`), `src/merge/reclaim.ts`
  (`ReclaimOptions` `:165`; the backup `:992`), `src/manifest/claudeSettings.ts` (`:585`), `src/cli/engine/emissionWrite.ts`
  (`outputWriteOptions` `:148-167`), `src/cli/commands/sync/report.ts` (`syncJsonPayload` `:314`),
  `test/merge/managedBlocks.test.ts`, `test/merge/bakEscape.test.ts`.
- **interfaces, added:**
  1. SECURITY (r1 P5.2): `ImportDecision.backup.path` must equal `${path}.bak`, or `${path}.bak.` followed by exactly eight
     lowercase hex digits — the two names `resolveNonClobberingBakPath` mints (`src/merge/atomicWrite.ts:1471-1478`) — in
     the decision's own folder; anything else is one error,
     `` `importChoice[${i}].backup.path` must be ${path}.bak or ${path}.bak.<8 hex digits>, beside the file it backs up ``.
     `clean`'s restore re-checks the bound before any `rename`.
  2. Byte-exact original (inbox ``"`prependManagedBlock`'s `trimStart` and final-newline normalisation keep a supplemented file's round trip inexact" at 077e8a78`` re-opened): `prependManagedBlock` keeps the owner's bytes verbatim —
     `` existing === "" ? `${incoming.trim()}\n` : `${incoming.trim()}\n\n${existing}` `` — so leading whitespace, a BOM and a
     missing final newline survive, and the strip join of this unit (one END terminator, one separator line) returns them
     exactly (r1 C5.d′). The join lives in `src/merge/managedBlocks.ts` as
     `export function stripManagedBlock(content: string, filePath?: string): string | null` (`null` with no block), used by
     the sweep's strip lane (`reclaim.ts:799`), `u1-import-mode-per-file` and `check` (`u1-drift-cause`). The prepend's
     idempotency guards (`safeWrite.ts:716-717`, `:1255-1258`) can no longer fire, since a prepend always adds bytes, and go.
  3. C5 backups (REQ-FLOW-046): `SafeWriteFileOptions.backupDir?: string`, `ReclaimOptions.backupDir?: string` and the same
     option on the settings lane — when set, every backup the write takes lands at
     `<backupDir>/<repository-relative path>.bak` (`.bak.<8 hex>` when taken), never beside the file. Before the first one
     the folder gets a `.gitignore` holding `*\n` (written `wx`; an existing one is left alone), so git ignores the folder
     and everything in it and the root `.gitignore` gains no line (measured 2026-10-06 in the planning lab (`gi/`): the
     nested rule ignores itself and the backups, `git status --porcelain --ignored` shows the folder as `!!`). The path is
     containment-checked against `boundaryDir` and `backupDir`, so a symlinked `.stamity/backups` refuses the overwrite (a
     backup or nothing). `sync` passes `backupDir: join(rootDir, ".stamity", "backups")` to every write lane and to its
     sweep; `init` passes it for every output except a `replace`-decided path, whose original stays beside the file,
     bounded and recorded, so `clean` restores it in a fresh clone; `clean` passes none. Names end in `.bak`, so no client
     loads a mirrored skill, agent or rule from the folder.
- **testCriteria, added:** **Given** a decision whose `backup.path` is `../secret.txt`, `docs/AGENTS.md.bak`,

  **given** a forged record naming `notes/a.md` with its true hash and a deleted `AGENTS.md`, **when** `clean -y`, **then**
  `notes/a.md` is not moved (`test/cli/commands/clean.test.ts`). **Given** an owner `AGENTS.md` of `\n\n  # Team\n\nno final newline`
  and an owner `CLAUDE.md` opening with a BOM, **when** `init -y --tools claude --import-config supplement` then
  `clean -y`, **then** both are byte-identical (r1 sup, sup2) (`test/cli/commands/roundTrip.test.ts`); re-pin
  `test/merge/safeWrite.test.ts:413-436` (the prepend keeps bytes verbatim) and delete `:475-491` and `:874-890` (the

  **Given** a hand edit to the generated `.claude/agents/stamity-creator.md`, **when** `sync -y`, **then**

  `git status --porcelain` lists no backup, and `sync --json` `wrote[]` carries
  `backupPath: ".stamity/backups/.claude/agents/stamity-creator.md.bak"`; a second edit and sync add a `.bak.<8 hex>`
  beside it (r1 bak) (`test/cli/commands/syncEngine.test.ts`). **Given** a symlinked `.stamity/backups`, **then** the
  overwrite is refused and the file is untouched (`test/merge/bakEscape.test.ts`). **Given** `init -y --force` over an
  engine file the user edited, **then** its backup lands in `.stamity/backups/`; **given** `--import-config replace`,
  **then** `AGENTS.md.bak` stays beside `AGENTS.md`.
- **edgeCases, added:** An owner file that is only whitespace → kept verbatim below the block. A supplemented file written
  by a ≤1.12.0 normalising prepend → the strip returns it normalised as then (named residue for older setups). A
  `.stamity/backups/.gitignore` the user changed → left alone (the backups may then be tracked; their choice).

**Amended 2026-10-06 (the updated brief; read at `d10db029`; sentences of the table above that this amendment replaces are already replaced in place):**
- **interfaces, added:** file 0's `retainReferencedHookScripts` runs before this unit's sweep in both orders (REQ-FLOW-037). The default order's prune of `.stamity/` leaves every retained path and its directories, and replaces file 0's whole-directory keep. `--purge` removes `.stamity/` except the retained paths and reports each.
- **testCriteria, added:** **Given** a kept, unparseable `.cursor/hooks.json` that runs `.stamity/generated/hooks/cursor/stamity-portable-hook.mjs`, **when** `clean -y --purge`, **then** that script and its directories remain, everything else under `.stamity/` is gone, and the report names the script (`test/cli/commands/clean.test.ts`).
- **edgeCases, added:** a retained script under `.stamity/generated/` after a default `clean` → stays with its directories; the next `clean` retries it once the hooks file no longer runs it.
- **interfaces, added:** `.stamity/backups/` (REQ-FLOW-046) is user state: the default `clean -y` keeps it and lists
  `.stamity/backups/` in `kept` when it holds anything besides its own `.gitignore`; a folder holding only that
  `.gitignore` (bytes `*\n`) is engine scaffolding, which `removeStateScaffold` unlinks and the prune removes; `--purge`
  deletes it with the rest of `.stamity/`. The confirmation names backups among what stays: ``Learnings, handoffs,
  overrides, run records, backups and installed packs under ${STATE_DIR}/ stay; --purge deletes them too.`` The generated
  `st-setup` body keeps its list (it moves eval ranges; follow-up row).
- **testCriteria, added:** **Given** this unit's first state plus a `sync` that backed up a hand-edited generated file,
  **when** `clean -y`, **then** `.stamity/backups/**` is byte-identical and `kept` includes `.stamity/backups/`; **when**
  `clean -y --purge`, **then** it is gone; **given** a `.stamity/backups/` holding only its `.gitignore`, **when**
  `clean -y`, **then** the folder is removed (`test/cli/commands/clean.test.ts`).
- **edgeCases, added:** A `.stamity/backups/.gitignore` the user edited → kept with the folder.

### u1-foreign-paths — the CLI never writes inside a path another tool installed, and its remedy never offers `--force`

The sweep knows no other tool's paths. The lock shape as transcribed from apm-cli 0.29.0 and 0.30.0 runs (`test/ci/apmInstall.test.ts:143-178`): top-level `lockfile_version`, `apm_version`, `dependencies:` rows (`repo_url`, `name`, `package_type`, `resolved_ref`, `resolved_commit`, a `deployed_files:` list), `deployments`. `yaml` is a runtime dependency (`src/config/parse.ts:1`). **New module `src/detect/apmLock.ts`** (PLAN_MAP `"src/detect/apmLock.ts": { unit: "p20-u1-foreign-paths", wave: 2 }`; imports `node:fs/promises`, `node:path`, `yaml`, `../config/parse.ts::isPlainObject`) — the contract file 02 reuses: `export const APM_LOCK_FILE = "apm.lock.yaml";` · `export interface ApmLockDependency { readonly repoUrl: string \| null; readonly name: string \| null; readonly packageType: string \| null; readonly resolvedRef: string \| null; readonly resolvedCommit: string \| null; readonly deployedFiles: readonly string[] }` · `export interface ApmLock { readonly lockfileVersion: string \| null; readonly apmVersion: string \| null; readonly dependencies: readonly ApmLockDependency[] }` · `export type ApmLockRead = { readonly kind: "absent" } \| { readonly kind: "unreadable"; readonly detail: string } \| { readonly kind: "read"; readonly lock: ApmLock; readonly refusedPaths: readonly string[] }` · `export function parseApmLock(text: string): ApmLockRead` (pure, never throws: a YAML failure, a non-map root or a non-list `dependencies` → `unreadable` with the parser's first line and no file bytes; a scalar of another type reads `null`; an absent `deployed_files` reads `[]`; each entry trimmed, a leading `./` stripped, a trailing `/` kept as a folder claim; absolute, drive-letter, backslash, NUL or `..` entries go to `refusedPaths`) · `export async function readApmLock(rootDir: string): Promise<ApmLockRead>` (ENOENT → `absent`, any other read error → `unreadable`) · `export function apmDeployedPaths(read: ApmLockRead): ReadonlySet<string>` (the union of every dependency's `deployedFiles`; empty unless `read`). **New `emissionWrite.ts` export:** `foreignOutputPaths(rootDir: string, outputs: readonly AdapterOutput[], opts: { lockPaths: ReadonlySet<string>; ownedPaths: ReadonlySet<string> }): Promise<Map<string, string>>` → planned path → detail. A planned path is foreign when (a) `lockPaths` lists it or a folder claim covers it; or (b) it lies in a skill folder `<dir>/skills/<name>/` (the container shape `reclaim.ts:327-338` recognises) that a lock path lies in or claims; or (c) that skill folder exists on disk, no `ownedPaths` entry lies inside it (`ownedPaths` = the current ledger's paths, plus under `init --force` the previous manifest's), and some regular file inside it differs from the output planned at that path (an unplanned file counts as different) — the REQ-FLOW-030 "unmanaged pre-existing folder"; a folder holding exactly the engine's bytes (a lost manifest) is adopted, not foreign. Details: ``${path} is listed in apm.lock.yaml as deployed by another tool`` · ``${path} sits in ${folder}/, which another tool owns (apm.lock.yaml lists a file there, or the folder holds files this engine did not write)``. **Sync:** `CollisionKind` gains `"foreign-path"`; `planSync` reads the lock once (`unreadable` → one plan warning ``apm.lock.yaml could not be read (<detail>), so the paths another tool deployed are not protected — fix the file``), computes the foreign map before `planOutputEntries`, and emits `{ action: "collision", collisionKind: "foreign-path", detail }` for each; `COLLISION_KIND_ORDER` = `["unmanaged-name", "deny-scan", "shared-name", "linked-source", "foreign-path"]`; `COLLISION_REMEDY["foreign-path"]` = ``(paths) => `${paths.join(", ")} belong to another tool, so nothing is written there and --force does not change that. Remove the duplicate from that tool (for an APM package, drop it from apm.yml and run apm install), or move a folder of your own aside, then re-run.` ``; the apply never attempts a foreign path, forced or not (under `--force`, `refused` = source refusals ∪ foreign paths). A `TOOLS`-owned ledger row at a foreign path the lock does NOT list (the engine's own earlier write inside a foreign folder, e.g. 1.11.0's `.agents/skills/st-qa/agents/openai.yaml`) is added to `reclaim` as `deselected`, so the sweep removes it when its hash still matches. `SyncPlan` gains `foreignPaths?: ReadonlySet<string>` (the lock paths) for the sweep — optional in the type, as `warnings` is (`sync/engine.ts:184-190`), because fixtures assemble plans by hand; `planSync` always sets it. **Sweep:** `ReclaimOptions.foreignPaths?: ReadonlySet<string>`; in `planFor`, right after the shape check, a member returns `skip("skipped-user-content", "apm.lock.yaml lists this path as deployed by another tool, so this engine neither deletes nor rewrites it.")`; the sync sweep and both `clean` sweeps pass `apmDeployedPaths(await readApmLock(rootDir))`. **Init:** reads the lock, computes the map before the write loop, and returns `{ action: "skipped", warning: "Skipped <path>. <detail>" }` for each foreign path under every flag. **Check:** `collidingPaths` leaves `foreign-path` entries out; `foreignPathStep(entries)` = ``${n} path(s) belong to another tool — <first three> — so sync writes nothing there and --force does not change that. Remove the duplicate from that tool (for an APM package, drop it from apm.yml and run apm install), or move a folder of your own aside, then run ${packageCommand("sync")}.``, joining the human next steps and the JSON `next`. **Docs:** `docs/troubleshooting.md` gains `### \`sync\` refuses a path another tool installed` after `:172`, outside the doctor-row section `test/docsPages.test.ts:2755-2785` holds set-equal with `check.ts`'s probe ids. File 02 reuses `readApmLock`, `apmDeployedPaths`, `ApmLockDependency.repoUrl` (mirror matching, `u2-apm-backed-mode`) and `ApmLock.apmVersion` (`u2-apm-floor-current`), and adds the APM-backed mode to the remedy. **Follows:** `src/cli/commands/sync/engine.ts:771-773` (a refusal that is never attempted, forced or not) and `src/cli/commands/plugin/probe.ts:634-643` (tolerant `parseYaml`). **Backward compatibility:** no manifest field; a repository with no lock and no foreign folder plans byte for byte as before. **Coverage floor:** `src/merge/reclaim.ts` 100%. |

over the 0.29.0 lock, **then** `deployedFiles` is `[]`; over `{invalid`, a list root, or `dependencies: 3`, **then** `unreadable`; with `/etc/passwd`, `../x` and `a\\b` entries, **then** those three land in `refusedPaths`; **when** `init -y --force`, **then** the same and no `.bak` exists (`test/cli/commands/foreignPaths.test.ts`). **When** `sync` and `sync --force` run, **then** each exits 1, the entries carry `collisionKind: "foreign-path"`, the files are untouched, and stderr contains `apm.lock.yaml` and not `--force overwrites`. **When** `check --json` runs, **then** the exit code is `INTEGRITY_ERROR`'s and `error.next` contains no `sync --force`. **Given** a lock claiming `.agents/skills/st-qa/`, **when** `init -y --tools cursor`, **then** nothing appears under that folder. **Given** no lock and a user's own `.agents/skills/st-qa/SKILL.md`, **then** init writes nothing in that folder and `sync` reports both of its paths as `foreign-path`. **Given** a hashed ledger row for `.agents/skills/st-qa/agents/openai.yaml` beside a lock-listed `SKILL.md`, **when** `sync`, **then** the engine's file is deleted, its row dropped, and `SKILL.md` untouched. **Given** a user's `notes.md` in an engine-written, ledgered `.agents/skills/st-ask/`, **then** the folder is not foreign and its `SKILL.md` updates normally. **Given** `foreignPaths` holding a candidate's path, **when** swept with consent, **then** the action is `skipped-user-content` with the apm.lock.yaml detail even where the name and hash would delete (`test/merge/reclaim.test.ts`). **Given** a ledgered file the lock now lists, hash equal, **when** `clean -y`, **then** the file is kept. **Given** an unreadable lock, **then** `sync`'s report carries the warning and the run equals the no-lock case. **Given** no lock and no foreign folder, **then** every existing `syncEngine`, `initApply` and `check` case passes unedited. **Threshold:** `npm test -- --coverage` keeps `src/merge/reclaim.ts` at 100%. |
| `edgeCases` | A lost manifest (engine skill folders holding exactly the engine's bytes, no ledger) → adopted, not foreign. A lost or corrupt manifest across an engine upgrade that changed a skill body (including `init --force` over a corrupt manifest) → that folder reads foreign (no owner provable) and is skipped with the remove-or-move remedy, where 1.11.0 skipped it as `unmanaged-name` and `--force` overwrote it behind a `.bak` (named residue). A lock path outside every emitted path → no effect. A non-skill file the lock does not list (a user's `.github/agents/x.agent.md`) → `unmanaged-name` as today; `--force` still clears it. Windows: lock paths compare as POSIX strings; a backslash entry is refused, never normalised. |
| `depends_on` | u1-screen-references, u1-fork-neutral-tests (each edits `src/cli/commands/check.ts`), u1-secret-shaped-fixtures (both edit `test/cli/commands/check.test.ts`) |
| `verify` | `npx vitest run test/detect/apmLock.test.ts test/cli/commands/foreignPaths.test.ts test/cli/commands/syncEngine.test.ts test/cli/commands/initApply.test.ts test/cli/commands/check.test.ts test/merge/reclaim.test.ts test/cli/commands/clean.test.ts test/architecture/boundaries.test.ts` · `npm test -- --coverage` · `npm run lint && npm run typecheck && npm run test` · `npm run knip` |

### u1-manifest-stable — a no-op `sync` changes no byte, and the version stays only in the manifest and the pins

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| `id` | u1-manifest-stable |
| `requirements` | REQ-FLOW-032, REQ-UPSTREAM-016 |
| `files` | `src/manifest/manifest.ts` (`writeManifest` `:1003-1036`), `src/cli/commands/sync/engine.ts` (`:397`, `:869`, `:921-944`), `src/cli/engine/emissionWrite.ts` (`outputWriteOptions` `:111-167`; `ledgerRowsForOutput` doc `:196-199`), `src/cli/commands/init/apply.ts` (`writeOutput` `:538-560`), `src/emit/planner.ts` (`:947`, `:1074-1115`), `src/adapters/claude.ts` (`buildClaudeMd` `:711-717`, its caller `:533`), `src/types/manifest.ts` (`stampedVersion` doc `:95-96`), `test/emit/goldenFixture.ts` (`makeGoldenRepo` `:203-237` gains an optional `engineVersion`), `docs/workspaces.md` (`:328`), `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.stamity/manifest.json` (dogfood sync), `test/manifest/manifest.test.ts`, `test/cli/commands/syncEngine.test.ts`, `test/cli/commands/sync.test.ts`, `test/cli/flows.e2e.test.ts`, `test/cli/dogfoodDist.e2e.test.ts`, `test/upstream/workflowRecovery.test.ts`, `test/emit/planner.test.ts`, `test/adapters/claude.test.ts`, `test/emit/versionDelta.test.ts` (new), `test/emit/__snapshots__/crossClientGoldens.test.ts.snap`, `CHANGELOG.md` (`### Changed`) |
| `interfaces` | **Current:** `writeManifest` stamps `updatedAt` from `opts.now` and always writes (`src/manifest/manifest.ts:1018-1036`); `applySync` also sets `updatedAt: now` before calling it (`sync/engine.ts:923-928`), so every no-op sync rewrites the manifest (fx-a vs fx-a2 differ only in `createdAt`/`updatedAt`). Emission stamps the engine version into each managed block's BEGIN line — `wrapInManagedBlock(…, engineVersion)` for the `CLAUDE.md` bridge (`src/adapters/claude.ts:715`) and for supplement rows (`src/emit/planner.ts:1113`) — and the writer's `version` option (`emissionWrite.ts:157`) re-stamps on merge (`src/merge/safeWrite.ts:1301`) and reads a stamp change as staleness (`isMergeUnchanged`, `:630-643`). Under the pinned-call doctrine (`src/shared/cliCall.ts:4-23`, REQ-FLOW-002) the version inside `npx -y <pkg>@<v> <verb>` and inside `cliCallHint` remedies (`:139-146`) is a pin. In a three-client 1.11.0 tree the version sits in 21 files (fx-a, `grep -rIn 1.11.0`): a pin in 20 (st-work ×3, st-debug ×3, st-quick ×3, st-learn ×2, st-handoff ×2, five hook scripts, `.claude/settings.json`, `AGENTS.md`'s config hint) and a non-pin in one (`CLAUDE.md`'s `STAMITY:BEGIN v1.11.0`), plus every supplemented file. **New `writeManifest`:** `writeManifest(rootDir: string, manifest: SetupManifest, opts: { now?: Date } = {}): Promise<{ written: boolean; updatedAt: string }>` — reads the current file (absent → write); when it parses and its `updatedAt` is a timestamp, serialises `{ ...manifest, updatedAt: <that value> }`, and when that equals the current bytes (or equals them with every `\r\n` folded to `\n`) returns `{ written: false, updatedAt: <that value> }` without writing; otherwise stamps `now`, validates, writes, and returns `{ written: true, updatedAt }`. Existing callers ignore the result. `applySync` stops setting `updatedAt` and reports the returned value on `SyncApplyReport.manifest` ("exactly as persisted"). **Markers:** `buildClaudeMd()` and `applyImportDecision` call `wrapInManagedBlock(content, path)` with no version (the `engineVersion` parameter leaves `applyImportDecisions`/`applyImportDecision` and `buildClaudeMd`); `outputWriteOptions(managedBody, force, rootDir, ledgerPaths?, ledgerHashes?)` loses `engineVersion` and sets no `version` (callers `init/apply.ts:549-556`, `sync/engine.ts:397`, `:869`), so `isMergeUnchanged` decides by bytes and a stamped block from an earlier release is rewritten once to the bare marker. `ledgerRowsForOutput` keeps `stampedVersion: engineVersion` on managed rows (manifest only); its doc reads "the engine version that last wrote this managed block; the block itself carries no version". `src/merge/**` does not change — its `version` option stays for its own callers and tests. **Pins kept:** every `${STAMITY:CLI}` rendering and every `cliCallHint` remedy (the spec draft's default (a) for the REQ-FLOW-032 marker). **Test helper:** `makeGoldenRepo(opts: { tools; seed?; engineVersion?: string })` defaults to `GOLDEN_ENGINE_VERSION`. **Docs:** `docs/workspaces.md:328` — "plus the `updatedAt` stamp, which moves only when the manifest's content changes". **Follows:** `src/merge/safeWrite.ts:622-643` (only-when-stale: equal bytes are not rewritten). **Backward compatibility:** no field changes; an older manifest reads unchanged; the first sync after upgrading rewrites each stamped block once and moves `generatedBy`/`updatedAt` once. Skew residue: a 1.11.0 `check` over bare markers reports them stale (named risk). **Coverage floor:** `src/emit/planner.ts` 100/90/100/100 (`vitest.config.ts:149`) — a removed parameter adds no branch. |
| `testCriteria` | **Given** a manifest written at T0, **when** `writeManifest` runs at T1 with the same content, **then** the bytes and the mtime are unchanged and it returns `{ written: false, updatedAt: T0 }`; **when** one field differs, **then** `updatedAt === T1` and `written: true` (`test/manifest/manifest.test.ts`; re-pin `:553-561` with `// TEST CHANGE, justified: REQ-FLOW-032 — updatedAt moves only on change`); **given** the same content on disk in CRLF, **then** no write. **Given** a synced repository, **when** `sync` runs twice with no input change, **then** the second run leaves every file byte-identical, `.stamity/manifest.json` included (`test/cli/dogfoodDist.e2e.test.ts:213-238` tightened from `[MANIFEST_REL]` to `[]` and from "greater than" to "equal", with a TEST CHANGE comment). **Given** the golden repo, **then** `CLAUDE.md` is `<!-- STAMITY:BEGIN -->\n, then the line `@AGENTS.md`, then\n<!-- STAMITY:END -->\n` and a supplement-wrapped `AGENTS.md` opens on the bare marker (`test/adapters/claude.test.ts`, `test/emit/planner.test.ts`, snapshot updated and reviewed). **Given** `CLAUDE.md` carrying `<!-- STAMITY:BEGIN v1.11.0 -->` with today's body, **when** `sync`, **then** it is `updated` once to the bare marker and the next `sync` reports it `unchanged`. **Given** the golden tree rendered at `1.0.0-golden` and at `1.0.1-golden`, **then** the paths that differ are exactly `.stamity/manifest.json` plus the paths whose first render contains `@1.0.0-golden` (`test/emit/versionDelta.test.ts`, new). **Re-pins**, each with a TEST CHANGE comment citing REQ-FLOW-032: `test/cli/flows.e2e.test.ts:110` (after init then a no-op sync, `updatedAt` is equal), `test/upstream/workflowRecovery.test.ts:296-318` (a fresh sync of an unchanged target reproduces the manifest byte for byte, and recovery accepts it), and whichever of `test/cli/commands/syncEngine.test.ts:269`, `:588`, `:908`, `:933` and `test/cli/commands/sync.test.ts:163-164`, `:243` assert a moved `updatedAt` over a run that changed nothing — run them first and re-pin only the red ones. |
| `edgeCases` | A manifest with no `updatedAt`, or an unparseable one → written with a fresh stamp. A sync that only refreshes `detected` → content changed → stamped. `init --force` → a fresh `createdAt` (the `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` instant when set) → written. A repository synced by 1.12.0 and checked by 1.11.0 → 1.11.0 reports the bare markers as drift (named skew). A Windows autocrlf checkout of the manifest → the fold keeps it unwritten. |
| `depends_on` | u1-ignored-output (lane order, after u1-copilot-setup-steps), u1-git-isolation-headroom (both edit `test/upstream/workflowRecovery.test.ts`) |
| `verify` | `npx vitest run test/manifest/manifest.test.ts test/cli/commands/syncEngine.test.ts test/cli/commands/sync.test.ts test/emit/planner.test.ts test/adapters/claude.test.ts test/emit/versionDelta.test.ts test/emit/crossClientGoldens.test.ts test/upstream/workflowRecovery.test.ts test/cli/flows.e2e.test.ts test/cli/dogfoodDist.e2e.test.ts` · `npm test -- --coverage` · `npm run lint && npm run typecheck && npm run test` · `npm run build && node dist/cli.js sync && node dist/cli.js sync && git status --porcelain` (the second sync adds nothing to the status) |

| Field | Content |

| `files` | `src/cli/commands/init/plan.ts` (`collectExistingConfigPaths` `:398-410`; `buildInitDecisions` deps `:206-246`), `src/cli/commands/init.ts` (`:950-1011`; the `applyInit` call `:1070-1079`), `src/cli/commands/init/apply.ts` (`InitApplyOptions` `:85-129`; ownership indexes `:284-285`), `test/cli/commands/initPlan.test.ts`, `test/cli/commands/init.test.ts`, `test/cli/commands/initApply.test.ts`, `CHANGELOG.md` (`### Fixed`) |
| `interfaces` | **Current:** with `--force`, `alreadyInitialised` is false (`src/cli/commands/init.ts:950`), so `existingConfigPaths` — every present `AGENTS.md`/`AGENT.md` and each detected tool's file (`CROSS_TOOL_CONFIG_FILES` `init/plan.ts:162`, `TOOL_INSTRUCTION_FILES` `:171-176`, `collectExistingConfigPaths` `:398-410`) — includes the engine's own outputs, and `-y` maps the default `supplement` over all of them (`init.ts:989-1011`); the planner wraps the block-less charter row (`src/emit/planner.ts:1113`) and the writer prepends it above the engine's own unmarked `AGENTS.md` (`src/merge/safeWrite.ts:1252-1279`) — fx-a2: 10,711 bytes, `# Charter` at lines 2 and 87; re-run here, `check` exits 0 with only the `preserved-duplicate` warning (`AGENTS.md:87`). A `--force` re-init indexes ownership from pack rows only (`init/apply.ts:265-285`), so even without the duplication a core output takes a force-overwrite `.bak`. The rendered charter opens `# Charter` and carries an `Invariants version …` line (`src/content/charter.ts:165`). **New:** `isEngineCharterDocument` is imported from `src/manifest/ownedPaths.ts` (file 0): true when, after an optional BOM, the first non-blank line is exactly `# Charter`, the lines `## Repo facts`, `## Invariants`, `## Touchpoints` and `## Conditional layer` occur in that order, and no STAMITY marker is present. `buildInitDecisions(rootDir, overrides, deps)` gains `deps.previous?: SetupManifest \| null`; `collectExistingConfigPaths(rootDir, detectedTools, previous)` drops (a) every path a `TOOLS`-owned row of `previous.ledger` records and (b) every path whose text `isEngineCharterDocument` accepts. `init.ts` reads `previous` when `force` (`CONFIG_ERROR` → `null`, so only (b) applies), carries every `previous.importChoice` record whose path still exists — the persisted decision binds (`src/types/manifest.ts:389-405`), a `backup` record included — and asks or defaults the import mode only for the remaining targets. `InitApplyOptions` gains `previous?: SetupManifest \| null`; under `force`, `previous`'s `TOOLS`-owned rows at the instruction paths (`AGENTS.md`, `AGENT.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md`) join `ownedPaths`/`ownedHashes` (not the new ledger), so an unedited engine charter is overwritten with no `.bak` (`hasLedgerDrift` false, `safeWrite.ts:1192-1196`) and a hand-edited one takes the drifted-overwrite `.bak`. The previous-manifest read file 0's `u0-settings-ownership` hoisted (`readPreviousManifest`, `src/cli/commands/init/apply.ts`) is reused (one read per run). **Follows:** `src/cli/commands/init/apply.ts:583-625` (carry from a readable previous manifest; a corrupt one yields nothing and `--force` still repairs). **Backward compatibility:** no manifest field; a manifest without `importChoice` carries nothing. **Coverage floor:** none. |
| `testCriteria` | **Given** `init -y --tools claude,cursor,copilot` committed, **when** `init -y --force` with the same tools, **then** `AGENTS.md` is byte-identical to the first run's (one `# Charter`), no `AGENTS.md.bak*` exists, the manifest carries no `importChoice`, and `check` reports `preserved-duplicate` pass (`test/cli/commands/init.test.ts`, the fx-a2 case). **Given** the engine's charter with its managed-block markers removed (a supplemented `AGENTS.md` whose markers were deleted), **when** `init -y --force`, **then** `AGENTS.md` holds exactly one `# Charter` heading. **Given** a corrupt manifest and the engine's unmarked charter, **when** `init -y --force`, **then** exactly one `# Charter` heading (a `.bak` is allowed: ownership is unprovable). **Given** a repository whose `.stamity/` was deleted while the engine's `AGENTS.md` remained, **when** `init -y`, **then** `decisions.existingConfigPaths` excludes `AGENTS.md` and the file holds one `# Charter`. **Given** an `AGENTS.md` of user prose with no charter, **when** `init -y --force --import-config supplement`, **then** the prose survives byte for byte below the block. **Given** a first `init -y --import-config skip` over a user `AGENTS.md`, **when** `init -y --force`, **then** `importChoice` still reads `skip` and `AGENTS.md` is byte-identical. **Given** a first `init -y --import-config replace`, **when** `init -y --force`, **then** `importChoice[AGENTS.md]` keeps its `backup` record and no second `.bak` appears. **Given** `isEngineCharterDocument`, **then** it is true for the emitted `AGENTS.md` with and without a BOM, false for `# Charter\n\nmy notes\n` (no section headings), and false when STAMITY markers are present (`test/cli/commands/initPlan.test.ts`). |
| `edgeCases` | A user's own file titled `# Charter` that also carries an `Invariants version` line → read as the engine's; under `--force` it is overwritten behind a `.bak` (recoverable; named). A previous decision for a path that no longer exists → dropped. An interactive `--force` run → the prompt asks only about paths with no carried decision. A previous manifest from a newer schema generation → `readManifest`'s `CONFIG_ERROR` → treated as unreadable (content recognition only). |
| `depends_on` | u1-manifest-stable, docs/plans/016-fork-distribution-00.md (u0-ledger-bound: `isEngineCharterDocument`), u1-import-mode-per-file |
| `verify` | `npx vitest run test/cli/commands/initPlan.test.ts test/cli/commands/init.test.ts test/cli/commands/initApply.test.ts` · `npm run lint && npm run typecheck && npm run test` |

- **files, added:** `src/manifest/ownedPaths.ts` (read only: `isEngineCharterDocument`, from file 0's `u0-ledger-bound`).
- **interfaces, added:** the recogniser this unit needs exists before it runs, in `src/manifest/ownedPaths.ts`; this unit imports it and defines none of its own. Measured 2026-10-06 with the published releases: the charters 1.0.0 to 1.7.0 wrote carry no `Invariants version` line, while every release's charter opens `# Charter` and carries `## Repo facts`, `## Invariants`, `## Touchpoints` and `## Conditional layer` in that order.
- **testCriteria, added:** **Given** a charter written by 1.0.0 (`test/manifest/fixtures/charter-1.0.0.md`), **when** `init -y --force`, **then** `AGENTS.md` holds exactly one `# Charter` heading (`test/cli/commands/init.test.ts`).
- **interfaces, added:** Under `--force` the carried decisions bind (this unit), and a per-path `--import-config
  <path>=<mode>` (`u1-import-mode-per-file`) that differs from a carried decision is refused before any write:
  `VALIDATION_ERROR` ``init --force keeps the recorded import decision for ${path} (${carried}); change it with config set
  importChoice.${path} ${mode}, then run sync`` — a recorded decision changes only through `config set` plus `sync`
  (r2 N1-10). A bare `--import-config <mode>` and per-path flags apply to detected paths with no carried decision.
- **testCriteria, added:** **Given** a first `init -y --import-config skip` over a user `AGENTS.md`, **when**
  `init -y --force --import-config AGENTS.md=supplement`, **then** exit 1 naming `config set importChoice.AGENTS.md
  supplement` and an unchanged tree; **when** `init -y --force --import-config AGENTS.md=skip`, **then** it proceeds
  (`test/cli/commands/init.test.ts`).
- **edgeCases, added:** A carried decision for a file that is gone → dropped (this unit's rule); a per-path flag then
  decides that path afresh.

### u1-node-missing-hint — a guard that cannot start Node says so, names the floor, and still blocks

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| `id` | u1-node-missing-hint |
| `requirements` | REQ-FLOW-035 |
| `files` | `src/shared/cliCall.ts` (`NODE_FLOOR`, added by `u1-copilot-setup-steps`; read only), `src/adapters/claude.ts` (`guardFailClosedTail` and its doc `:300-356`), `test/shared/cliCall.test.ts`, `test/adapters/claude.test.ts` (`:105-160`), `test/emit/__snapshots__/crossClientGoldens.test.ts.snap`, `docs/troubleshooting.md` (`:213-271` only), `.claude/settings.json` (dogfood sync), `CHANGELOG.md` (`### Fixed`) |
| `interfaces` | **Current:** the core PreToolUse command ends `\|\| { s=$?; [ "$s" -eq 2 ] && exit 2; echo 'stamity: the pre-tool-use guard could not run; run \`stamity sync\` where the CLI is installed, else \`npx -y @zomarit/stamity@<v> sync\`' >&2; exit 2; }` (`src/adapters/claude.ts:345-356`, the remedy from `cliCallHint`, `src/shared/cliCall.ts:139-146`); with no `node` on `PATH` the shell returns 127, so the tail blocks (exit 2) and tells the model to run `sync`, which needs Node too. Measured here (`d1b-tail/`): a missing command enters the tail with 127, a non-executable file with 126. The session-start, tamper-notice and review-gate rows carry no tail by design (`:322-326`). Cursor's two guards are `failClosed: true` with no shell tail (`src/adapters/cursor.ts:811-816`); Copilot runs hooks through the portable runner (`src/adapters/copilot.ts:313-314`). The floor is `package.json`'s `engines.node: ">=22.22.2"`. **New:** the tail reads `NODE_FLOOR` from `src/shared/cliCall.ts` (`u1-copilot-setup-steps` adds it and its equality test). `guardFailClosedTail(cli)` returns the tail of item 2 above — on a launch failure a `stamity-` caller still exits 2, and the main thread and the client's own agents pass only when Node cannot start or `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` is unset; the tail stays POSIX (the PowerShell residual at `:327-342` is unchanged); the Node branch names no CLI verb. Cursor's `subagentStart` gains the matcher `^stamity-`, both Cursor guards stay `failClosed: true`, and Copilot's pre-tool-use guard is no longer wired; no other row changes. **Docs:** `docs/troubleshooting.md:213-271` quotes the new command, adds the Node branch ("`Node.js could not be started`: install Node.js 22.22.2 or newer and restart the client; `sync` cannot help until Node runs") and states what each other client shows for a hook with no fallback message — measured where that client's CLI is installed on the executing machine (started with `PATH` stripped of `node` in a fixture repository), otherwise written as unmeasured with the date. **Follows:** `src/shared/cliCall.ts:26-34` (a literal held equal to its source by a test) and `src/adapters/claude.ts:345-356` (the status is classified, never forwarded). **Backward compatibility:** emission only; the next `sync` replaces the engine's `PreToolUse` entry of `.claude/settings.json` (recorded and proved per entry by file 0's `u0-settings-ownership`, inside its bound, so no `.bak`; an owner's own `PreToolUse` entries are untouched). **Coverage floor:** none. |
| `testCriteria` | **Given** `guardFailClosedTail` for the canonical package at a fixed version, **then** it equals the literal above with `22.22.2` (re-pin `test/adapters/claude.test.ts:105-160` with `// TEST CHANGE, justified: REQ-FLOW-035 — a launch failure for want of Node names Node and the floor`). **Given** the rendered PreToolUse command with `node` replaced by a binary that does not exist, run through `/bin/sh -c` (skipped on win32), **then** a governed payload exits 2, a main-thread payload exits 0, and stderr contains `Node.js 22.22.2 or newer` and not `sync`; **given** a script exiting 2, **then** exit 2 and empty stderr; exiting 1, **then** exit 2 and stderr contains `node --version reports 22.22.2`; exiting 0, **then** exit 0. **Given** the rendered tail, **then** it names `NODE_FLOOR`'s value (the equality with `package.json` is `u1-copilot-setup-steps`'s test). **Given** the golden `.claude/settings.json`, **then** the Node message appears exactly once (the core PreToolUse row) and every session-start row is unchanged (snapshot updated with `npx vitest run test/emit/crossClientGoldens.test.ts --update`, reviewed). **Given** `test/emit/noBareCliCall.test.ts`, **then** it passes (the generic branch keeps the `cliCallHint` sentence; the Node branch names no verb). **Given** Cursor's emitted hooks, **then** `subagentStart` and `beforeMCPExecution` stay `failClosed: true` and `subagentStart` carries `matcher: "^stamity-"` (`test/adapters/cursor.test.ts`). |
| `edgeCases` | Node present but below the floor → the guard may start and fail on syntax (exit 1) → the generic branch, which now names the floor. `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` unset → Node starts, the script is missing (exit 1) → the unset branch: governed callers 2, everyone else 0. A Windows host without Git Bash → the POSIX tail does not parse there (the existing `claude-hook-shell` doctor row). Plugin mode → the plugin's guard carries no tail (unchanged). A fork with no npm channel → the generic branch renders `npx --no` (unchanged). |
| `depends_on` | u1-repo-root |
| `verify` | `npx vitest run test/adapters/claude.test.ts test/shared/cliCall.test.ts test/emit/crossClientGoldens.test.ts test/emit/noBareCliCall.test.ts test/adapters/cursor.test.ts` · `npm run lint && npm run typecheck && npm run test` · `npm run build && node dist/cli.js sync && node dist/cli.js check` |

     ``|| { s=$?; [ "$s" -eq 2 ] && exit 2; if [ "$s" -eq 126 ] || [ "$s" -eq 127 ]; then m='${NODE_MESSAGE}'; elif [ -z "${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-}" ]; then m='${PROJECT_DIR_MESSAGE}'; else echo '${GENERIC_MESSAGE}' >&2; exit 2; fi; grep -Eq '"(agent_type|subagent_type|agentType|subagentType)"[[:space:]]*:[[:space:]]*"stamity-'; g=$?; echo "$m" >&2; [ "$g" -eq 1 ] && exit 0; exit 2; }``
     — when Node is absent or not executable, or `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` is unset, the payload (still unread on stdin) is
     matched for the four identity fields the guard reads (`src/hooks/scripts.ts:1709`): a `stamity-` caller exits 2, any
     other 0, and a failing or missing `grep` exits 2; a missing script with the variable set, a syntax error and every
     other non-zero status still exit 2 for every caller. `NODE_MESSAGE` = ``stamity: Node.js could not be started (not
     found on PATH, or not executable), so the pre-tool-use guard cannot run; calls by stamity agents stay blocked.
     Install Node.js ${NODE_FLOOR} or newer (https://nodejs.org/en/download), make sure the client starts with it on PATH,
     then restart the client.``; `PROJECT_DIR_MESSAGE` = ``stamity: CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR is not set, so this client cannot
     locate the pre-tool-use guard; calls by stamity agents stay blocked.``; `GENERIC_MESSAGE` = this unit's generic
     sentence. Measured 2026-10-06 (the planning lab, `tail/run.bash`, after r3's prototype): Node absent → main thread
     0, `stamity-researcher` 2 (spaced and camelCase keys too), `Explore` 0, a Bash input quoting `\"agent_type\":

| `files` | `.github/workflows/upstream-update.yml` (header `:1-80`; `prepare` outputs `:206-217` and step "Extract the outcome and the report" `:302-339`; `publish` env `:443-454`, step "Validate the prepared control data" `:497-543`, and a new step between "Open or update the conflict issue" `:1114-1161` and "Retain publication and recovery evidence" `:1163`), `test/ci/upstreamWorkflow.test.ts`, `scripts/upstream.mjs` (the JSON document comment `:83-118`; `fetchUpstream` `:1013-1034`; `deriveState` `:1233-1344`; `createDocument` `:1686-1717`; `fillStateInto` `:1723-1750`; `prepare` `:1918-1937`; a new export `buildFreshness`), `test/upstream/lane.test.ts`, `test/upstream/fixtures.ts` (`LaneDocument` `:567-616`), `docs/enterprise-forks.md` (header; the marker paragraph `:1220-1224`; a new subsection after `:1299`), `test/docsPages.test.ts` (one new case; `REATTESTATION_DATE` only per the hand-page rule), `CHANGELOG.md` (`### Added`) |
| `interfaces` | **Current, issues.** `publish` opens or updates one issue per release and kind, found by `gh issue list --state open --search "\"stamity-upstream-lane: <tag> <kind>\" in:body" --json number,body` plus an exact `contains($marker)` (`upstream-update.yml:879`, `:1143`); the marker `<!-- stamity-upstream-lane: <tag> <kind> -->` (kinds `reviewed-push` `:838`, `conflict` `:1120`) is the first line of every body the lane writes (`printf '%s\n' "$MARKER"` first, `:842`, `:1124`); nothing closes an issue. `prepare`'s report step extracts `outcome`, `tag`, `branch` and `merge_commit` from the lane's JSON (`:317-338`); `publish` re-validates each before use (`:497-543`; the tag shape `^v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+(-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?$` at `:518`), holds `issues: write` (`:439`), and runs only git, `gh` and jq (REQ-UPSTREAM-013; `test/ci/upstreamWorkflow.test.ts:749-765` holds the recovery step to no `node`/`npm`/`npx`). The document's `integrated` is the highest release in the target branch's ancestry whose record agrees (`scripts/upstream.mjs:87-89`; `deriveState` `:1285-1286`). **New, the integrated tag across the boundary.** The report step adds `INTEGRATED_TAG="$(jq -r '.integrated.tag // ""' "$JSON")"` and `echo "integrated_tag=$INTEGRATED_TAG"`; `prepare` outputs `integrated_tag: ${{ steps.report.outputs.integrated_tag }}`; `publish` env `INTEGRATED_TAG: ${{ needs.prepare.outputs.integrated_tag }}`; the validation step rejects a non-empty value that fails the tag shape (`reject INTEGRATED_TAG "INTEGRATED_TAG is '…', which is not a v<major>.<minor>.<patch>[-prerelease] release tag. It decides which lane issues close."`) and names it in its closing echo (`:543`). **New step** `Close superseded lane issues` (`id: superseded`), `if: needs.prepare.outputs.integrated_tag != ''` (no status function, so a failed earlier step skips it), env `INTEGRATED_TAG`; body (bash, `set -euo pipefail`): (1) `gh issue list --state open --limit 1000 --json number,body` (the GraphQL listing, not the eventually consistent search index) into `$ARTIFACT_DIR/open-issues.json`; (2) jq keeps the lane's own issues: the body's first line, a trailing `\r` removed, matches exactly `^<!-- stamity-upstream-lane: (?<tag>v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+(?:-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?) (?<kind>conflict\|reviewed-push) -->$`; (3) jq keeps those whose tag sorts at or below `INTEGRATED_TAG` by semantic-version precedence, with a jq `semcmp`: the numeric `[major,minor,patch]` arrays first, then no prerelease above a prerelease, then the prerelease identifiers as arrays of numbers and strings — jq's own array order (numbers before strings, numbers by value, strings by code point, a shorter prefix first) is exactly semver §11; (4) each: `gh issue close <n> --reason completed --comment "Closed by the upstream lane: \`<INTEGRATED_TAG>\` is integrated on \`<INTEGRATION_BRANCH>\`, and it carries \`<tag>\`. Run: <RUN_URL>"`; a failed close is recorded and the loop goes on; (5) `$ARTIFACT_DIR/superseded-issues.json` = `{integrated, closed:[…], failed:[…], kept:[…]}` (kept: lane issues for newer tags), retained by the existing `if: always()` evidence step; outputs `closed=<numbers>`; one summary line; then, when the listing failed or any close failed, `::error title=Lane issues left open::…` naming each issue it could not close (or the failed listing) and exit 1 — after the evidence file is written, so the verdict step (`if: always()`) still runs. The step never edits, deletes, reopens or labels an issue, and closes no issue whose first line is not the marker. The workflow header gains one paragraph: superseded lane issues are closed with a comment, never deleted, and only the lane's own. **Current, status.** The document is one object for every verb (`createDocument` `:1686-1717`); `fillStateInto` (`:1723-1750`) sets `integrated` `{tag, commit, record}`, `target` `{tag, commit, date, isPrerelease}`, `candidates` `[{tag, commit, date}]` (every admitted release newer than the integrated one) and `divergence` (commit counts); `deriveState` has `state.releases` (admitted by `releases.pattern` and the prerelease setting, ordered, each with `commit` and `date`, the tag's `creatordate`, `:1239-1248`) and the records at the target head (`readRecords`, `:1250`; each `record.releaseCommit`). Nothing records when the upstream was last fetched; `--offline` reads the last fetch's refs (`prepare` `:1924-1928`). **New, status.** Export `buildFreshness({ releases, integrated, candidates, records, tagCommits }, fetchedAt) → { fetchedAt, latestRelease, integratedRelease, releasesBehind, oldestUnintegratedRelease, movedTags }`: `latestRelease` = `releases.at(-1)` as `{tag, commit, date}` or null; `integratedRelease` = `integrated` as `{tag, commit, date}` (date from `releases`, null when not admitted) or null; `releasesBehind` = `candidates.length` on the `tags` basis, or the number of candidates published with at least one asset on the `published-releases` basis; `oldestUnintegratedRelease` = `candidates[0]` as `{tag, date}` or null; `movedTags` = `state.movedTags` (`[{tag, recordedCommit, currentCommit, accepted}]`, computed in `deriveState` from the records' `releaseCommit` and `coveredCommits` against the fetched tag map, annotated tags peeled) — the "a tag moved" signal and the stop. `createDocument` gains `freshness: null` after `divergence`; `deriveState` keeps `records` and the tag map on `state`; `fillStateInto` sets `doc.freshness = buildFreshness(state, context.fetchedAt)`, so `status`, `preview` and `integrate` carry it and an error document carries null. **`fetchedAt`:** after a successful tag fetch, `fetchUpstream` writes `{"fetchedAt":"<ISO-8601 UTC>","remote":"<name>"}` to `<git common dir>/stamity-upstream-fetch.json` (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, resolved against the root; one temporary file and a rename, as `scripts/fork-identity.mjs:268-277`) and `prepare` stores the same time in `context.fetchedAt`; with `--offline` or `fetch: false`, `prepare` reads that file instead (absent → null; unreadable or another shape → null and one message, "the last fetch time could not be read from <path>; run without --offline to record it"). `DOCUMENT_VERSION` stays 1 (an added key); the header comment (`:83-118`) gains a `freshness` row; the markdown report is unchanged. `LaneDocument` gains `freshness: { fetchedAt: string \| null; latestRelease: { tag: string; commit: string; date: string \| null } \| null; integratedRelease: { tag: string; commit: string; date: string \| null } \| null; releasesBehind: number; oldestUnintegratedRelease: { tag: string; date: string \| null } \| null; movedTags: { tag: string; recordedCommit: string; currentCommit: string }[] } \| null`. **Docs.** `docs/enterprise-forks.md:1220-1224` gains: the lane closes its own issues once the integrated release reaches their tag — a newer release landed, or the fork is up to date — with a comment naming the release and the run; it closes nothing it did not open, and a renamed issue keeps its marker and is closed too. A new subsection `### Watch freshness from a heartbeat` after `:1299`: `set +e; node scripts/upstream.mjs status --json > status.json; code=$?; set -e` (exit 0 and 1 are results; 2 means the lane could not read the upstream, an alert by itself); `jq '.freshness' status.json`; `gh run list --workflow upstream-update.yml --limit 1 --json createdAt,conclusion`; and the three signals: the lane is stalled when the newest run is older than the threshold above (three hours for hourly polling); a tag moved when `freshness.movedTags` holds a row with `accepted` false (the lane also stops on it); a release is left unadopted when `freshness.releasesBehind` is above 0 and `freshness.oldestUnintegratedRelease.date` is older than your adoption window — run `status` with `--releases-file`, produced by `gh api --paginate repos/zomarit/stamity/releases --jq '.[] \| {tag_name, draft, published_at, assets: (.assets \| length)}' > releases.jsonl`, so only published releases that carry assets count and dates are `published_at`, never a tag's date; and your own release is left unadopted when `gh api --paginate repos/<your fork>/releases` shows no published release with assets for the integrated base within the window; `freshness.fetchedAt` is this run's fetch time, or with `--offline` the last recorded one, or null. A new `test/docsPages.test.ts` case pins the subsection's `status --json` command and the four field paths. Header and `REATTESTATION_DATE` per the hand-page rule. **CHANGELOG** `### Added`: "**The upstream lane closes the issues it superseded, and `status --json` reports freshness.** Once the integrated release reaches an open lane issue's release — a newer release landed, or the fork is up to date — the lane closes its own `needs conflict resolution` and `needs a reviewed push` issue with a comment naming the release and the run; an enterprise had closed four of them by hand. `status --json` gains `freshness`: when the upstream was last fetched, the latest and the integrated release, how many releases the fork is behind, and any upstream tag that moved since the fork integrated it — what a heartbeat needs, without git." **Follows:** `upstream-update.yml:1114-1161` (the conflict-issue step: marker identity, `gh` only, outputs and a summary line) and `scripts/upstream.mjs:1723-1750` (state filled into the one document shape). |
| `testCriteria` | **Given** `buildFreshness` over releases `v1.0.0`, `v1.1.0`, `v1.1.1`, `v1.2.0`, `v1.3.0` with `v1.0.0` integrated and `fetchedAt` `"2026-10-01T12:00:00.000Z"`, **then** `latestRelease.tag` is `v1.3.0`, `integratedRelease.tag` `v1.0.0`, `releasesBehind` 4, `oldestUnintegratedRelease.tag` `v1.1.0` and `movedTags` `[]`; with nothing integrated, `integratedRelease` is null and `releasesBehind` is the admitted count; with every release integrated, `releasesBehind` is 0 and `oldestUnintegratedRelease` null; with a record for `v1.0.0` whose `releaseCommit` differs from the tag's current commit, `movedTags` names it with both commits. **Given** a fork fixture (`test/upstream/lane.test.ts`, the lifecycle describe at `:735`), **when** `status` runs online, **then** `freshness.fetchedAt` is an ISO timestamp between the times read before and after the run, `latestRelease.tag` equals `doc.target.tag`, `releasesBehind` equals `doc.candidates.length` (4 with the fixture's maintenance tag `v1.1.1`), and `<git common dir>/stamity-upstream-fetch.json` holds the same `fetchedAt`; **when** `status --offline` runs next, **then** `fetchedAt` equals the recorded value; after the file is deleted, **then** `--offline` gives null; with the file holding `{`, **then** null and the message. **Given** a fork that integrated `v1.3.0`, **then** `releasesBehind` is 0 and the outcome is `up-to-date`. **Given** `status --release <older tag>`, **then** `latestRelease` is still the newest admitted release. **Given** the guide's heartbeat subsection, **when** its `jq` filters run (with jq on PATH; skipped without) against a fixture fork's real `status --json` document, **then** each prints a non-null value — the recipe's commands are ones the lane runs. **Given** the close step's body run with a stub `gh` on PATH (in the style of `test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts:779-791`) answering `issue list` from a fixture — lane conflict `v1.8.0` (#11), lane reviewed-push `v1.9.0` (#12), lane conflict `v1.11.0` (#13), lane conflict `v1.10.0-rc.2` (#16), an issue titled `Upstream v1.8.0 needs conflict resolution` without the marker (#14), a first-line marker with tag `v1.8` (#15) — **when** `INTEGRATED_TAG=v1.10.0`, **then** the closes are exactly #11, #12 and #16, each with `--reason completed` and a `--comment` naming `v1.10.0` and the run, and #13, #14 and #15 stay open; **when** the stub's second listing omits the closed ones and the step runs again, **then** no `gh issue close`, `comment` or `edit` call is made. **Given** tags `v1.9.0`/`v1.10.0` and `v1.10.0-rc.2`/`v1.10.0-rc.10`, **then** the jq order is numeric (`v1.9.0` < `v1.10.0`, `rc.2` < `rc.10`, `v1.10.0-rc.10` < `v1.10.0`). **Given** a stub whose `issue close` fails for #12, **then** #11 and #16 are still closed, the evidence file lists #12 under `failed`, and the step exits 1 with an error naming #12; **given** `issue list` failing, **then** it closes nothing and exits 1. **Given** the step, **then** its body contains `gh issue close` with `--comment` and no `gh issue delete`, `gh issue edit`, `gh issue reopen` or `--search`, no line invokes `node `, `npm ` or `npx `, and the verdict step stays last (`test/ci/upstreamWorkflow.test.ts:613-616`). **Given** the control-data case (`test/ci/upstreamWorkflow.test.ts:385-431`), **then** `INTEGRATED_TAG` joins the fields that must be rejected by name, and `prepare`'s `integrated_tag` output and `publish`'s `INTEGRATED_TAG` env are pinned as `merge_commit`'s are (`:424-430`). |
| `edgeCases` | A newer release that conflicts → the integrated release has not moved, so only issues at or below it close; the newer release's own issue stays open. `validation-failed` → the release is not integrated (invariant 2), so the integrated tag is the older one and its issue stays. An `ancestry-missing` fork → `integrated` is null, the output is empty and the step does not run. A dry-run dispatch → `publish` does not run, so nothing closes. A lane issue a reviewer renamed → its body still opens with the marker, so it closes like the others. A human issue that begins with the exact marker line → treated as the lane's, the identity rule the update path already applies when it rewrites such a body; the close is reversible and comments. A forged `integrated_tag` from the job that ran the fork's code → shape-checked only, so the worst case is lane issues closed early, each with a comment and the run link (Risks). An exact `releases.pattern` whose tag is absent upstream (Package 18's `ls-remote` path) → no fetch ran, so `fetchedAt` is not rewritten. unverified until Package 18 lands: whether that path should count a successful `ls-remote` as a fetch. |
