### u1-lane-package-files — the lane derives `package.json` and `package-lock.json` from the release and the fork's identity, and sets the next `-<suffix>.N`

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| `id` | u1-lane-package-files |
| `requirements` | REQ-UPSTREAM-024, REQ-UPSTREAM-001 (amended), REQ-PLUGIN-053 |
| `files` | `scripts/identity-files.mjs` (new), `scripts/fork-identity.mjs` (`IDENTITY_KEYS` `:59` moves to the new module and is imported back), `scripts/upstream.mjs` (header `:56-73`, `:83-118`; `CONFIG_DEFAULTS` and `CONFIG_KEYS` `:233-246`; `parseConfig` `:287-355`; `buildRecord` `:819-847`; `createDocument` `:1686-1717`; `renderReport` `:1790-1875`; `runPreview` `:1958-1998`; `finishMerge` `:2015`, `:2080-2100`; `startMerge` `:2122-2153`), `test/upstream/identityFiles.test.ts` (new), `test/upstream/fixtures.ts` (a `createPackageFilesUpstream` builder beside `createUpstream` `:405`; `LaneDocument` `:567-616`), `test/upstream/lane.test.ts` (one new describe; configuration cases), `docs/enterprise-forks.md` (the key table `:266-279`; a new `### Let the lane carry your package files` after `:331`; `## Resolve a conflict` `:422-426`; the record bullet `:383`), `test/docsPages.test.ts` (one new case), `CHANGELOG.md` (`### Added`) |
| `interfaces` | **Current.** The lane has no package-file logic: `package.json` and `package-lock.json` are ordinary merge inputs (neither appears in `scripts/upstream.mjs`), and the guide's `generatedPaths` omits both (`docs/enterprise-forks.md:297-303`). Every upstream release moves the version and usually dependencies, while a fork's identity and suffixed version live in both files. r4 measured (2026-10-06, a copy of the two files and the presets): a fork at `1.11.0-<suffix>.1` merging an upstream bump to `1.12.0` leaves `UU package-lock.json` and `UU package.json`; `fork-identity.mjs` then refuses mid-merge, first "package.json is not valid JSON" (`scripts/fork-identity.mjs:189`), after staging upstream's side "has uncommitted changes" (`:337`). The lane never sets the version, which is how an older fork merged v1.7.0 and stayed at `1.6.0-<suffix>.1`. Invariant 4 forbids a side preference (`scripts/upstream.mjs:65-67`); generated paths are resolved by derivation from their sources (`:67`). Measured 2026-10-06 (a planning-lab prototype over `git show v1.10.0:` and `v1.11.0:`): both files round-trip byte for byte through `JSON.stringify(value, null, 2) + "\n"`; between the releases only `version` and `devDependencies` changed in `package.json` and 61 of 433 lock entries moved; a fork at `1.10.0-acme.2` carrying `fork-identity.mjs`'s identity, a `stamity.distribution.sources` entry and a root-only lock refresh derives `1.11.0-acme.1`, a `package.json` equal to upstream's outside the fork-owned keys and a lock equal to upstream's outside the root identity; one added dependency, or a lock refreshed by npm 10 (38 `libc` fields dropped), fails the guard. The CI `prepare` job runs Node 22.22.2 (`upstream-update.yml:236-239`), whose npm 10 drops those 38 fields on any re-lock. **New module `scripts/identity-files.mjs`** (Node built-ins only, no I/O; imported by `scripts/fork-identity.mjs`, `scripts/upstream.mjs` and, in `u1-fork-ci-job`, `scripts/ci/fork-probe.mjs`): `export const IDENTITY_KEYS` (moved from `fork-identity.mjs:59`, same order); `export const FORK_OWNED_PACKAGE_KEYS = [...IDENTITY_KEYS, 'version']`, and every key under `stamity.distribution` is fork-owned too (the guide documents `branch`, `tagPattern`, `ownerEmail` and `sources` there, `docs/enterprise-forks.md:869-878`; file 02's `u2-channels-promote` adds `channels` and `soakDays`); `export function packageChangesBeyondIdentity(base, fork): string[]` — the sorted dotted key paths where the fork's `package.json` differs from the merge base's outside the fork-owned keys; `export function lockChangesBeyondRoot(base, fork): string[]` — `lockfileVersion` and `requires` when they differ, and `packages[<key>]` for every entry that differs, the root entry's `name` and `version` (and the top-level pair) ignored; `export function nextForkVersion(upstreamVersion, forkVersion, suffix): string \| null` — `null` when `upstreamVersion` is not a plain `X.Y.Z`; `upstreamVersion` when `suffix` is undefined; otherwise `<X.Y.Z>-<suffix>.<N>`, N = the fork's N + 1 when its version is `<the same X.Y.Z>-<suffix>.<N>` (a re-release of the same base), else 1 (a new base); `export function deriveIdentityFiles({ base, fork, upstream, suffix }): { derived: true, pkg, lock, version } \| { derived: false, reasons: string[] }` — each side `{ pkg, lock }`, parsed; the derived `pkg` is upstream's object with each fork-owned key the fork set differently from the base copied from the fork (an absent key included) and `version` = `nextForkVersion(…)`; the derived `lock` is upstream's with `name`, `version`, `packages[""].name` and `packages[""].version` set to the derived pair; `reasons` holds up to five paths from each guard and `upstream version <v> is a prerelease` when `nextForkVersion` is null; `export function serialiseJson(value): string` = `${JSON.stringify(value, null, 2)}\n`. **New config key** `identityFiles` (`CONFIG_KEYS` `:244`): absent (the default, today's behaviour) or an object whose only key is `suffix`, a string matching `^[a-z0-9]+$` (the `--suffix` rule of `u1-renovate-suffix`); anything else is a config error, exit 2: `"identityFiles" must be an object whose only key is "suffix" (lowercase letters and digits)`; `parseConfig` returns `identityFiles: null \| { suffix?: string }`. **New, the merge (`startMerge` `:2122-2153`):** right after `classifyConflicts` (`:2134`), when `config.identityFiles !== null`, the lane reads both files at `session.state.mergeBase`, `HEAD` and `MERGE_HEAD` with `showFile` (`:902`), parses them (an absent or unparseable side → no derivation, and a message naming the path and the side), and calls `deriveIdentityFiles`. Derived → both files are written into the worktree with `serialiseJson`, `git add -- package.json package-lock.json` runs, `session.state.derived` = `[{ path: "package.json", version }, { path: "package-lock.json", version }]`, and the conflict entries for the two paths gain `derived: true` and `resolvedBy: "derivation"`. Not derived → both files stay exactly as git left them and one message names the reasons: `package.json and package-lock.json were left for a person: <reasons>; resolve both (take <tag>'s side, re-apply your identity with scripts/fork-identity.mjs, set the version, re-lock with npm 11), git add them, then run continue`. The derivation runs whether or not the files conflicted (the version must move on a clean merge too) and writes only bytes that differ. The human test at `:2142` becomes `conflicts.some((c) => !c.generated && !c.derived)`; `conflictedBlobs` (`:2138-2140`) and `finishMerge`'s generated set (`:2015`) skip derived entries; `finishMerge`'s `resolvedBy` map (`:2080-2086`) keeps `"derivation"`; the record (`:2087-2100`) carries `derived`. A regenerate command that rewrites a derived file shows as a tracked path outside `generatedPaths` and ends `regenerate-failed` (`:2033-2044`). **Document.** `createDocument` gains `derived: []` after `unlistedGenerated`; `buildRecord` gains `derived: input.derived ?? []`; `DOCUMENT_VERSION` stays 1 (added keys); the document comment (`:100-104`) names `resolvedBy` `derivation` and the `derived` rows; `renderReport` prints `Derived from <tag> and this fork's identity: package.json (<version>), package-lock.json` when `derived` is not empty. **`preview`** (`:1958-1998`): with `identityFiles` it runs the same pure guard on the three sides after its merge (no write in the throwaway worktree) and adds `derivable: true` to the two conflict entries when `integrate` would derive them, plus `integrate derives package.json and package-lock.json from <tag> and this fork's identity (version <v>)`; its outcome rule (`:1989-1995`) is unchanged. **No network:** the derivation runs no npm (invariant 7; the root rewrite is what npm 11's `install --package-lock-only` writes when nothing else changed, measured in plan 016's 2026-10-01 probe); the recommended `regenerate` list's first entry, `npm ci --ignore-scripts` (`docs/enterprise-forks.md:289`), then installs from the derived pair through the fork's own npm configuration with lifecycle scripts off, and fails when the two are out of sync. **Invariant text:** the header's invariant 4 (`:65-67`) gains "With `identityFiles`, `package.json` and `package-lock.json` are derived from the release's copy and the fork's identity, and only when the fork changed nothing else in them, so the fork's side holds nothing the derived file loses (invariant 5)." **Docs.** The key table (`:266-279`) gains `\| identityFiles \| absent \| …the lane derives the files that carry your identity… \|` (the recommended block gains the key in `u1-lane-config`); a new `### Let the lane carry your package files` after `:331` says what the lane derives, the guard, the version rule (`<X.Y.Z>-<suffix>.1` for a new upstream base, the next N for a re-release of the same base, linking `u1-renovate-suffix`'s `### Version a fork's releases`), why no npm runs, what stops it (an added or bumped dependency, a hand-edited script, a lock an npm 10 refresh rewrote) and the hand path; `## Resolve a conflict` (`:422-426`) gains "Paths derived from your identity are marked `resolvedBy: derivation`."; the record bullet (`:383`) names `derivation`. Header and `REATTESTATION_DATE` per the hand-page rule. **CHANGELOG** `### Added`: "**The upstream lane carries a fork's package files.** With `identityFiles` in `.stamity/upstream.json`, `integrate` takes each release's `package.json` and `package-lock.json`, re-applies the fork's identity, sets the next `<version>-<suffix>.<n>` and re-locks the root without a network call, so a fork that changed nothing in those files beyond its identity integrates without a person. Every release from 1.7.0 to 1.11.0 stopped there before. A fork that changed its dependencies keeps the hand path, and the report says why." **Follows:** `scripts/upstream.mjs:2005-2120` (measured resolution, recorded per path) and `scripts/fork-identity.mjs:150-171` (`targetManifest`: the identity as data, every other key kept). |
| `testCriteria` | **Given** `deriveIdentityFiles` over three inline sides shaped like `v1.10.0` → `v1.11.0` (a fork at `1.10.0-acme.2` with `name` `@acme/stamity`, `repository`, `homepage`, `bugs`, `stamity.publisher`, `publishConfig.registry`, `stamity.distribution.sources`, and a root-only lock change), **then** `derived` is true, `version` is `1.11.0-acme.1`, `lockChangesBeyondRoot(upstream.lock, derived.lock)` is `[]`, `packageChangesBeyondIdentity(upstream.pkg, derived.pkg)` is `[]`, and the seven fork values are carried (`test/upstream/identityFiles.test.ts`). **Given** `nextForkVersion`, **then** `("1.11.0", "1.11.0-acme.1", "acme")` is `1.11.0-acme.2`, `("1.12.0", "1.11.0-acme.3", "acme")` is `1.12.0-acme.1`, `("1.12.0", "1.11.0", undefined)` is `1.12.0`, and `("1.12.0-rc.1", "1.11.0-acme.1", "acme")` is null. **Given** a fork with `dependencies.left-pad`, **then** not derived and `reasons` name `dependencies.left-pad` and `packages["node_modules/left-pad"]`; **given** a fork lock with one `libc` field dropped, **then** not derived, naming that entry; **given** a fork `scripts["fork:check"]`, **then** not derived, naming `scripts.fork:check`; **given** an upstream that adds `stamity.distribution.channels` the fork never set, **then** the derived file carries upstream's; **given** a fork that set it, **then** the fork's. **Given** `createPackageFilesUpstream` (releases `v1.0.0` and `v1.1.0`, each with a `package.json` of one dependency and a lockfileVersion-3 `package-lock.json`) and a fork at `v1.0.0` renamed `@acme/fixture` at `1.0.0-acme.1` with its lock root renamed and `identityFiles: { "suffix": "acme" }`, **when** `integrate --release v1.1.0` runs, **then** the outcome is `integrated`, the merge commit's `package.json` carries `@acme/fixture`, `1.1.0-acme.1` and upstream's dependency, its lock's root carries the same pair and every other entry equals upstream's, the record lists both paths `resolvedBy: "derivation"`, `doc.derived` names both, and no marker remains (`test/upstream/lane.test.ts`); **given** the same fork without `identityFiles`, **then** the outcome is `conflict` naming both paths; **given** a fork that added a dependency, **then** `conflict`, both files hold git's merge result unchanged, and a message names the dependency; **when** a person resolves both and runs `continue`, **then** `integrated` with `resolvedBy: "human"`; **given** `preview` with `identityFiles`, **then** both conflicts carry `derivable: true` and the operator's tree is byte-identical before and after. **Given** `identityFiles` set to `{ "suffix": "Acme" }`, `{ "suffix": "a.b" }`, `{ "other": 1 }` or `true`, **then** every verb exits 2 naming `identityFiles`; `{}` and `{ "suffix": "acme" }` parse. **Given** `npx vitest run test/docsPages.test.ts`, **then** the key table names `identityFiles` and the new subsection names `resolvedBy: derivation`. |
| `edgeCases` | A fork whose lock an earlier npm 10 refresh rewrote → stops once with today's hand path; after a person takes the release's lock plus the root identity, later integrations derive. An upstream prerelease target (`--prerelease`) → no derivation, a message, today's path. Upstream deletes or renames one of the files → that side is absent, no derivation, git's result stands. A fork with no lock → no derivation of the pair, a message. A fork without a suffix (`identityFiles: {}`) → the derived version is the release's own (`1.12.0`); a re-release of the same base needs a suffix, and the guide says so. `continue` after a derivation → the two paths are staged and not derived again. A fork whose identity was hand-made rather than written by `fork-identity.mjs` → the derivation copies the fork's values, and the identity gate of `u1-lane-config` then reports any difference from the script's target. A fork's own `regenerate` command that re-locks with npm 10 → it rewrites a derived file and the run ends `regenerate-failed` naming the lock. |
| `depends_on` | u1-renovate-suffix (`scripts/fork-identity.mjs`, `docs/enterprise-forks.md`, `test/docsPages.test.ts`, `CHANGELOG.md`), u1-secret-shaped-fixtures (`scripts/upstream.mjs`, `test/upstream/lane.test.ts`) |
| `verify` | `npx vitest run test/upstream/identityFiles.test.ts test/upstream/lane.test.ts test/ci/forkIdentityScript.test.ts test/docsPages.test.ts && npm run lint && npm run typecheck && npm run knip` |

### u1-lane-config — `fork-identity.mjs --lane-config` writes and checks the fork's `.stamity/upstream.json` from a template upstream ships outside `.stamity/`

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| `id` | u1-lane-config |
| `requirements` | REQ-UPSTREAM-025, REQ-PLUGIN-028 (amended) |
| `files` | `scripts/upstream-config.template.json` (new), `scripts/fork-identity.mjs` (header `:2-37`; `USAGE` `:49-53`; `parseArgs` `:65-85`; `plan` `:217-252`; `main` `:306-353`; new exports), `test/ci/forkIdentityScript.test.ts` (one new describe), `test/upstream/lane.test.ts` (one case in the configuration describe `:137`), `docs/enterprise-forks.md` (header `:5`; the flag list `:144-160`; `### Turn the workflows on last` `:212-214`; the `shadows` row `:279`; `### Start from these values for a fork of this repository` `:281-325`), `test/docsPages.test.ts` (one new case; `REATTESTATION_DATE` `:597` per the hand-page rule), `CHANGELOG.md` (`## [Unreleased]`, `### Added`) |
| `interfaces` | **Current.** The recommended configuration is a hand-copied block in the guide (`docs/enterprise-forks.md:283-307`) whose one gate is `{ "name": "check", "run": "npm ci --ignore-scripts && npm run check" }` (`:287`); no test reads that block. Upstream ships no `.stamity/upstream.json` on purpose: its presence arms the lane (`docs/specs/enterprise-upstream-lane.md:175-177`, `.github/workflows/upstream-update.yml:3-6`, the probe's sparse checkout of that one path `:137`, `:150`). `scripts/fork-identity.mjs` targets `package.json` and the two presets only (`:217-252`), runs both generators (`:61`, `:283-304`), and under `--check` writes nothing and exits 1 on drift (`:327-332`); a file with uncommitted edits it would change is refused (`:334-340`). A hand-copied file never learns of upstream's changes: `docs/measurements.md` joined `generatedPaths` on 2026-09-23 (`3fa99f9c`), and the enterprise's older file lacked it. The lane runs every gate through the platform shell (`scripts/upstream.mjs:1490-1492`, `shell: true`), and `validate` runs the gates in a worktree it may have just re-created, with no `node_modules` (`:2329-2353`, no regenerate step). **New, the template** `scripts/upstream-config.template.json`: the guide's recommended configuration without the two parts that depend on the fork's arguments — `{ "version": 1, "upstream": "https://github.com/zomarit/stamity", "gates": [{ "name": "check", "run": "npm ci --ignore-scripts && npm run check" }], "regenerate": [<the seven commands of :289-295>], "generatedPaths": [<the entries of :298-302>], "watch": [<the four of :304>] }`, two-space JSON with a final newline. It sits outside `.stamity/`, so it arms nothing, and outside `files` (`package.json:41-43`), so the npm package does not carry it. **New exports of `scripts/fork-identity.mjs`:** `LANE_CONFIG_PATH = '.stamity/upstream.json'`; `LANE_TEMPLATE_PATH = 'scripts/upstream-config.template.json'`; `identityGate({ repository, scope, registry, suffix }) → { name: 'identity', run: string }` with `run` = `npm ci --ignore-scripts && node scripts/fork-identity.mjs --repository <repository> [--scope <scope>] [--registry <registry>] [--suffix <suffix>] --lane-config --check` (each optional flag only when given, in that order; the install first because `validate` may run the gates in a fresh worktree and both generators import the dependencies); `renderLaneConfig(template, gate, suffix) → object` — the template with `gate` inserted before the gate named `check` (first when there is none) and `identityFiles` added after `watch`: `{ suffix }`, or `{}` without a suffix (the opt-in of `u1-lane-package-files`); `mergeLaneConfig(existing, rendered) → { config, labels: string[] }` — keeps every key and entry of `existing` and adds what the render has and `existing` lacks: the identity gate (a gate whose `run` starts `npm ci --ignore-scripts && node scripts/fork-identity.mjs ` or `node scripts/fork-identity.mjs ` and ends ` --check` is the identity gate; its `run` is replaced by the render's; when none exists, the render's gate goes before the gate named `check`, else first), every template `regenerate` entry it lacks (inserted right after the nearest preceding template entry it holds, else first), every template `generatedPaths` and `watch` entry it lacks (appended), and `identityFiles.suffix` set to the render's when `identityFiles` is present (an absent `identityFiles` is the fork's opt-out and stays absent); `labels` names each key that changed, in the order `gates`, `regenerate`, `generatedPaths`, `watch`, `identityFiles`. `upstream`, `remote`, `branch`, `releases`, `shadows`, `acceptedTagMoves` (`u1-lane-issues-freshness`) and every extra entry are the fork's and are never compared or rewritten. **New flag `--lane-config`** (boolean; `parseArgs` `:71`): `plan()` gains a fourth target at `LANE_CONFIG_PATH` — absent → the render, reported `created .stamity/upstream.json`; present → `mergeLaneConfig`, written as `JSON.stringify(config, null, 2) + "\n"` and reported `updated .stamity/upstream.json (<labels>)` or `unchanged .stamity/upstream.json`; not JSON or not an object → `Refusal(".stamity/upstream.json is not a JSON object; fix it and rerun.")`, exit 1, nothing written. Under `--check` the same comparison prints `drift .stamity/upstream.json (<labels>)`, and an absent file prints `drift .stamity/upstream.json (absent)`. Because the gate is a shell command, `--lane-config` refuses, before any write, an argument value carrying a character outside `[A-Za-z0-9@%+=:,./_~-]`: `Refusal("--lane-config writes the identity gate as a shell command, so every argument must be letters, digits and @%+=:,./_~- only; the value is not echoed.")`, exit 1. The dirty-file refusal covers the new target. `USAGE` and the header gain the flag; without it nothing about the lane file is read or written. **Docs.** `docs/enterprise-forks.md:281-325`: the heading stays; the block becomes `renderLaneConfig` for the guide's sample identity (`--repository https://github.com/acme/stamity-acme --registry https://npm.pkg.github.com --suffix acme`), so it opens with the identity gate, ends with `"identityFiles": { "suffix": "acme" }`, and carries no `shadows` line (its example moves into the `shadows` row's text, `:279`); the prose opens with the command that writes the file (`node scripts/fork-identity.mjs --repository "$STAMITY_PRIVATE_URL" --registry <url> --suffix <id> --lane-config`), names the template's path, says a rerun adds what a later upstream release added to the template and keeps every key and entry of yours, and that the identity gate runs `--lane-config --check`, so a lane run whose merged tree carries a newer template fails that gate until you rerun the command on the update branch; the paragraph explaining each list (`:309-325`) stays. The flag list (`:144`) names `--lane-config`; `### Turn the workflows on last` (`:212`) names the command. Header and `REATTESTATION_DATE` per the hand-page rule. **CHANGELOG** `### Added`: "**`fork-identity.mjs --lane-config` writes the fork's lane configuration.** Upstream now ships the recommended `.stamity/upstream.json` as `scripts/upstream-config.template.json`, where it arms nothing. The flag writes your file from it, with an identity gate ahead of `check` and your suffix, and a rerun adds what a later release added to the template while keeping your own keys; `--check` names a file that fell behind, so a hand-kept copy no longer misses a new generated path." **Follows:** `scripts/fork-identity.mjs:199-215` (a target computed and verified before any write) and `:327-348` (one report line per target). |
| `testCriteria` | **Given** a `fork()` checkout with no `.stamity/upstream.json`, **when** `fork-identity.mjs --repository <FORK_URL> --registry https://npm.pkg.github.com --suffix acme --lane-config` runs, **then** it exits 0, stdout contains `created .stamity/upstream.json`, and the file parses to the template plus `gates[0]` = `{ "name": "identity", "run": "npm ci --ignore-scripts && node scripts/fork-identity.mjs --repository <FORK_URL> --registry https://npm.pkg.github.com --suffix acme --lane-config --check" }`, `gates[1].name` = `"check"` and `identityFiles` = `{ "suffix": "acme" }`; **when** it reruns, **then** stdout contains `unchanged .stamity/upstream.json` and the file's sha256 is unchanged; **when** `--check` runs with the same arguments, **then** it exits 0 (`test/ci/forkIdentityScript.test.ts`). **Given** an existing file holding an `upstream` mirror URL, a `shadows` entry, an extra gate, an extra `generatedPaths` entry, a `regenerate` list without `node scripts/generate-docs.mjs` and a `generatedPaths` list without `docs/measurements.md`, **when** `--check` runs, **then** it exits 1, prints `drift .stamity/upstream.json (gates, regenerate, generatedPaths)` and the file is unchanged; **when** `--lane-config` runs, **then** the mirror URL, `shadows`, the extra gate and the extra entry are unchanged after a parse, `node scripts/generate-docs.mjs` sits right after `node scripts/generate-capability-matrix.mjs`, `docs/measurements.md` is present, and stdout names the same labels. **Given** an existing file without `identityFiles`, **when** `--lane-config --suffix acme` runs, **then** the file still has no `identityFiles` and `--check` names no `identityFiles` drift. **Given** `--lane-config` with `--registry https://registry.example.invalid/x;y`, **then** it exits 1, stderr names `--lane-config`, neither stream contains `x;y`, and no target's sha256 changed. **Given** `.stamity/upstream.json` holding `[`, **then** exit 1 naming the file and nothing written. **Given** `parseConfig(<the template's text>, <its path>)` (`test/upstream/lane.test.ts`), **then** it returns without throwing and its `gates`, `regenerate` and `generatedPaths` equal the template's. **Given** `npx vitest run test/docsPages.test.ts`, **then** a new case parses the `json` block under `### Start from these values for a fork of this repository` and it deep-equals `renderLaneConfig(<the template>, identityGate(<the sample arguments>), "acme")`, and the section names `--lane-config`. **Given** the canonical tree (an `it.skipIf(!canonical().canonical)` case titled through `canonicalOnly`, `test/support/identity.ts:74,165`, so a fork that committed its own file passes), **then** `git ls-files .stamity/upstream.json` prints nothing (`test/ci/forkIdentityScript.test.ts`). |
| `edgeCases` | A fork that never runs `--lane-config` → nothing changes; its hand-written file keeps working. A fork that deleted `identityFiles` to keep the package files for a person → it stays deleted and `--check` reports nothing for it. A template entry a fork removed on purpose (it ships no APM, so it dropped `generate-apm-package.mjs`) → each `--lane-config` run adds it back and `--check` reports drift; the guide says to keep the command, since a regenerate step that rewrites nothing costs seconds. `.stamity/upstream.json` with uncommitted edits → the existing dirty refusal, naming it. Windows → the gate's `&&` runs under cmd.exe as under sh. A fork whose `upstream` is an approved mirror → kept. The canonical checkout → nothing here runs `--lane-config`; a file written there by hand would arm the lane and show in `git status`, and the canonical-only criterion fails the gate on it. |
| `depends_on` | u1-lane-package-files (the lane accepts `identityFiles`, which the render writes, `scripts/fork-identity.mjs`, `docs/enterprise-forks.md`, `test/docsPages.test.ts`, `CHANGELOG.md`), u1-renovate-suffix (`--suffix`, `test/ci/forkIdentityScript.test.ts`) |
| `verify` | `npx vitest run test/ci/forkIdentityScript.test.ts test/upstream/lane.test.ts test/docsPages.test.ts && npm run lint && npm run typecheck` |

### u1-lane-app-token — the lane's publish job mints a per-run GitHub App token, narrowed to this repository and three permissions

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| `id` | u1-lane-app-token |
| `requirements` | REQ-UPSTREAM-026, REQ-UPSTREAM-013 (amended) |
| `files` | `.github/workflows/upstream-update.yml` (header `:36-52`, `:69-72`; `publish` env `:440-454`; new steps before `Checkout the integration branch` `:469-475`; `Detect the push credential` `:545-562`, moved and widened; `Compose the report body` `:699-746`; every step that runs `gh`: `:581`, `:830`, `:907`, `:1114` and `u1-lane-issues-freshness`'s close step; the pull-request error `:1085`), `test/ci/upstreamWorkflow.test.ts` (`:231-281`; one new describe), `docs/enterprise-forks.md` (`### Add the optional token, and what it buys` `:1240-1264`), `test/docsPages.test.ts` (the lane-section case `u1-lane-issues-freshness` adds), `CHANGELOG.md` (`### Added`) |
| `interfaces` | **Current.** `publish` holds one credential: the job env `GH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.STAMITY_UPSTREAM_TOKEN \|\| github.token }}` (`upstream-update.yml:446`) serves every `gh` call, and the same expression is the checkout's push `token:` (`:475`). With the organisation setting "Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests" off — the enterprise's case — `gh pr create` fails on the repository token (`:1083-1086`), so the lane depends on a long-lived personal token. A GitHub App appears only in prose (`:51-52`; guide `:1256-1259`; `docs/specs/enterprise-upstream-lane.md:456-458`) and on plan 010's not-built list, "revisit only when a company asks" (`docs/plans/010-enterprise-release-01.md:61,66`); the enterprise asks. Vendor (read 2026-10-06, https://github.com/actions/create-github-app-token README): inputs `client-id` (or the legacy `app-id`) and `private-key`, plus `owner`, `repositories` and `permission-<name>`; `owner` set with `repositories` empty scopes the token to every repository of the owner's installation, both empty to the current repository; the token is revoked in the action's post step; the newest release is `v3.2.0`, commit `bcd2ba49218906704ab6c1aa796996da409d3eb1` (resolved 2026-10-06 with `gh api repos/actions/create-github-app-token/commits/v3.2.0 --jq .sha`). It is GitHub-owned (the `actions` organisation), so an allowed-actions policy of GitHub-owned actions admits it. A `schedule` event's payload is "Not applicable" (plan 014 f3's reading), so `github.event.repository.name` may be empty on the hourly run — and an empty `repositories` with `owner` set would widen the token to the whole installation. **New.** (1) `Detect the push credential` moves to right after `Name the artifact directory` and answers three ways, testing each value for emptiness only and printing none: env `APP_CLIENT_ID: ${{ vars.STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_CLIENT_ID }}`, `APP_KEY: ${{ secrets.STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_KEY }}`, `STAMITY_UPSTREAM_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.STAMITY_UPSTREAM_TOKEN }}`; both App values → `credential=app`; exactly one → `::error title=Lane App misconfigured::STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_CLIENT_ID (a repository variable) and STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_KEY (a secret) are set together or not at all, and one of them is missing. Nothing was pushed, edited or opened.` and exit 1; else the personal token → `credential=token`; else `credential=repository`. It also writes `repository_name=${GITHUB_REPOSITORY#*/}` and exits 1 when that is empty (`GITHUB_REPOSITORY` is set on every trigger). The `elevated` output stays (`true` for `app` and `token`) so the body step reads it unchanged; a notice names the credential in use. (2) New step `Mint the lane's App token` (`id: app-token`), `if: steps.credential.outputs.credential == 'app'`, `uses: actions/create-github-app-token@bcd2ba49218906704ab6c1aa796996da409d3eb1 # v3.2.0`, `with`: `client-id: ${{ vars.STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_CLIENT_ID }}`, `private-key: ${{ secrets.STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_KEY }}`, `owner: ${{ github.repository_owner }}`, `repositories: ${{ steps.credential.outputs.repository_name }}`, `permission-contents: write`, `permission-pull-requests: write`, `permission-issues: write` — the three writes the job declares (`:436-439`); no `permission-workflows` (the lane never pushes a workflow file, `:21-34`) and no `permission-administration`. (3) `TOKEN` = `${{ steps.app-token.outputs.token \|\| secrets.STAMITY_UPSTREAM_TOKEN \|\| github.token }}` is the checkout's `token:` (`:475`) and the `env.GH_TOKEN` of every step whose `run` invokes `gh`; the job-level `GH_TOKEN` (`:446`) goes, because a job's `env` cannot read the `steps` context — so the artifact download and upload steps no longer hold a token in their environment. `probe` and `prepare` read no App value. (4) The provenance row (`:722-726`) names `the lane's GitHub App (a per-run installation token for this repository)`, `STAMITY_UPSTREAM_TOKEN` or the repository token; the approval paragraph (`:730-733`) prints only for the repository token; the pull-request error (`:1085`) names the App's Pull requests permission beside the personal token's. (5) The header (`:36-52`) states the three credentials in order and that the App's token is minted in `publish`, narrowed to this repository and three permissions, and revoked when the job ends; the pin sentence (`:69-72`) names the one action this file pins that no other workflow pins, with its resolution command and date. **Docs.** `### Add the optional token, and what it buys` becomes `### Add an App or a token, and what each buys`: the App (recommended) — create it under the organisation with Contents, Pull requests and Issues set to read and write (Metadata read comes with them) and no Workflows or Administration permission, install it on this repository only (its key can mint a token for any repository it is installed on), store the client ID as the repository variable `STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_CLIENT_ID` and the private key as the secret `STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_KEY`; each run mints a token for this repository with those three permissions, valid at most an hour and revoked when the job ends; the organisation setting "Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests" governs the repository token, not the App's; the personal token stays as the alternative; the caveats at `:1259-1264` stay. **CHANGELOG** `### Added`: "**The upstream lane can publish with a GitHub App.** With an App's client ID in `STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_CLIENT_ID` and its private key in `STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_KEY`, the publish job mints a token per run for this repository only, with Contents, Pull requests and Issues write and nothing else, instead of relying on a long-lived personal token where Actions may not open pull requests. The personal token and the repository token stay the fallbacks." **Follows:** `upstream-update.yml:545-562` (a credential tested in the shell, never printed) and `release.yml:907-914` (a GitHub-owned action pinned to a full SHA with its exact version). |
| `testCriteria` | **Given** `npx vitest run test/ci/upstreamWorkflow.test.ts`, **then** the mint step's `uses` is `actions/create-github-app-token@<40 hex>` with the trailing `# v3.2.0`, its `if` is `steps.credential.outputs.credential == 'app'`, and its `with` keys are exactly `client-id`, `private-key`, `owner`, `repositories`, `permission-contents`, `permission-pull-requests`, `permission-issues`, with `owner` = `${{ github.repository_owner }}`, `repositories` = `${{ steps.credential.outputs.repository_name }}` and each permission `write`. **Given** the `publish` steps, **then** they begin `Name the artifact directory`, `Detect the push credential`, `Mint the lane's App token`, `Checkout the integration branch`. **Given** `secretsReadBy`, **then** `publish` reads exactly `["STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_KEY", "STAMITY_UPSTREAM_TOKEN"]` (TEST CHANGE of `:271`, justified: the App key is the second publish-only secret) and `probe` and `prepare` read none; `configOf("prepare")` contains no `STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_CLIENT_ID`. **Given** the checkout step and every `publish` step whose `run` contains `gh `, **then** each carries the `TOKEN` expression (as `token:` or `env.GH_TOKEN`), and `publish` has no job-level `GH_TOKEN` (TEST CHANGE of `:275`). **Given** the detection step's shell run with each combination of the three inputs and `GITHUB_REPOSITORY=acme/stamity-acme`, **then** it writes `credential` = `app`, `token` or `repository` and `repository_name=stamity-acme`; with exactly one App value **then** it exits 1, its output names both App names and contains neither value; with `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` empty **then** it exits 1. **Given** `jobOf("publish").permissions`, **then** it still equals `{ contents: write, pull-requests: write, issues: write }` (`:240-244`). **Given** `npx vitest run test/ci/workflow.test.ts`, **then** the pin-policy case (`:4177`) passes for the new `uses` line. **Given** `npx vitest run test/docsPages.test.ts`, **then** the lane section names `STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_CLIENT_ID` and `STAMITY_UPSTREAM_APP_KEY`. |
| `edgeCases` | An App and a personal token both configured → the App wins and the notice says the token is unused. An App installed on several repositories → the minted token still covers this one only; the guide says to install it here alone, because the key itself can mint wider tokens. An App missing one of the three permissions → the mint fails (an installation token cannot exceed the installation) and the run is red before any push. An expired or revoked key → the mint fails; nothing pushed. Pull requests opened with the App token start their `pull_request` runs without an approval prompt, as with a personal token — unverified until a fork's first App-token run; its checks are the probe. The classic-protection read needs Administration and stays unread under the App token; a rulesets-only repository declares it with `STAMITY_LANDING_POLICY` (`u1-lane-issues-freshness`). |
| `depends_on` | u1-lane-issues-freshness (`.github/workflows/upstream-update.yml`, the close step's `GH_TOKEN`, `test/ci/upstreamWorkflow.test.ts`, `docs/enterprise-forks.md`, `test/docsPages.test.ts`, `CHANGELOG.md`) |
| `verify` | `npx vitest run test/ci/upstreamWorkflow.test.ts test/ci/workflow.test.ts test/docsPages.test.ts && npm run lint && npm run typecheck` |

### u1-fork-docs-identity — a fork's hand-written docs carry its identity, `--check` keeps them so, and the lane re-applies it

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| `id` | u1-fork-docs-identity |
| `requirements` | REQ-PLUGIN-052, REQ-PLUGIN-028 (amended), REQ-UPSTREAM-024 |
| `files` | `scripts/identity-files.mjs` (new exports), `scripts/fork-identity.mjs` (`plan()` `:217-252`; header; `USAGE`), `scripts/upstream.mjs` (the derivation of `u1-lane-package-files` widened to the pages), `SECURITY.md` (`:153` reworded), `docs/packs-and-trust.md` (`:215` reworded), `docs/troubleshooting.md` (`:232` reworded), `test/docsPages.test.ts` (`SCOPED_PACKAGE` `:345`, `ADVISORY_URL` `:364`, `INSTALL_COMMAND` `:361`, `ALLOWED_URL` `:407`, the issue URL `:2790`, the managed-settings case `:2332-2334`; one new case), `test/ci/forkIdentityScript.test.ts` (one new describe), `test/upstream/identityFiles.test.ts`, `test/upstream/lane.test.ts` (one case), `docs/enterprise-forks.md` (the identity step `:137-160`; `### Let the lane carry your package files`), `CHANGELOG.md` (`### Added`); the pages it rewrites in a fork, never upstream: `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, `docs/getting-started.md`, `docs/migration.md`, `docs/packs-and-trust.md`, `docs/plugins.md`, `docs/troubleshooting.md` |
| `interfaces` | **Current.** At `d10db029` `git grep -c 'zomarit/stamity'` counts 43 lines on six hand pages (`docs/enterprise-forks.md` 5, `getting-started` 5, `migration` 12, `packs-and-trust` 3, `plugins` 6, `troubleshooting` 12) plus `CONTRIBUTING.md` 1, `SECURITY.md` 4, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` 1 and `README.md` 1. The five in the fork guide name upstream on purpose (the import `:106`, `:110`, the lane's `upstream` `:286`, the managed-settings sample `:1047`, `:1058`). `scripts/fork-identity.mjs` rewrites no page (`:217-252`), so a fork's pages show upstream's install commands, issue tracker and advisory form; a fork that edits them conflicts whenever upstream edits a line nearby; and `test/docsPages.test.ts` fails a fork that fixed them: `ALLOWED_URL` admits only `https://github.com/zomarit/stamity…` (`:407`, asserted `:800`), README must contain `@zomarit/stamity` (`:345`, `:830`), and SECURITY.md and the troubleshooting guide must contain the canonical advisory and issue URLs (`:364`, `:1576`, `:2790-2794`). **New, `scripts/identity-files.mjs`:** `CANONICAL_IDENTITY = { name: '@zomarit/stamity', route: 'zomarit/stamity', url: 'https://github.com/zomarit/stamity' }`; `IDENTITY_DOC_PATHS` = the nine pages above in path order (`docs/enterprise-forks.md` is not one); `docsIdentityOf(pkg) → { name, route, url }` from `name` and `repository.url` (a `git+` prefix and a `.git` suffix removed, the rule of `test/support/identity.ts:112-125`), throwing on a URL off the public GitHub host; `rewriteDocsIdentity(text, from, to) → string` — exactly four spellings change, wherever they occur: (1) the scoped name `<from.name>` not followed by `[\w-]`; (2) `<from.url>/issues` and `<from.url>/security/advisories/new`; (3) `apm install <from.route>` followed by `#`, white space or a line end; (4) the Renovate extends spelling `github><from.route>//`. Everything else stays: the `actions/runs/` evidence links (`SECURITY.md:316-317`), a bare `<owner>/<repo>` in prose (`docs/plugins.md:109`, `:132`, the 2026-09-26 walk of the canonical distribution), and the owner's attribution (`README.md:19`, `:159`). Line endings are kept. **Upstream text, three rewordings** so no sentence uses spelling (1) to contrast the canonical package with a fork: `docs/troubleshooting.md:232` ("a renamed fork's package stands in place of `@zomarit/stamity`" → "a renamed fork's own package name stands in its place"), `docs/packs-and-trust.md:215` and `SECURITY.md:153` ("the public one for `@zomarit/stamity`" → "the public npm registry for this repository's own package"); `docs/plugins.md:551-558` names the canonical extends as `github>zomarit/stamity//renovate/plugins.json` and `…/companion.json` (written by `u1-renovate-suffix`'s fold of inbox ``"the Renovate presets passage does not say that a fork releasing suffixed versions runs `fork-identity.mjs --suffix`" at 077e8a78``). **`fork-identity.mjs`:** `plan()` gains one target per existing file of `IDENTITY_DOC_PATHS`: `rewriteDocsIdentity(text, CANONICAL_IDENTITY, target)`, then, when the current `package.json` carries another non-canonical identity, the same from that identity (a second rename moves the pages too); label `identity`; a page with nothing to rewrite is `unchanged`; `--check` prints `drift <page> (identity)`; the dirty refusal applies. The canonical tree under its own identity → every page `unchanged`. **Lane:** with `identityFiles`, the derivation of `u1-lane-package-files` also covers `IDENTITY_DOC_PATHS`, one page at a time: when the fork's copy equals the base copy, or equals `rewriteDocsIdentity(<base copy>, CANONICAL_IDENTITY, docsIdentityOf(<HEAD package.json>))`, the derived copy is that rewrite of the `MERGE_HEAD` copy, written and staged (`resolvedBy: "derivation"` when it had conflicted, listed in `derived`); otherwise git's result stands — a page the fork customised beyond its identity is the fork's to merge. **`test/docsPages.test.ts`:** `ALLOWED_URL` becomes `^https://github\.com/(?:zomarit/stamity\|<repositoryRoute().slug, escaped>)(?:/[\w./-]*)?$` (one alternative on the canonical tree); `SCOPED_PACKAGE` (the README checks `:830`, `INSTALL_COMMAND` `:361`) = `canonical().name`; `ADVISORY_URL` (`:364`, used `:1576`, `:2793`) = `${repositoryRoute().url}/security/advisories/new`; the issue URL at `:2790` = `${repositoryRoute().url}/issues`; the managed-settings case (`:2317-2344`) keeps canonical constants, renamed `CANONICAL_PACKAGE` and `CANONICAL_OWNER`, because that block names upstream's distribution on purpose; `OWNER` stays canonical (attribution). On the canonical tree every value equals today's literal. **Docs.** The identity step (`:137-160`) says the script also rewrites the install commands, the issue and advisory links and the Renovate extends lines of nine named pages, and leaves the fork guide's upstream URLs alone; `### Let the lane carry your package files` says the lane derives those pages too. Header and `REATTESTATION_DATE` per the hand-page rule, on every hand page this unit rewords. **CHANGELOG** `### Added`: "**A fork's docs carry its own identity.** `fork-identity.mjs` now rewrites the install commands, the issue and advisory links and the Renovate extends lines of nine pages to the fork's package and repository, `--check` reports a page that fell back, and with `identityFiles` the upstream lane re-applies the identity to each release's copy instead of leaving a conflict. The fork guide keeps naming upstream where it means upstream." **Follows:** `scripts/fork-identity.mjs:199-215` (a value rewritten in place, verified, the file's formatting kept). |
| `testCriteria` | **Given** `rewriteDocsIdentity` over a fixture text holding the four spellings, an `actions/runs/123` link, a bare `zomarit/stamity` in prose, `@zomarit/stamity-x` and `zomarit/stamityx`, **then** exactly the four spellings change; applying it twice equals applying it once; a CRLF text keeps CRLF; `docsIdentityOf` on a `git+https://github.com/acme/stamity-acme.git` manifest gives route `acme/stamity-acme` and url `https://github.com/acme/stamity-acme`, and throws for a host other than the public GitHub host (`test/upstream/identityFiles.test.ts`). **Given** a `fork()` copy, **when** `fork-identity.mjs --repository <FORK_URL> --registry https://npm.pkg.github.com` runs, **then** stdout has `updated <page> (identity)` for each of the nine that carries a spelling; afterwards none of the nine contains `CANONICAL_IDENTITY.name` (imported, not typed), `docs/enterprise-forks.md` is byte-identical, and `SECURITY.md` keeps its two `actions/runs/` links; a rerun prints `unchanged` for all nine; `--check` exits 0; with one page restored from the canonical tree, `--check` exits 1 printing `drift <page> (identity)`; a second rename to `<OTHER_URL>` moves every page to the second identity (`test/ci/forkIdentityScript.test.ts`). **Given** `npx vitest run test/docsPages.test.ts` on the canonical tree, **then** every existing case passes; a new case applies `rewriteDocsIdentity` with the identity `@fork-probe/stamity` / `fork-probe/stamity-fork` to each of the nine in memory and finds no rewriteable spelling of the canonical identity left, and the canonical route mentions that remain are exactly `SECURITY.md` 2 (the `actions/runs/` links) and `docs/plugins.md` 2 (`:109`, `:132`), every other page 0; the URL rule built for that route admits `https://github.com/fork-probe/stamity-fork/issues`. **Given** a lane fixture page holding `npx @zomarit/stamity init`, a fork that applied the rewrite, and a release that adds `npx @zomarit/stamity sync` beside it, **when** `integrate` runs with `identityFiles`, **then** the merged page names the fork's package on both lines and carries no marker; **given** a page the fork customised beyond the identity, **then** git's result stands (`test/upstream/lane.test.ts`). |
| `edgeCases` | A fork that has not rewritten its pages yet → the first integration with `identityFiles` rewrites them (its copy equals the base copy); without `identityFiles`, its identity gate reports `drift <page> (identity)` until `fork-identity.mjs` runs once. A fork that edited a listed page beyond its identity → the lane leaves the page to git; the script still rewrites only the four spellings and never the fork's own text. A private fork (no registry) → the rewritten commands read `npx @<scope>/stamity …`, which run only where the package is installed; the CLI itself prints `npx --no` for such a fork (`docs/enterprise-forks.md:190-195`), and the pages keep the shorter form (named, not changed). Package 21 rewrites these pages after Package 20 → it must keep the four spellings as the only canonical mentions there, and the new docs case fails if it does not. The docs site keeps upstream's `REPO_URL` (`website/docusaurus.config.ts:38`); forks do not build it (`u1-lane-issues-freshness` guards `docs-site.yml`'s build). The leak gate gains no rule: the rewrite introduces only the fork's own names. |
| `depends_on` | u1-lane-package-files (`scripts/identity-files.mjs`, the derivation in `scripts/upstream.mjs`), u1-lane-app-token (`docs/enterprise-forks.md`, `test/docsPages.test.ts`, `CHANGELOG.md`), u1-renovate-suffix (`docs/plugins.md:551-558`) |
| `verify` | `npx vitest run test/upstream/identityFiles.test.ts test/upstream/lane.test.ts test/ci/forkIdentityScript.test.ts test/ci/forkIdentity.test.ts test/docsPages.test.ts && npm run gate && npm run lint && npm run typecheck` |

### u1-fork-release-hardening — a fork release is rehearsed before its tag, stamps its distribution commit, carries both changelogs, and refuses a public destination

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| `id` | u1-fork-release-hardening |
| `requirements` | REQ-PLUGIN-050, REQ-PLUGIN-051, REQ-PLUGIN-027 (amended) |
| `files` | `.github/workflows/fork-release.yml` (header `:17-31`; `probe` outputs `:83-89` and step `:91-163`; `gates` outputs `:174-182`, a new step after `Build plugin distribution` `:344-372`, the upload `:375-381`; `publish`: `Refuse an unarmed destination` `:419-441`, a new verify step after `:456-473`, two new steps around `Publish to the registry` `:644-666`, a new step after `Push plugin distribution` `:671-751`, `Create GitHub release` `:757-797`; `dry-run-summary` `:803-847`), `test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts`, `docs/enterprise-forks.md` (the distribution-commit paragraph `:901-904`; the variable table `:928-933`; `### Tag a release` `:947-985`; `### Know what proves a release` `:996-1003`), `test/docsPages.test.ts` (one new case), `CHANGELOG.md` (`### Added`, `### Fixed`) |
| `interfaces` | **Current.** A tag-push run meets npm only in `publish`, after the environment approval, with the immutable tag already pushed (the enterprise burned `1.10.0-<suffix>.1` that way); `u1-npm-dist-tag` as amended runs the dry run in `gates`, and the guide's `### Tag a release` (`:947-955`) still tags with no rehearsal step. `release.json` carries `distribution.commit: null` on the branch (`:901-904`); the canonical release stamps the asset copy (`release.yml:1005-1034`); `fork-release.yml` computes the commit (`PLUGINS_COMMIT`, `:751`) and stamps nothing, so a fork release's asset says `commit: null`. The release body is a fixed paragraph (`:793-797`), because a suffixed version has no `CHANGELOG.md` section (`:30-31`). The one privacy check is the manifest's `private` flag (`:221`, `:255-257`): nothing reads the repository's or the package's visibility, so a fork repository turned public, or a package left public, publishes without a word. Vendor (read 2026-10-06, https://docs.github.com/en/rest/packages/packages): `GET /orgs/{org}/packages/{package_type}/{package_name}` and `GET /users/{username}/packages/{package_type}/{package_name}` return `visibility` (`private` or `public`) and document classic tokens with `read:packages`; whether the per-run token reads them is not documented. **New.** (1) **Stamp.** `publish` gains `release.yml`'s step `Stamp the release manifest with the distribution commit` (`:1005-1034`), byte for byte, right after `Push plugin distribution`; the branch keeps `null`, the release asset carries the commit. (2) **Release notes.** `gates` gains `Compose release notes` after `Build plugin distribution`: `BASE` = `package.json`'s version with its prerelease part removed; the upstream section is `CHANGELOG.md`'s `## [<BASE>]` section and the fork's is `FORK-CHANGELOG.md`'s `## [<version>]` section, each extracted by `release.yml`'s awk program (`:463-482`, its link-definition hold included), copied; `FORK-CHANGELOG.md` is a root file the fork owns and upstream never ships, so the lane never conflicts on it. `release-notes.md` = `## <package> v<version>`, `Built from <sha> by .github/workflows/fork-release.yml, on upstream <BASE>.`, `### Changes in this fork` (the fork's section, or `No fork notes: FORK-CHANGELOG.md has no "## [<version>]" section.`), `### Upstream <BASE>` (upstream's section, or `Upstream's CHANGELOG.md has no "## [<BASE>]" section.` with a `::warning`), the tarball's digest row and one closing sentence on checksums (and on attestations, `u1-fork-attestations`); `gates` outputs `notes_sha256`; the file joins the `fork-release-dist` upload (`:375-381`); `publish` gains `Verify the release notes digest` after `Verify tarball digest`, refusing on a mismatch the way that step does; `gh release create` takes `--notes-file release-notes.md` in place of `--notes "…"` (`:793-797`); a re-run that finds the release changes its body no more than today. (3) **Repository visibility.** Probe env `VISIBILITY: ${{ github.event.repository.visibility }}` and `PUBLIC_INPUT: ${{ vars.STAMITY_RELEASE_PUBLIC }}` (the only read of that variable); `PUBLIC_INPUT` must be empty or `true` (else `::error title=Fork release misconfigured::STAMITY_RELEASE_PUBLIC must be true or unset.`, exit 1); `VISIBILITY` = `public` without `PUBLIC_INPUT` = `true` → `::error title=Fork release refused::This repository is public, and a package published from it may be public too. Set the repository variable STAMITY_RELEASE_PUBLIC to true to release from a public repository.`, exit 1, no output written; an empty `VISIBILITY` → exit 1, "the run could not read this repository's visibility" (fail closed); job output `allow_public`. `Refuse an unarmed destination` repeats the repository check from the same two expressions. (4) **Package visibility, GitHub Packages only** (the registry host exactly `npm.pkg.github.com`, the rule of `:646`) and only when `allow_public` is not `true`: `Refuse a public package` before `Publish to the registry` reads `gh api /<orgs\|users>/<owner>/packages/npm/<unscoped name>` with `GH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}` (`orgs` when `github.event.repository.owner.type` is `Organization`): `public` → `::error` and exit 1 before any npm call; 404 → a first publish, continue; any other failure → `::warning title=Package visibility unverified::…confirm in the package settings that it is not public` and continue. `Confirm the package is not public` after the publish reads the same: `public` → `::error` naming the package settings page and exit 1 (the version is out, the run is red); unreadable → the warning. Another registry → one notice that it exposes no visibility this workflow reads. (5) `dry-run-summary` adds the notes digest, the visibility verdict and the stamp line. **Docs.** `### Tag a release` (`:947-985`): commit the version, push the branch, dispatch `fork-release.yml` from it with `dry_run` true (`gh workflow run fork-release.yml --ref <branch> -f dry_run=true`, or the Actions tab), wait until it is green — its `gates` job runs `npm publish --dry-run` with the real registry and dist-tag — and only then `git tag` and push the tag; one paragraph shows `FORK-CHANGELOG.md`'s format (`## [1.11.0-acme.1]` sections, Keep a Changelog groups). The variable table (`:928-933`) gains `\| STAMITY_RELEASE_PUBLIC \| repository variable, optional \| true lets a public repository release; unset, the run refuses a public repository and, on GitHub Packages, a public package. \|`. `:901-904`: both release workflows stamp the asset copy. `### Know what proves a release` names the stamped commit. Header and `REATTESTATION_DATE` per the hand-page rule. **CHANGELOG** `### Fixed`: "**A fork release names its distribution commit and carries its changelog.** `fork-release.yml` now stamps `distribution.commit` into the `release.json` it attaches, as the canonical release does, and writes the release body from upstream's `CHANGELOG.md` section for the base version plus the fork's own `FORK-CHANGELOG.md` section." `### Added`: "**A fork release refuses a public destination.** A public repository fails the probe unless `STAMITY_RELEASE_PUBLIC` is `true`, and on GitHub Packages the workflow reads the package's visibility before and after publishing." **Follows:** `fork-release.yml:456-473` (a digest checked against the `gates` outputs before use) and `:122-142` (a variable refused by name). |
| `testCriteria` | **Given** `npx vitest run test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts`, **then**: the stamp step's `run` equals `release.yml`'s and sits after `Push plugin distribution` and before `Create GitHub release`; run on a fixture `plugins/release.json` with `distribution.commit: null` and `PLUGINS_COMMIT=<40 hex>` it writes that commit, and on a stamped one it exits 1. The notes step run in a fixture checkout with `package.json` at `1.11.0-acme.2`, a `CHANGELOG.md` holding `## [1.11.0] - 2026-10-01` and `## [1.10.0] - …`, and a `FORK-CHANGELOG.md` holding `## [1.11.0-acme.2]` writes a `release-notes.md` carrying both sections under their headings and nothing of 1.10.0's; without `FORK-CHANGELOG.md` it carries the "No fork notes" line; without the upstream section it carries the warning line and prints `::warning`; a link definition inside a section survives (release.yml's rule). The release step's create call carries `--notes-file release-notes.md` and no `--notes ` (TEST CHANGE of `:1083`, justified: the body is composed in `gates` now), and `Verify the release notes digest` precedes it. The probe with `VISIBILITY=public` and `PUBLIC_INPUT` unset exits 1 naming `STAMITY_RELEASE_PUBLIC`; with `PUBLIC_INPUT=true` it passes; `internal` and `private` pass; an empty `VISIBILITY` exits 1; `PUBLIC_INPUT=yes` exits 1. The package steps with a stub `gh` answering `{"visibility":"public"}` exit 1 before any npm call (the stub npm's log is empty); `{"visibility":"private"}` continues; a 404 continues; a 403 prints the warning and continues; a registry other than GitHub Packages prints the notice and makes no `gh` call. `vars.STAMITY_RELEASE_PUBLIC` occurs once. **Given** `npx vitest run test/docsPages.test.ts`, **then** the release-section case (`:2293-2315`) passes with the new variable in the table, and a new case finds the rehearsal line (`dry_run=true`) before the first `git tag` line of `### Tag a release`. |
| `edgeCases` | A fork releasing from an upstream base that has no `CHANGELOG.md` section yet → the warning line, the release goes on. Most forks keep no `FORK-CHANGELOG.md` → the "No fork notes" line. A public fork that means to publish publicly → `STAMITY_RELEASE_PUBLIC=true` skips the repository refusal and both package checks. The packages endpoint refuses the per-run token → the warning, the release goes on; the guide says to confirm the package's visibility once in its settings. A package made public between two runs → the next run's pre-publish read refuses before npm. The stamp on a re-run → the artifact downloaded again carries `null`, and the stamp writes the same commit (the orphan commit is a pure function of the tree). `github.event.repository.visibility` on a tag push and on a dispatch → both payloads carry the repository object; an empty value fails closed, unverified for every shape until the first armed run. |
| `depends_on` | u1-npm-dist-tag (`.github/workflows/fork-release.yml`, the `gates` dry run), u1-secret-shaped-fixtures (`test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts`) |
| `verify` | `npx vitest run test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts test/ci/workflow.test.ts test/docsPages.test.ts && npm run lint && npm run typecheck` |

**Amended 2026-10-06 (the updated brief; read at `d10db029`; sentences of the table above that this amendment replaces are already replaced in place):**
- **interfaces, added (merge, 2026-10-06):** an optional deploy-key push for `plugin-dist` and `plugins/v*`, as `promote.yml` does for channels: when the `fork-release` environment holds the secret `STAMITY_RELEASE_DEPLOY_KEY`, the distribution push step authenticates with that key over SSH instead of the per-run token, so a tag ruleset can restrict creation with the deploy key as its only bypass; without the secret the push uses the per-run token as today. The canonical `release.yml` push step keeps its byte parity with the fork's where the parity test reads it, with the key path behind the same condition.
- **testCriteria, added (merge):** **Given** `fork-release.yml` **then** its distribution push step uses `STAMITY_RELEASE_DEPLOY_KEY` when that secret is set and the per-run token otherwise, and the key is written to a file with mode 600 and removed in an `always()` step (`test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts`).
- **edgeCases, added (merge):** a key without write access → the push fails with git's refusal and the release stops before the GitHub release step, naming the secret.
- Inbox fold (2026-10-08, run 2026-10-08_inbox-pass): `2026-09-17_plugin-lifecycle/prove/107` — the branch guard also requires the remote head's committer `github-actions[bot]` and a subject opening `plugins: v`.

### u1-fork-attestations — fork releases can carry build attestations, and the canonical release attests `release.json` and the tarball too

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| `id` | u1-fork-attestations |
| `requirements` | REQ-PLUGIN-049, REQ-PLUGIN-027 (amended) |
| `files` | `.github/workflows/fork-release.yml` (header `:23-28`; `probe` `:83-89`, `:91-163`; `publish` outputs and one new step after the stamp of `u1-fork-release-hardening`; a new job `attest`; `dry-run-summary` `:803-847`), `.github/workflows/release.yml` (`Attest plugin archives` `:907-914`; a new step after `Stamp the release manifest with the distribution commit` `:1015-1034`), `test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts` (`:204-224`, `:226-280`, `:343-351`; one new describe), `test/ci/workflow.test.ts` (`:2848-2872`), `docs/enterprise-forks.md` (the variable table `:928-933`; `### Know what proves a release` `:996-1003`), `test/docsPages.test.ts` (`:2293-2315` passes once the table names the variable), `CHANGELOG.md` (`### Added`) |
| `interfaces` | **Current.** `fork-release.yml` carries no attestation by the decision of 2026-09-24, "until a company asks for them" (`:23-28`; `publish` holds `contents: write` and `packages: write`, "No id-token" `:410-415`; the body says "This release carries no provenance and no attestation" `:795`); the guide repeats it (`docs/enterprise-forks.md:996-1003`); inbox ``"GitHub build attestations for fork releases are not built" at 077e8a78`` waits on that trigger. The enterprise asks, and its fork is internal in an Enterprise Cloud organisation. The canonical release attests `plugins/*.zip` only (`release.yml:911-914`, `actions/attest-build-provenance@4d101475d8b20a2381f78447822ac1eab6504dd8 # v4.2.2`), before the distribution push (`:923`) and the stamp (`:1015-1034`): neither copy of `release.json` nor the tarball asset is attested. Vendor (read 2026-10-06, https://github.com/actions/attest README, which `attest-build-provenance` v4 wraps): `subject-path` takes a glob or a comma- or newline-separated list of at most 1024 subjects; the job needs `id-token: write`, `attestations: write` and `artifact-metadata: write`; attestations work in public repositories on every current plan, and in private or internal ones only on GitHub Enterprise Cloud, never on GitHub Enterprise Server. A fork gets no npm provenance either way (npmjs only, public source; `fork-release.yml:642-643`). **New, fork.** (1) Probe: env `ATTEST_INPUT: ${{ vars.STAMITY_RELEASE_ATTEST }}` (the only read); empty → `attest=false`; exactly `true` → `attest=true`; anything else → `::error title=Fork release misconfigured::STAMITY_RELEASE_ATTEST must be true or unset.`, exit 1, no output; job output `attest`. (2) `publish` gains, after the stamp step, `Hand the distribution commit on` (`id: dist`): `commit=$PLUGINS_COMMIT` when it is 40 lowercase hex, else exit 1; job output `plugins_commit: ${{ steps.dist.outputs.commit }}`. (3) A new job `attest` — `name: attest the release assets`, `needs: [probe, gates, publish]`, `if: needs.probe.outputs.attest == 'true' && needs.publish.result == 'success'`, `runs-on: ubuntu-latest`, `timeout-minutes: 10`, `permissions: { id-token: write, attestations: write, artifact-metadata: write }`, no `environment`, no secret, no checkout. Steps: `Download release artifacts` and `Download plugin distribution` (publish's own, `:443-452`); `Verify tarball digest` and `Verify plugin distribution digest` (byte-equal to publish's, `:456-520`); `Stamp a copy of the release manifest` — the stamp step's node program with `PLUGINS_COMMIT: ${{ needs.publish.outputs.plugins_commit }}`, reading the verified `plugins/release.json` and writing `stamped/release.json`; `Attest the release assets` — `uses: actions/attest-build-provenance@4d101475d8b20a2381f78447822ac1eab6504dd8 # v4.2.2` (release.yml's pin), `with: subject-path: \|` then `plugins/*.zip`, `plugins/release.json`, `stamped/release.json`, `${{ env.TARBALL }}` on four lines. The OIDC grant stays out of `publish`, so npm never runs beside an `id-token` there (npm 11's own trusted-publishing path is never offered one), and the attestation runs after the release exists, so a plan that cannot attest leaves a complete release with checksums and a red run. (4) The release notes' closing sentence (`u1-fork-release-hardening`) reads `needs.probe.outputs.attest`: true → "Verify each asset's build attestation with `gh attestation verify <file> --repo <owner>/<repo>`."; false → "This release carries no build attestation." (5) `dry-run-summary` gains a row `\| attestation \| <the tarball, the archives and both copies of release.json, or none> \|`. (6) The header (`:23-28`) says attestations are opt-in, in a job of their own, and what proves a release either way. **New, canonical.** `Attest plugin archives` (`release.yml:911-914`) becomes `Attest the release assets` with `subject-path` `plugins/*.zip`, `plugins/release.json` (the copy the distribution branch carries) and `${{ env.TARBALL }}`; a new step `Attest the stamped release manifest` after the stamp (`:1015-1034`) and before `Create GitHub release` attests `plugins/release.json` as stamped, which is the release asset. Same pin, same job grants (`:674-678`). **Docs.** The variable table gains `\| STAMITY_RELEASE_ATTEST \| repository variable, optional \| true adds GitHub build attestations for the tarball, every archive and both copies of release.json, in a job after the release. They need a public repository, or GitHub Enterprise Cloud for a private or internal one; GitHub Enterprise Server has none. \|`; `### Know what proves a release` (`:996-1003`): checksums always; with the variable, `gh attestation verify <asset> --repo <owner>/<repo>` (signed in, for a private repository) checks each asset against the run that built it; still no npm provenance. **CHANGELOG** `### Added`: "**Fork releases can carry build attestations, and the canonical release attests every asset.** Setting `STAMITY_RELEASE_ATTEST` to `true` adds a job that attests a fork release's tarball, plugin archives and both copies of `release.json`; the canonical release now attests `release.json` and the tarball beside the archives." **Follows:** `release.yml:907-914` (the pinned action and its documented grants) and `fork-release.yml:456-520` (verify against the `gates` outputs before acting). |
| `testCriteria` | **Given** `npx vitest run test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts`, **then**: the probe with `ATTEST_INPUT` unset writes `attest=false`, with `true` writes `attest=true`, and with `yes`, `TRUE` or `1` exits 1 naming `STAMITY_RELEASE_ATTEST`; `vars.STAMITY_RELEASE_ATTEST` occurs once. The `attest` job's `needs`, `if` and `permissions` are exactly as above, it names no `environment`, reads no `secrets.` and runs no `actions/checkout`; its two verify steps' `run` and `env` equal publish's; its attest step's `uses` equals `release.yml`'s attest pin and its `subject-path` lines are exactly the four. `publish.permissions` still equals `{ contents: write, packages: write }`, and no job but `attest` holds `id-token` (TEST CHANGE of `:347-349`, justified: the loop now excludes the one job whose purpose is the attestation). The stamp-copy step and publish's stamp step, run on the same fixture manifest and commit, write byte-identical JSON. The conditions evaluated over `TRIGGER_SHAPES` run `attest` only on an armed run with `attest` true whose publish succeeded. **Given** `npx vitest run test/ci/workflow.test.ts`, **then** the canonical step is named `Attest the release assets` with the three `subject-path` lines, `Attest the stamped release manifest` follows the stamp and precedes `Create GitHub release` with `subject-path: plugins/release.json`, and `publish.permissions` is unchanged (TEST CHANGE of `:2849`, `:2854`, `:2866-2871`, justified: the subjects widen and a second step attests the asset copy). **Given** `npx vitest run test/docsPages.test.ts`, **then** the release-section case passes with `STAMITY_RELEASE_ATTEST` in the table. |
| `edgeCases` | A private fork outside Enterprise Cloud sets the variable → the attest job fails on the API's refusal after the release is complete; unset the variable, the checksums still prove the release. A re-run of a published release → `publish` stays idempotent and `attest` attests again; a second attestation over the same digests is harmless. `env.TARBALL` is exported by the verify step; an empty value has already failed that step. GitHub Enterprise Server → not supported; the guide says so. A consumer verifying the `release.json` fetched from `plugin-dist` and one verifying the release asset → both verify (two subjects). |
| `depends_on` | u1-fork-release-hardening (`.github/workflows/fork-release.yml`, its stamp step and release notes, `docs/enterprise-forks.md`, `test/docsPages.test.ts`, `CHANGELOG.md`) |
| `verify` | `npx vitest run test/ci/forkReleaseWorkflow.test.ts test/ci/workflow.test.ts test/docsPages.test.ts && npm run lint && npm run typecheck` |

- Inbox fold (2026-10-08, run 2026-10-08_inbox-pass): ``"the attestation subject lists omit the `plugins/*.sha256` assets" at 077e8a78`` — adds the subjects or narrows REQ-PLUGIN-049's claim.

a test holds the counts equal over the deny-scan corpus), `column` = index − line start + 1 when that copy's line equals the raw line, else `null`, keeps one finding per (patternId, line) (the one with a column first), and sorts by line, column (null last), patternId; `excerpt` = for `inline-secret-assignment` the key as written, its separator and `[redacted N chars]` for the value (`api_key: [redacted 24 chars]`), for every other row `[redacted N chars]` (the mask rule of `DenyHit.snippet`, `:54-69`). **`src/merge/managedBlocks.ts`:** `export function blankManagedBlock(content: string, filePath?: string): string` — the detected block's characters replaced by its own `\n`s (no block or a truncated one: unchanged), so a scan reports the file's lines and never joins text across the block. **`safeWrite.ts`:** `blockingDenyHits` goes; `refusePreservedContent(filePath, view, boundaryDir?)` scans `view` with `screenPreservedText` — the first-adoption lane passes the whole file (`:1254`), the merge and repair lanes `blankManagedBlock(existing)` / `blankManagedBlock(terminated)` (`:1296`, `:1367`, `:1388`); `denyRefusalMessage(filePath, view, boundaryDir)` = ``Refusing to write ${display}: ${n} line(s) outside the managed block match a block-severity screen, and this write would keep them next to engine-authored output:\n${lines}\nEdit or remove each line, then re-run; --force does not clear this. new `export async function predictGitignoreRefusal(rootDir: string, required: readonly string[], previous: CoOwnership \| null): Promise<string \| null>` — the message `reconcileGitignore` (`u1-gitignore-lines`) would throw, computed without writing; `null` when it would write nothing or nothing refuses. **`emissionWrite.ts`:** `export type OutputRefusalKind = "deny-scan" \| "shared-name" \| "linked-source"` · `export async function predictOutputRefusal(rootDir: string, output: AdapterOutput, ctx: { ownedPaths: ReadonlySet<string>; mcpServers: readonly string[]; packServers: readonly PackSuppliedServer[]; settingsOwnedKeys: readonly string[]; force: boolean }): Promise<{ kind: OutputRefusalKind; detail: string; findings?: readonly ScreenFinding[] } \| null>` — every refusal a write would THROW for this output: the producer's `sourceRefusal`, the managed lane's `predictPreservedContentRefusal`, a merged MCP document's link refusal (`predictMcpDocumentMerge`), the settings lane's `shared-name` collision, and under `force` a hard-linked whole-file target (the backup's refusal). `planOutputEntries` takes its refusal branches from it (behaviour unchanged) and copies `findings` onto `SyncPlanEntry.findings?: readonly ScreenFinding[]`; `COLLISION_REMEDY["deny-scan"]` names the lines. **`init`:** `applyInit` plans before the state directories (the plan reads nothing they hold); right after planning, live and dry runs alike, it collects `predictOutputRefusal` for every output and `predictGitignoreRefusal(rootDir, required, previous)`; any refusal throws `export class InitRefusalError extends EngineError { readonly refusals: readonly { path: string; kind: OutputRefusalKind \| "gitignore"; detail: string; findings: readonly ScreenFinding[] }[] }`, code `INTEGRITY_ERROR` when any refusal is `deny-scan` else `FS_ERROR`, message ``init refused: ${n} file(s) cannot be set up as they are, so nothing was written``, `why` = the details, `next` = ``edit the lines named above, or keep a file out of the merge with --import-config skip, then re-run init`` (`u1-import-mode-per-file` turns it into `--import-config <path>=skip`). A source refusal stops the run instead of becoming a skipped row (`:325-328`); an unproven-file skip is not a refusal and stays a skipped row. `init.ts` catches `InitRefusalError` and returns `{ exitCode: 1, json: { error, refusals, dryRun } }` (the `learn`/`add`/`check` returned-exit-1 precedent). **`check`:** `collidingPaths` leaves `deny-scan` entries out; new `denyScanStep(entry: SyncPlanEntry): string` = ``${entry.path} keeps owner text the screen refuses: ${findings as path:line:column rule}. Edit those lines and re-run; --force does not clear this.`` **Follows:** `src/merge/safeWrite.ts:326-348` (one composition, every copy, one dedupe) and `src/cli/commands/init/apply.ts:192-205` (a refusal ahead of any write that holds in dry runs too). **Backward compatibility:** nothing persisted changes; the refusal set narrows only by reference-only values and `.gitignore` comments, and a match assembled across the block (an artifact of the joined slice) can no longer occur. **Coverage floor:** `src/merge/safeWrite.ts` 97/95/100/98 and `src/merge/managedBlocks.ts` 99/98/100/100 (`vitest.config.ts:187`, `:197`); both arms of each new branch are in the criteria. |

**when** `check --json`, **then** `error.next` names `AGENTS.md:7:1` and contains no `--force` (r2 M9) (`test/cli/commands/syncEngine.test.ts`, `test/cli/commands/sync.test.ts`, `test/cli/commands/check.test.ts`, `test/adapters/codex.test.ts`). **Given** the deny-scan, hard-link, linked-source and MCP-link fixtures, **then** `predictOutputRefusal` and `planOutputEntries` report the same kind for each (`test/cli/engine/emissionWrite.test.ts`), and every existing `syncEngine` case passes unedited. **Re-pins**, each with `// TEST CHANGE, justified: REQ-FLOW-039 — a refusal names file lines, not offsets`: every `at offset` assertion `grep -rn "at offset" test` finds. |
| `edgeCases` | `token: ${{ secrets.X }} and hunter2hunter2` → refused (a literal follows the reference). `TOKEN=${A} SECRET=${B}` on one line → refused, since the rule reads the rest of the line as one value (named residue: split the line). A reference inside a fenced code block → exempt like any line (the screen does not parse Markdown). A `.gitignore` line ` # note` (leading space) is a pattern in git's grammar → screened. A hit only a normalised copy finds → `path:line` with no column, the excerpt still masked. CRLF text → lines counted on `\n`, so CRLF and LF give the same numbers. `init --force` over a setup → the same preflight; a refusal leaves the old setup byte-identical. An unproven file at an output path → not a refusal: written around and reported skipped (exit 0; `init --strict` makes it 1, `u1-import-mode-per-file`). Owner text under `--import-config replace` → not screened (nothing is kept). |
